# RASM Architecture

This document describes how RASM is built **today** (branch `feat/pbr`, Oct 2026). Planned changes live in [development_plan.md](development_plan.md); known defects are tracked by ID (e.g. `B1`, `D4`) in [code_review.md](code_review.md).

## 1. Overview

RASM is a C++20 rendering engine with a single implemented backend (Vulkan 1.4). It is built as two static libraries, `rasm::renderer` (the renderer alone) and `rasm::rasm` (the renderer, the SDL platform and the file loaders), and driven by small example programs. Its rendering core is *GPU-driven*: compute passes cull the scene's draws against the camera frustum and, in two passes, against a hierarchical depth buffer (Hi-Z), and the visible ones are drawn with one `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirectCount` call per run of draws with the same alpha mode and cull mode (usually one to three), through one pipeline per alpha mode. See-through (glTF `BLEND`) draws come last, sorted back to front on the GPU. Vertices are pulled from one engine-wide geometry pool through buffer device addresses, textures are accessed through one bindless descriptor array, and the only per-draw binding is a 16-byte push constant.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    App["Application (examples/)"] --> Engine["rasm::Engine<br/>(include/rasm/engine.h)<br/>a Renderer + the platform it picks + an AssetLoader"]
    Integrator["Integrator (examples/19, 21)"] --> Renderer
    Integrator -. "19 only" .-> Loader
    Engine -- "is a" --> Renderer["rasm::Renderer<br/>(include/rasm/renderer.h)<br/>library rasm::renderer"]
    Engine -- "owns" --> Loader["rasm::AssetLoader<br/>(include/rasm/assetLoader.h)<br/>meshLoad.cpp, materialImport.cpp, textureLoad.cpp,<br/>environmentLoad.cpp, meshImport.cpp (tinygltf, tinyobj),<br/>ktxImage.cpp (libktx); library rasm::rasm"]
    Loader --> Renderer
    Engine -- "passes SDL" --> Platform["Platform: window + input events<br/>sdlPlatform.cpp (SDL3), nativePlatform.cpp or headlessPlatform.cpp"]
    Renderer --> Platform
    Renderer -. "LoadHooks" .-> Loader
    Renderer --> Registry["Registry<br/>entities, meshes, materials,<br/>cameras, lights, scenes"]
    Renderer --> SC["ShaderCompiler<br/>(Slang API → SPIR-V)"]
    Renderer --> RG["RenderGraph<br/>(pass ordering only)"]
    Renderer --> RC["RenderContext<br/>(handle → rhi object tables,<br/>the bindless set)"]
    RG --> RC
    RC --> DEV["rhi::Device<br/>(backend-neutral interface)"]
    DEV --> VK["rhi::VulkanDevice<br/>volk + vk-bootstrap + VMA"]
    VK --> GPU[(GPU)]
```

| Layer | Files | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| Public API | `include/rasm/` (`rasm.h`, `renderer.h`, `engine.h`, …) and `include/rasm/render/` (`renderGraph.h`, `renderContext.h`, `resource.h`) | `Renderer`: the renderer alone, on the application's windows (`RendererConfig::nativeWindow`) or offscreen images; everything is created through it and referenced by handles. `Engine` (`engine.h`, `src/engine.cpp`): a `final` `Renderer` that picks its platform from `EngineConfig::platform` (SDL opens windows and reads their events); it adds behaviour only by the `Platform` it passes in, and overrides nothing. `RendererConfig` is `EngineConfig` without `platform`; `toRendererConfig` (`types.h`) converts, and `tests/rendererConfigTests.cpp` checks every field. The low-level API (render graph, `RenderContext`, the resource descriptors) lives in `render/`. |
| Renderer frame | `src/renderer/renderer.cpp` | Construction, frame loop (`beginFrame`/`render`/`endFrame`), windows and swapchain recreation; it calls the attached `AssetLoader`'s `finishLoads` through `LoadHooks` (`src/renderer/loadHooks.h`) at the start of `beginFrame`. |
| Asset loader | `include/rasm/assetLoader.h`, `src/assets/assetLoader.cpp` (`AssetLoader::Impl` in `assetLoaderImpl.h`), `meshLoad.cpp`, `materialImport.cpp`, `textureLoad.cpp`, `environmentLoad.cpp` | `AssetLoader(Renderer&, AssetLoaderConfig)`: the worker pool, the loads in flight and the path caches; it finishes the ready loads within the frame's budget (§6). It reaches the renderer's objects as its friend (`friend struct AssetLoader::Impl`), and the renderer reaches it only through `LoadHooks`. |
| Libraries | `src/CMakeLists.txt`, `cmake/rasmConfig.cmake.in` | `rasm::renderer` (`rasm_renderer`): everything but the files below; it links no SDL3, tinyobjloader or libktx and compiles no tinygltf. `rasm::rasm`: `engine.cpp`, `sdlPlatform.cpp`, the loader and the importers, `thirdparty_loaders_impl.cpp`; it links `rasm::renderer` PUBLIC and SDL3, tinyobjloader and libktx. Both are installed, each in its own export set; `find_package(rasm COMPONENTS renderer)` loads only `rasm::renderer` and finds only its dependencies. `examples/21_renderer` links only `rasm::renderer`. |
| C API | `include/rasm/rasm_c.h`, `src/c/` | A stable C ABI over `Renderer`, `Engine` and `AssetLoader` (§13), built into two shared libraries: `rasm_c` (`rasm::c`, links `rasm::rasm`) and `rasm_c_renderer` (`rasm::c_renderer`, links `rasm::renderer`, without `cLoader.cpp`). |
| Core utilities | `src/core/` | Handle manager, file reading, range allocator, the worker pool (`workerPool.h/.cpp`: the loader's and the shader compiler's), `lowerThreadPriority` (`include/rasm/threadPriority.h`, `threadPriority.cpp`), which every pool thread calls. |
| Platform | `src/platform/` | `Platform` (`platform.h`): the window, as a `Window` with a `NativeWindow`, and its events, as `InputEvent`s the engine applies (`input.cpp`, §8). `sdlPlatform.cpp` opens an SDL3 window, turns its events into `InputEvent`s and does the UI's cursor, clipboard and text input; `nativePlatform.cpp` wraps the application's window (`EngineConfig::nativeWindow`) and reads nothing; `headlessPlatform.cpp` has no window (`EngineConfig::platform`). No file here has a Vulkan type. |
| Assets | `src/assets/` | The mesh data on its way in (`meshData.h`, private: `ObjRaw`, `GLTFRaw`, `GltfImportCache`), glTF/OBJ import on the load workers (`meshImport.h/.cpp`, also used by tests; `loadJobs.h`, what a load's jobs write), LOD building with meshoptimizer (`meshLod.h/.cpp`), meshes and the geometry pool (`mesh.cpp`), textures (`texture.cpp`), KTX2 reading and Basis transcoding with libktx (`ktxImage.h/.cpp`), materials and glTF material/texture import (`material.cpp`). |
| Scene | `src/scene/` | Scenes, entities, cameras, lights, transforms. |
| Renderer | `src/renderer/` | Readbacks (`readback.cpp`: `Renderer::readFrame`, `readTexture`, `takeReadback`, `writePng`; §3), picking (`picking.cpp`: `Renderer::pick`, `isPickReady`, `takePick`; §3), the GPU-driven scene path (`gpuScene.cpp`: GPU scene tables, `syncGpuScene`, the scene passes of `addScenePasses`; `viewGraph.cpp`: `ViewGraph`, the graph behind `Renderer::render`, for a list of views; `view.cpp`: `getViewPixels`, `getInputArea`; `gpuTable.cpp`: per-slot tables with dirty-range uploads; `geometryPool.cpp`: the geometry pool and its table; `lightClusters.cpp`: the light table, the scenes' light lists and each shaded view's light clusters), the tone-map pass (`toneMap.cpp`: `Renderer::addToneMapPass`), the bloom passes (`bloom.cpp`: `Renderer::addBloomPasses`), the MSAA resolve (`msaaResolve.cpp`: `Renderer::addMsaaResolvePass`), TAA (`taa.cpp`: `Renderer::addTaaPass`), the frame stats and GPU timers (`frameStats.cpp`) and their overlay (`statsOverlay.cpp`), render graph, Slang shader compiler, GPU struct definitions (`uberMaterialTypes.h`). |
| Render context | `src/rhi/renderContext.cpp` | Backend-neutral API over handles; owns the handle → `rhi::` object tables, the creation-dedup caches and render targets. |
| RHI | `src/rhi/device.h` | The backend-neutral GPU interface: `Device`, `CommandBuffer`, `Queue`, `Swapchain` and the resource objects (§12). |
| Vulkan backend | `src/rhi/vulkan/` | `VulkanDevice` (instance, device, VMA, resource creation), `VulkanCommandBuffer` (recording, barriers), `VulkanSwapchain` (surface swapchain and its semaphores), `VulkanQueue` (submission), one `.h`/`.cpp` pair each. `vulkanTypes.h`: the Vulkan objects behind each `rhi::` struct. |
| Third-party implementations | `src/thirdparty_impl.cpp`, `src/thirdparty_loaders_impl.cpp` | The only translation units with `*_IMPLEMENTATION` defines: the renderer's (stb_image, stb_image_write, stb_image_resize2, VMA) and the loaders' (tinygltf, tinyobjloader; tinygltf calls the renderer's stb_image). |
| Shaders | `shaders/lib/`, `shaders/passes/` | `lib/`: shared modules: GPU structs (`core.slang`), PBR BRDF (`pbr.slang`), unlit shading (`unlit.slang`), the shadow map lookup (`shadow.slang`), a terrain tile's lifted grid (`terrain.slang`), vector features (`vector.slang`), the light clusters' layout and slices (`lightClusters.slang`), the frustum test (`frustumCulling.slang`) and the Hi-Z occlusion test (`occlusionCulling.slang`). `passes/`: entry shaders: the uber shader (`rasm.slang`), the depth-only scene pass (`shadow.slang`), culling (`cull.slang`), the Hi-Z build (`hiz.slang`), the light assignment (`lightClusters.slang`) and the stats overlay (`statsOverlay.slang`). |

Applications compile against `include/rasm/` and glm only. `src/`, the backend libraries and the tinygltf/stb include paths are PRIVATE to the `rasm` target, and static linking still pulls the libraries in. `RenderContext` keeps its `rhi::Device` and object tables in a private `RenderContext::Impl`, defined in `src/rhi/renderContext.cpp`; it knows no `Renderer`: it is made from a `RenderContextDesc`. `Renderer` holds `ShaderCompiler` and `Platform` through forward declarations.

The dependencies go one way: `Engine` → `Renderer` → `RenderContext` → `rhi::Device`, `Renderer` → `Platform`, and `AssetLoader` → `Renderer`. The renderer calls a loader only through the `LoadHooks` interface, so `rasm::renderer` links no loader. The renderer must outlive its loader: an `Engine`'s is a member, made after its `Renderer` base and destroyed before it; a renderer destroyed first logs an error and calls `LoadHooks::detach`, after which the loader drops its loads and refuses every call. Scenes, entities, cameras, lights, materials and meshes stay the `Renderer`'s by design (D3): the GPU tables mirror them row by row, and a `Scene` class of their own would only add change lists, for no use. `RenderGraph` and the private `ViewGraph` (the graph behind `Renderer::render`) hold a `Renderer`. `Renderer` has a virtual destructor (an application may delete an `Engine` through a `Renderer*`) and no other virtual function, so no frame call is virtual.

## 2. Object model: handles and registries

**Handles** (`rasm/handle.h`) are typed `Handle<Tag>{ uint64_t index; uint64_t generation; }` values. `generation == 0` means invalid. Every table hands out its handles from a `HandlePool` (`src/core/handle.cpp`): `allocate` pops the index released last (a stack, so indices stay low) or adds a new one from 1, with the slot's current generation; `release` adds 1 to the generation and pushes the index; `isAlive` is true only for the handle the slot gave out last, while it is not released. A pool made with a limit holds at most `limit - 1` live indices (index 0 means full), which is how the `MAX_*` limits work; a slot whose generation reaches `UINT64_MAX` is not reused. Unit tests: `tests/handleTests.cpp`.

There are two kinds of handle owner:

- **CPU-side objects** live in `Renderer::registry` (a private struct of `std::vector`s) and are indexed directly by `handle.index`. These are `Entity`, `Mesh`, `Material`, `Camera`, `Light` and `Scene` (environments in `Environments::environments`, readbacks in `Readbacks::pending`). Each has a `HandlePool` in the registry (`entityHandles`, …, `readbackHandles`): `MAX_ENTITIES`, `MAX_CAMERAS`, `MAX_SCENES`, and `EngineConfig::maxLights + 1` and `maxMaterials + 1` (set by the constructor) are their limits. Slot 0 is an unused default-constructed element, and a vector grows to its pool's `size`. Every public call checks its handles with `Renderer::isLive` (the pool's `isAlive`), so a destroyed object's handle is refused. Every object can be destroyed (below).
- **GPU objects** live in `RenderContext`. Each type has a table (`BufferTable`, `TextureTable`, …, in `renderContext.cpp`): a `HandlePool` and a `std::vector<rhi::Xxx*>` indexed by `handle.index`, pointing at objects the `rhi::Device` created (null when free). A lookup checks the generation and reads the vector: no hash (`P4`). A stale or unknown handle logs `<caller>: <kind> handle {i, g} is invalid or was destroyed.` and fails. `TextureTable::slots` holds each texture's bindless slot (§5). Render targets are in `std::deque`s (`renderTargets`, `renderTargetInfos`), so a `getRenderTarget` reference survives a new target. Each `rhi::` struct carries a copy of the descriptor it was created from, and the backend derives from it to add its native objects (§12).

**Deferred destruction** (`docs/guides/resource-lifetime.html`). `RenderContext::destroyTexture`, `destroyBuffer` and `destroyRenderTargets` (`Renderer::destroyTexture`, `destroyBuffer`, `destroyRenderTarget` for applications) release the handle at once and give the free to `rhi::ReleaseQueue` (`src/rhi/releaseQueue.cpp`): a list of `std::function` frees, untagged until the frame ends. `Renderer::endFrame` calls `RenderContext::tagReleases` after `submitBatches`, also for a dropped frame: the untagged entries wait for `frameFenceValue`, `computeFenceValue` and the `lastSubmit` of both upload managers, the frames' and the loads' (`RELEASE_TIMELINES` = 4), the values signaled last. `beginFrame` tags and collects at its start too, before it may skip the frame, so frees go on while frames are skipped (a minimized window): nothing is recorded then, so the values signaled last cover everything. It collects again after the slot's fence wait. `collectReleases` reads the four fences (no wait) and runs the entries they all reached, in order; with nothing tagged it returns at once. A render target's textures are marked (`TextureTable::attachments`) and `destroyTexture` refuses them; only the target frees them. A transient target that is destroyed leaves its group at once, since its index may go to a new target. The graphics value covers the frame's compute work (the last graphics batch waits for it); the compute value covers a frame whose later batches failed; the upload values cover a copy flushed after the frame, on the graphics queue or the transfer queue. What waits: a texture (then `textureStates.forget`, `cancelUploads`, destroy) and its bindless slot (back to `freeBindlessSlots`), a buffer (also the old buffer of `resizeBuffer`), the heaps of transient render targets, and storage slots of destroyed storage targets. `RenderContext::cleanup` waits for the device and runs them all (`releaseAll`). Rows of a `GpuTable` need no queue: each frame slot has its own copy, written only after its fence (§4). Shaders, pipelines and descriptor set layouts are deduplicated, so another holder may share a handle: they have no destroy and live as long as the device. Unit tests: `tests/releaseQueueTests.cpp`.

**Destroy calls** (`docs/guides/resource-lifetime.html`, example `17_streaming`). Every object the public API creates can be destroyed; the handle dies at once.

| Call | Through the release queue | Reused at once | Users |
|---|---|---|---|
| `destroyEntity` (`entity.cpp`) | nothing | its instance rows (`releaseEntityRows`), its animated row (written stopped, `clip` -1) and its ranges of the animation buffers (`releaseEntityAnimation`) | its scenes' draw lists rebuild; `Scene::entities` is compacted there |
| `destroyMesh` (`mesh.cpp`) | the five geometry pool ranges and the geometry table row of each sub-mesh, the animation pool range (`releaseMeshAnimation`) | index; `loadedMeshes` entry dropped | entities on it lose their rows and their mesh (searched only while `Mesh::users` > 0); `Mesh::ownedMaterials` and `ownedTextures` (what its glTF import made) are destroyed with it |
| `destroyMaterial` (`material.cpp`) | nothing | its material table row | `detachMaterial`: entities made with it (`Registry::materialUsers`) and the sub-meshes of its import's mesh (`Registry::materialOwners`) let go; their rows are rewritten; the default material is refused |
| `destroyTexture` (`texture.cpp`) | image, bindless slot | index | materials lose it |
| `destroyLight` (`light.cpp`) | nothing | its light table row | its scenes' light lists rebuild and are compacted there; a shadow of it casts nothing (`isLive` in `prepareSceneView`) |
| `destroyEnvironment` (`environment.cpp`) | its four textures and slots | index | scenes have none; a bake recorded in the frame being made keeps the storage slots (`Environments::bakeDestroyed`) |
| `destroyCamera`, `destroyScene` (`camera.cpp`, `scene.cpp`) | a scene's draw list and light list buffers | index (the scene's bit is cleared in its entities and lights) | refused with an error naming the view while a graph other than `Renderer::render`'s holds a view of them (`viewHolds`); then `ViewGraph::release` lets that graph go |

`removeEntityFromScene` and `removeLightFromScene` clear the scene's bit and rebuild its list; the vectors are compacted at that rebuild, which keeps an entry only while its object lives, is in the scene, and was not met before in the same rebuild (`listStamp`). `addEntityToScene` refuses an entity already in the scene, and compacts a full list before it checks `MAX_ENTITIES_PER_SCENE`. `GeometryPool::check` refuses a sub-mesh whose streams do not add up before any range is taken, so only a real lack of space logs the pool's use. Ranges come from `RangeAllocator` (`src/core/rangeAllocator.cpp`, tests in `tests/rangeAllocatorTests.cpp`): free ranges in a `std::map` by offset, first fit, neighbours joined on free, `grow` for buffers that grow. `Renderer::getPoolStats` returns the live objects and pool use (`PoolStats`); nothing walks the objects for it but the 1024 bindless slots.

**Descriptors** (`rasm/render/resource.h`). Each GPU resource has its own creation descriptor: `BufferDesc`, `TextureDesc`, `ShaderDesc`, `PipelineDesc`, `RenderTargetDesc`, `BindlessDescriptorSetLayoutDesc` and `DescriptorPoolDesc` (fixed `D5`). Each is an aggregate with a `std::string name` and default member initializers, so it is filled with designated initializers and a field left unset has a defined value. Each has a defaulted `operator==`, so every field takes part in equality. Passing the wrong kind of descriptor is a compile error. `ShaderDesc` owns its SPIR-V (`std::vector<uint8_t> code`). A `PipelineDesc` with `computeShader` set describes a compute pipeline (§12). Descriptor sets, command buffers and fences only take a name. `DescriptorType` names the descriptor kind of a layout or pool.

**Creation dedup.** Only immutable objects are deduplicated: `createShader`, `createPipeline` and `createBindlessDescriptorSetLayout` first look the desc up in a `*DescCache` (`unordered_map<XxxDesc, Handle, XxxDescHash>`) and return the existing handle for an equal desc. Equality is the defaulted one, so it covers every field, including the name and the SPIR-V bytes. The hashes (`ShaderDescHash`, `PipelineDescHash`, `BindlessDescriptorSetLayoutDescHash`, defined in `src/rhi/resource.cpp`) fold the fields with `hashCombine` from `rasm/handle.h`, which is order-sensitive, unlike the old XOR. `HandleHash` uses it too. Buffers, textures, descriptor pools and descriptor sets are mutable, so every `create*`/`allocate*` call makes a new object (fixed `B1`). Callers that want sharing cache at the asset level: `loadTexture` caches by path and format, and glTF import caches per model by image index and color space.

## 3. Engine lifetime and the frame loop

### Construction (`Renderer::Renderer`, `src/renderer/renderer.cpp`)
`Renderer(const RendererConfig&)` takes the native platform when `RendererConfig::nativeWindow` has a type, else the headless one; `Engine(const EngineConfig&)` (`src/engine.cpp`) calls the protected `Renderer(const RendererConfig&, std::unique_ptr<Platform>)` with `toRendererConfig(config)` and the platform of `EngineConfig::platform` (`createSdlPlatform`, `createNativePlatform` or `createHeadlessPlatform`), then makes its `AssetLoader` member from `toAssetLoaderConfig(config)` (§6), after the renderer has started. Both run these steps.
0. Resolve `shaderDir` beside the binary that holds RASM, the executable or the C API's shared library (the `AssetLoader` resolves `assetRoot` the same way), check the window size, then resolve `EngineConfig::cacheDir` (`resolveCacheDir`, `src/platform/paths.cpp`, §7): the folder is made and a test file written in it; a relative one that fails moves to the user's local cache folder plus `rasm/<executable name>`; one that still fails, or `""`, leaves the engine without disk caches. `getConfig().cacheDir` is the folder in use, or empty.
1. Create the main window through the platform (`Platform::createWindow(WindowDesc)`, with `EngineConfig::nativeWindow` for `NATIVE`), the first of `Renderer::windows` (handle index `MAIN_WINDOW_INDEX`, from `Renderer::windowHandles`, a `HandlePool` of `MAX_WINDOWS` (8)). An SDL or `NATIVE` window carries its `NativeWindow` (handles, type) and its content scale; a window without a native handle stops startup, except headless. A headless window has no handle, and its pixel size is `windowWidth` × `windowHeight`; a headless engine runs `EngineConfig::ui` with only the input the application sends (§8).
2. `RenderContext::initialize(RenderContextDesc)` gets the backend, the validation, queue and cache settings, the main window, `headless` (`Platform::type()` is `HEADLESS`) and `sampledDepth` (`occlusionCulling`), and calls `rhi::createDevice` for `RenderContextDesc::backend`. For Vulkan, `VulkanDevice::initialize`:
   - loads Vulkan (`loadVulkan`, §12): the loader volk finds, with a window or headless. No loader, or one older than 1.3, stops startup with an error,
   - creates the instance. `EngineConfig::enableValidation` turns on the Khronos validation layer; with it, `enableSyncValidation` and `enableGpuValidation` (copied into `DeviceDesc`) add the `validate_sync` and `gpuav_enable` layer settings through `VK_EXT_layer_settings`. Without validation (or without the layers installed) those two are ignored with a warning,
   - creates the surface from the main window's `NativeWindow` (`DeviceDesc::window`; `createVulkanSurface` in `src/rhi/vulkan/vulkanSurface.cpp`: `vkCreateWin32SurfaceKHR`, `vkCreateMetalSurfaceEXT`, `vkCreateXlibSurfaceKHR`, `vkCreateXcbSurfaceKHR` or `vkCreateWaylandSurfaceKHR`, loaded with `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` because volk is built without `VK_USE_PLATFORM_*`; the X11, XCB and Wayland types are declared there, so no development package is needed). A window's instance enables `VK_KHR_surface` and every platform surface extension the loader has (vk-bootstrap). A headless engine passes no window: the instance is made with `set_headless`, there is no surface, and the device needs no `VK_KHR_swapchain`,
   - selects a physical device with Vulkan 1.4 and the required features (below). When none fits, each GPU older than 1.4 is named with its version,
   - creates the device, loads its function table, loads the pipeline cache (`VulkanPipelineCache::load`, §7) from `DeviceDesc::cacheDir`, and creates the graphics queue and the VMA allocator. With `EngineConfig::asyncCompute` (copied into `DeviceDesc`) it also takes a compute queue from a queue family without graphics, if the GPU has one whose timestamps have as many valid bits as the graphics queue's. Then every buffer and image is created `VK_SHARING_MODE_CONCURRENT` for both families, so either queue can use any resource without ownership transfers. With `EngineConfig::transferQueue` (on by default) it also takes the queue of the first family that only copies (transfer, and at most sparse binding or protected) whose `minImageTransferGranularity` is 1 × 1 × 1, since the uploads copy rows at any offset: the GPU's copy engine (NVIDIA, AMD). Without one (lavapipe, MoltenVK, most Intel GPUs) the loads' copies run on the graphics queue (§12). Buffers are then shared by every family in use; images filled from the staging ring are shared by all three when there is a compute queue, and otherwise stay exclusive to the graphics family and move to it by an ownership transfer after their copies (`rhi::Texture::ownershipTransfer`, §12). The log says which (`Load uploads on the transfer queue of family 1.`).

   It then creates the main window's swapchain (`RenderContext::createSwapchain`, on the device's surface; `B8G8R8A8_SRGB`, with `TRANSFER_SRC` usage when the surface's `supportedUsageFlags` has it, so `readFrame` can copy it), registers its images as texture handles, and creates the shared sampler set. A headless engine gets an `OffscreenSwapchain` instead (`src/rhi/offscreenSwapchain.cpp`, backend-neutral, on `rhi::Device` textures): `MAX_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT` readable `R8G8B8A8_SRGB` color attachments with an `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` view (`TextureDesc::encodedView`: `VK_IMAGE_CREATE_MUTABLE_FORMAT_BIT` and a format list of the two), which the UI draws through as through a window's (§12), handed out in turn. Its `acquireNextImage` waits for the frame fence value of the last submit that drew into the image (`submitted`, called by `RenderContext::submit` for the present submit), `present` does nothing, and submits get no acquire or present swapchain.
3. Create the frame fence (a timeline), the compute fence when there is a compute queue, and, for each of the `MAX_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT = 2` slots, a command buffer and a depth texture (`D32_SFLOAT`, `SAMPLED_DEPTH_ATTACHMENT`, so occlusion culling can read it).
4. Initialize the shader compiler (§7): the main thread's Slang session (SPIR-V 1.4, scalar layout, column-major matrices, search path `lib/` under `EngineConfig::shaderDir`), the shader workers (`RendererConfig::shaderThreads`; 0: half the hardware threads, at most 8) and, with a cache folder, the shader cache's base key. A missing folder fails here, so a wrong path stops startup instead of failing every frame. After the geometry pool and the GPU scene (step 5), `warmPipelines` queues every shader a frame can need for the shader workers (§7, Warm-up), before the stats overlay and the UI make the first pipelines on the main thread (their shaders are not queued: they are made right away, while the workers start).
5. Create the geometry pool (`GeometryPool::create`, `src/renderer/geometryPool.cpp`): one device-local vertex buffer of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolVertices` `Vertex`es (STORAGE, read through its device address), one index buffer of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolIndices` `uint32_t`s, a color pool of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolColors` packed vertex colors for the meshes that have them, a skin pool of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolSkins` `SkinVertex`es (joints and weights) for the skinned meshes, a morph pool of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolMorphs` `MorphDelta`s (morph target deltas) for the morphed meshes, and the geometry table of `EngineConfig::geometryPoolSubMeshes` rows. Every mesh lives in these buffers (§6). Their size is fixed for the engine's lifetime, so an app that draws little can make them smaller and one with big scenes larger. `createGpuScene` then creates the instance and material tables and the scene data buffers (§4), `createGpuLights` the light table of `EngineConfig::maxLights` + 1 rows (§4, lights; 0 or `UINT32_MAX` stops startup with an error, and so does a table larger than the device's `maxBufferSize`, which `VulkanDevice::createBuffer` checks for every buffer), and `createStatsOverlay` the timestamp query pools and the overlay's text buffers (§3, frame stats). With `EngineConfig::ui` (and `RASM_IMGUI`), `createUi` makes the ImGui context and the UI pipeline after them (§3, the UI).
6. Take the single bindless descriptor set, which `RenderContext::initialize` made after the samplers (`getBindlessDescriptorSetLayout`, `getBindlessDescriptorSet`): layout, pool and set. Binding 0 of set 0 is an array of `RendererConfig::maxBindlessTextures` (16384 by default) `COMBINED_IMAGE_SAMPLER`s, lowered with a warning to the device's update-after-bind sampled image and sampler limits and to `MAX_BINDLESS_TEXTURES` (65536, so a slot fits the 16 bits TAA packs it into; `getBindlessTextureCapacity`, and the renderer's `getConfig`), binding 1 an array of `MAX_BINDLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` (1024) `STORAGE_IMAGE`s (`BindlessDescriptorSetLayoutDesc::storageImageCount`; `VulkanDevice::createBindlessDescriptorSetLayout` fails with an error that names both numbers when the device allows fewer update-after-bind storage images, `maxDescriptorSetUpdateAfterBindStorageImages` and the per-stage limit), both with `UPDATE_AFTER_BIND | PARTIALLY_BOUND` and visible to every shader stage (`ShaderType::ALL`), so compute shaders can sample too.
7. Create the BRDF LUT of image-based lighting (`createEnvironments`, §4), which the first frame bakes.
8. `isRunning = true`. Any failure returns early and leaves `running() == false`.

**Required Vulkan features**: API 1.4.
- 1.0: `multiDrawIndirect`, `drawIndirectFirstInstance` (each draw names its instance row in `firstInstance`), `samplerAnisotropy`, `shaderInt64`.
- 1.1: `shaderDrawParameters`.
- 1.2: descriptor indexing (runtime arrays, partially bound, update-after-bind for sampled and storage images, variable count, non-uniform sampled image indexing), `drawIndirectCount`, `bufferDeviceAddress`, `scalarBlockLayout`, `hostQueryReset`, `timelineSemaphore`, `shaderInt8`, `storagePushConstant8`.
- 1.3: `dynamicRendering`, `synchronization2`, `shaderDemoteToHelperInvocation` (`discard` demotes, §7).
- Extensions: `VK_EXT_memory_budget`, and `VK_KHR_swapchain` with a window.
- Optional, each on when the GPU has it: `textureCompressionBC`, `textureCompressionASTC_LDR`, `textureCompressionETC2` (1.0), for block-compressed textures (§5), `hostImageCopy` (1.4, with `EngineConfig::hostImageCopy`), for textures copied on the host where that costs the GPU nothing (§5), and `shaderBufferInt64Atomics` (1.2, `RenderContext::hasBufferInt64Atomics`), for the compute path of small points (§4, Point clouds). The log says which are on (`Host image copy: N formats, in video memory` or `off`, `64-bit buffer atomics: yes`).

### Per frame (`Renderer::render`)

`Renderer::render(views, count)` calls `ViewGraph::render` (`src/renderer/viewGraph.cpp`); `Renderer::render(scene, camera)` is one `View` of the whole window. A `View` (`rasm/view.h`) is a scene, a camera, a `ViewOutput` (a `ViewRect` of the window, or of a color `target`: a view texture) and the overrides `bloom` (`ViewToggle`) and `antiAliasing` (`AntiAliasing::DEFAULT` takes `EngineConfig`'s). The `ViewGraph` keeps one `RenderGraph` with, per view in list order, the scene passes into the view's HDR target (`Renderer::addScenePasses`, §4, with the view textures of the views before it as `sampled`), the MSAA resolve or TAA, the bloom, and the `Tone Map` pass into the view's output (`Renderer::addToneMapPass`, §11), then `addUiPass` with every view texture as `shown`. Before it builds or runs anything, `render` polls the events and calls `Renderer::updateSwapchain` (the minimized check and the swapchain recreation `beginFrame` also does), so the sizes are this frame's. It compares the list (`View::operator==`) and each view's size in pixels (`getViewPixels` of its rect on the swapchain or its target) with the ones the graph was built from, in vectors that keep their capacity, and builds the graph again only when one differs (also after a view failed: its failures come from bad handles, a full `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` or a failed allocation; the failed view is left out with an error, and the others draw). What a view keeps lives outside the graph in a `ViewState`, found by the view's camera and `ViewOutput::target` and never by its place in the list: its HDR target (`Renderer::createRenderTarget`, named `scene_hdr`, then `scene_hdr_1`, ... by the lowest free `number`), with TAA its history target (`getTaaHistoryDesc`) and `TaaState`, given to a private `Renderer::addTaaPass` overload. The new graph is built before the old one is replaced, so the views it adds again find their `SceneView` still held and keep its Hi-Z, visibility and light clusters. A view whose size changed gets `Renderer::resizeRenderTarget` on its targets; one whose scene or anti-aliasing changed gets new targets; a state no view of the list has is destroyed (`destroyRenderTarget`). Two views with one camera and target: the second is left out with an error. `beginFrame` takes the camera of the first view in the window (no output target) for the built-in controller. A destroyed output target sizes its view at 0, so the graph is built again without it; `Renderer::destroyRenderTarget` also takes the target's colors out of the materials that sample them (`detachTexture`, as `destroyTexture` does). An application with its own graph calls `addScenePasses` and `addToneMapPass` itself and runs its graph instead; the frame is the same.

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant App
    participant E as Engine
    participant C as RenderContext/rhi
    App->>E: render(views, count) → ViewGraph::render: pollEvents, updateSwapchain, build the graph if the views or sizes changed → RenderGraph::execute(the first window view's camera)
    E->>E: beginFrame: pollEvents() unless the app called it (events, frameTime and deltaTime), then the camera's controller unless NONE
    E->>E: finishLoads → publish loads whose copies the GPU has, fill loads that are ready, their copies out at once (§6)
    E->>E: updateSwapchain: per window, recreateSwapchain(window) if resized or out of date; skip frame if no window can show (all minimized / 0×0 / failed)
    E->>C: waitForFence(frameFence, frameResources[frame].fenceValue), and computeFence with async compute
    E->>C: collectReleases: free what was destroyed and the fences now cover (no wait, §2)
    E->>E: readPicks(slot): the slot's picks, before its sync gives rows away and its endFrame records new ones
    E->>C: updateFrameStats: read the slot's last timers and cull counts, reset them, write the overlay text (without the UI)
    E->>E: buildUi (EngineConfig::ui): ImGui::NewFrame, the RASM window, the app's callback, ImGui::Render, UI textures, vertices into the slot's buffers
    E->>C: syncGpuScene: assign instance rows, rebuild dirty draw lists and light lists, upload this slot's dirty rows (§4)
    E->>C: acquireNextImage(window) for each window that can show (WindowFrame::acquired; OUT_OF_DATE → that window sits out); skip frame if none has an image
    E->>C: open the graphics batch (useQueue), clear new history copies and new kept targets (clearNewHistory)
    E->>C: recordEnvironmentBakes: the BRDF LUT and at most one loaded environment, before any pass (§4)
    loop each pass in the graph's order (scene view: Cull 1 with the light clusters, Scene 1, Hi-Z, Cull 2, Scene 2, Label Depth, Transparent, then Tone Map and UI)
        E->>E: useQueue: an async compute pass records into the compute batch, any other pass into the graphics batch
        E->>E: waits for the other queue's batches that used the pass's resources (closes an open one)
        E->>C: aliasingBarrier before a transient target's first pass, one memoryBarrier for the pass's declared buffers if needed, read textures and depths → SAMPLED
        alt compute pass
            E->>C: GPU timer around the pass's dispatches (Cull: prepareSceneView, then countMain, COMPUTE→COMPUTE, writeMain)
        else graphics pass
            E->>C: beginPass: attachments → COLOR / DEPTH (discard unless the pass loads them), beginRendering with the graph's AttachmentOps, viewport/scissor, cull NONE
            E->>C: the pass's draws (Scene: per run setCullMode, push constant, drawIndexedIndirectCount)
            E->>C: endPass: endRendering (a render target's color → SAMPLED)
        end
    end
    E->>C: endFrame: an empty "Clear" pass on each acquired window no pass drew, on the main window a "UI" pass if the UI has draws and no UI pass ran and the stats overlay pass (without the UI), the picks waiting (`recordPicks`), the readbacks asked for since the last frame (`recordReadbacks`), each acquired window's image → PRESENT_SRC (not headless), COMPUTE → HOST barrier (cull stats; without a compute queue), close the batches
    E->>C: submitBatches: end every batch (the last compute one after a COMPUTE → HOST barrier), then one submit per batch in the order they were closed, each signals its queue's fence; the first graphics one waits for every acquired window's image, the last presents them all; a failed one or a dropped frame → discardRecording
    E->>C: tagReleases(frameFenceValue, computeFenceValue): what was destroyed since waits for them (§2)
    E->>E: finishEnvironmentBakes: a bake whose textures stayed SAMPLED went out (READY), one in a dropped frame waits again
    E->>C: present(window) for each acquired window (SUBOPTIMAL / OUT_OF_DATE → recreate that window next frame)
    E->>E: frameCount = (frameCount + 1) % 2
```

Notes:
- `frameCount` is the frame-in-flight **slot index** (0 or 1), not a running frame counter.
- A steady frame makes **no heap allocation** on the main thread (`P27`, `docs/guides/frame-allocations.html`): per-frame lists are members that keep their capacity (`syncGpuScene` swaps `movedEntities` and `settlingEntities` instead of moving one into the other), text is built in stack buffers, `RenderContext::getTextureDesc` returns a reference. `rasm_image_tests` checks it: after each test it counts `operator new` on the main thread over 4 more frames (`tests/image/allocationCounter.cpp`, Windows only). Dear ImGui still allocates when it bakes a new glyph, and creating or destroying objects allocates.
- Input handling and delta-time measurement run in `pollEvents`, once per frame: the application may call it before `render`/`beginFrame` to read the frame's input first, else `beginFrame` does. `beginFrame` then runs the controller of the camera passed to it, unless the camera's controller is `NONE` or the handle is invalid.
- `beginFrame` returns `false` when the frame is skipped. The caller then records nothing and does not call `endFrame`.
- Frame pacing uses one timeline fence per queue, `Renderer::frameFence` for graphics and `Renderer::computeFence` for the compute queue. Every submit signals the next value of its queue's fence, and the slot remembers the last one in `FrameResources::fenceValue` and `computeFenceValue`. Before reusing a slot, `beginFrame` waits for both. A slot takes a new value only when the submit succeeds, because waiting for a value nothing signals would block forever. Nothing is reset, so a skipped frame needs no cleanup.
- **Batches.** A frame is one batch, or with async compute a few: a batch is one command buffer and one submit on one queue. Each queue records into one open batch at a time (`Renderer::useQueue`), and `FrameResources::commandBuffer` names the one being recorded, so code that records through it follows the graph between the queues. A batch is closed when the other queue needs what it did (`waitForQueue`, called by the graph, §9) or at the end of the frame, and its waits for the other queue's fence are fixed then. `endFrame` makes the last graphics batch wait for all of the frame's compute work, so a slot's graphics value covers the whole frame. All batches are submitted at the end of the frame, in the order they were closed (`submitBatches`), so every wait is on a submit that went out before it, and the GPU starts the frame once it is recorded. A closed batch is ended only in `submitBatches`, so its last compute batch can still take the cull stats barrier. A frame's command buffers per queue are made as needed and kept per slot (`batchCommandBuffers`). If one can't be made or begun, `useQueue` returns false and sets `recordingFailed`: the graph stops recording, `endFrame` records nothing more, and `submitBatches` ends every batch and submits none of them, like a failed submit. The scene view's passes make four batches: compute `Cull 1`, graphics `Scene 1`, compute `Hi-Z` + `Cull 2`, graphics `Scene 2` + `Tone Map` + overlay.

  ```
  compute : [Cull 1]                 [Hi-Z, Cull 2]
  graphics:      ↘ [Scene 1] ──────────↗        ↘ [Scene 2, Transparent, Tone Map, overlay] → present
  ```
- **Windows** (`docs/guides/multiple-views.html#windows`). `Renderer::addWindow(WindowDesc)` asks the platform for another window (an SDL window, `WindowDesc::native` for `NATIVE`, nothing headless), gives it a `WindowHandle` and a swapchain (`RenderContext::createSwapchain`: a surface of its own, whose present support on the graphics family `VulkanDevice::createSwapchain` checks; headless an `OffscreenSwapchain` of its size), and `createSwapchainResources(window)` reads its images and size into its `Renderer::WindowFrame`. `removeWindow` refuses the main window and a recording frame, then `RenderContext::destroySwapchain` waits for the device to be idle, destroys the swapchain and its surface, and the platform closes the window. Each `WindowFrame` holds the window's `Swapchain`, the frame's `imageIdx`, `acquired` (an image this frame), `rendered` (a pass drew it) and `outOfDate`. The graph skips a pass whose window has no image (`writeSwapchain(window)`), and `beginPass` draws `currentWindow`'s image, which the graph sets per pass; a hand-written pass on a window without an image is an empty pass (`RenderContext::beginEmptyPass`: its draws record nothing). `beginFrame` calls `RenderContext::beginSwapchainFrame` before its acquires, so a window that kept an image from a frame that never went out takes part again only once acquired again, and an acquire that fails marks the window out of date, so its swapchain is made again. The UI, the stats overlay and the swapchain-scaled targets and camera aspect follow the main window. `RenderContext` keeps a `WindowSwapchain` per window (its swapchain, its images' handles, `acquired`): a submit that waits for or presents the swapchain names every window with an acquired image (`SubmitDesc::acquireSwapchains`, `presentSwapchains`), and `present(window)` clears it.
- Every frame presents each acquired window, and present needs the image in `PRESENT_SRC_KHR`. A window that no pass drew gets an empty swapchain pass in `endFrame`, so it presents the clear color. A headless frame "presents" its offscreen image: it stays a color attachment, and the next acquire of it drops what it holds.
- Acquire, present and `recreateSwapchain` return `SwapchainStatus` (`OK`, `SUBOPTIMAL`, `OUT_OF_DATE`, `FAILURE`). `SUBOPTIMAL` from acquire still renders the frame, because the image was acquired and its semaphore will be signaled. The window's `WindowFrame::outOfDate` schedules its recreation.
- Swapchain-sized resources (render targets with `swapchainScale`, viewport, camera aspect) use the swapchain extent in pixels (`Swapchain::width`/`height`). `Window::width`/`height` are logical and are only used for mouse input.
- The swapchain always uses `VK_PRESENT_MODE_FIFO_KHR`, so the frame rate is capped at the display's refresh rate (see `B21` in the code review).
- `beginPass(name)` and `endPass` put a GPU timer around each graphics pass, the graph puts one around each compute pass, and the frame's graphics work gets one, from the first graphics batch to the last (frame stats, below).
- `endFrame` emits the Tracy frame mark, so skipped frames add none. Zones cover the frame functions, the backend-neutral `RenderContext` calls that block or submit, and whole upload loops. `fillBuffer` has no zone of its own because it runs per primitive per frame. See the [build guide](build_guide.md#profiling-with-tracy) for connecting a profiler.

### Readbacks

`Renderer::readFrame(ReadbackFormat)` and `readTexture(texture, format, layer, mip)` copy an image back to the CPU without stalling the frame (`src/renderer/readback.cpp`, state in `Readbacks`). The concept: `docs/guides/headless-readback.html`.

```
frame F    readFrame()            → PendingReadback (handle, texture or the frame's swapchain image, format)
           endFrame: recordReadbacks → a hostReadable buffer, RenderContext::copyTextureToBuffer into the last graphics batch
           submitBatches            → finishReadbackSubmit: fenceValue = the frame's graphics value (a dropped frame records it again next frame)
frame F+2  beginFrame waits slot F  → isReadbackReady(h) is true
           takeReadback(h, image)   → readBuffer, convertReadback, destroy the buffer
```

- Only a readable texture can be copied: `TextureDesc::readable` adds `TRANSFER_SRC` usage, and `RenderTargetDesc::readable` gives every color and the depth of a target that usage. A graph makes a readable target in memory of its own, like a history target (`RenderGraph::createRenderTarget`), and `createTransientRenderTarget` refuses one, since a later pass could reuse the memory before the copy at the end of the frame. The offscreen swapchain's images are always readable; a window's are when its surface allows it. Multisampled textures are refused (read the resolve).
- `rhi::CommandBuffer::copyFromTexture` copies a width × height corner (the whole mip for a readback, the used corner for picks: `RenderContext::copyTextureToBuffer` with a size and a buffer offset). It moves the layer and mip to `TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL` and back to its tracked state around `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer2`, so the texture states do not change, then makes the bytes visible to the host (`COPY` → `HOST` buffer barrier). With a compute queue the copy waits for all of the frame's compute work first, and the compute batches of later frames wait for the frame that made it (`readbackComputeWait`), since no graph sees the copy's use of the texture.
- `convertReadback` turns the tightly packed texels into a `ReadbackImage` on the CPU, when the image is taken: 8-bit sources keep their bytes as `RGBA8_SRGB` (BGRA swizzled), float sources are clamped and sRGB-encoded for it; `RGBA32_FLOAT` decodes sRGB and widens halves; a depth reads only as `RGBA32_FLOAT`. Formats: RGBA8 and BGRA8 (UNORM, sRGB), `R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT`, `R16G16_SFLOAT`, `R32G32B32A32_SFLOAT`, `R32G32_SFLOAT`, `D32_SFLOAT`. `writePng` writes an `RGBA8_SRGB` image through `stbi_write_png_to_func` to a UTF-8 path, and `readPng` reads one back (`stbi_load_from_file`).
- `takeReadback` blocks on the frame fence when the copy is recorded but not done, and returns false without blocking when no frame has recorded it yet.

### Picking

`Renderer::pick(view, PickDesc)` (or `pick(scene, camera, renderTarget, desc)` for an application's graph) asks what a view drew under a rect of its pixels (`src/renderer/picking.cpp`, `include/rasm/picking.h`, private state `Picks` in `src/renderer/picking.h`; guide `docs/guides/picking.html`; example `26_picking`). Nothing runs while no pick waits.

```
any time    pick(view, desc)      → PickRequest (WAITING) from Picks::spare, a PickHandle of Picks::handles
endFrame    recordPicks           → per pick: the view (ViewGraph::getSceneTarget, then the shaded SceneView prepared this frame),
                                    its SceneData with the unjittered projection, the rect's planes (getPickClipMatrix), the slot's
                                    cull buffer; countMain + writeMain (frustum only); the draws with the pick pipelines into the
                                    pick's column of the slot's targets; one copy of the used corner (RECORDED)
            finishPickSubmit      → SUBMITTED with the frame's fence value, or WAITING again for a dropped frame
F + 2       readPicks(slot)       → after beginFrame's fence wait (or from isPickReady once the fence passed): group the pixels,
                                    rows → entities (InstanceOwners), toWorldPosition (DONE)
            takePick(h, hits)     → copies the hits, gives the request back to Picks::spare
```

- **The view's frame.** `prepareSceneView` keeps the view's `gfx::SceneData` of the frame (`SceneView::sceneData`), its projection without jitter (`SceneView::projection`), its origin and `preparedSync` (`GpuScene::syncCount`). A pick whose view did not prepare in the frame that records it fails; a view with nothing to draw (`ready` false) answers no hits.
- **The pick's SceneData.** A copy of the view's at `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` + its index in `GpuScene::sceneData[frame]` (`MAX_FRAME_PICKS`, 8, more slots), with `jitter` 0, the frustum planes of `getPickClipMatrix(rect) · projection · view` (`getFrustumPlanes`, now in `gpuScene.h`), `hizLevelCount` 0, no meshlets, no blend sort and no debug view, and `drawInfoPtr`, `cullSlotsPtr`, `groupCountsPtr`, `drawCountsPtr` and `cullStatsPtr` in the slot's cull buffer (`PickCullLayout`; the stats words are scratch). The LOD plane, `lodPixelsPerUnit`, the viewport size and `labelsPtr` stay the view's, so LODs, vector widths and label alphas match the view. A view drawn unculled (no culling pipelines) is picked from its whole draw list with `drawIndexedIndirect`.
- **Recording.** Up to `MAX_FRAME_PICKS` picks a frame, side by side in a row up to `MAX_PICK_ROW_WIDTH` (2048) pixels wide; the others wait. They share the slot's cull buffer one after another (`GRAPHICS → COMPUTE` before each cull but the first, `COMPUTE → GRAPHICS` after it). After a `COMPUTE → COMPUTE | GRAPHICS` barrier for the frame's compute work (`waitForQueueWork` with async compute, as readbacks do), each pick begins rendering on the slot's `Pick Ids` and `Pick Positions` (`R32G32B32A32_UINT`) and `Pick Depth` (`D32_SFLOAT`) textures (the first clears them, the others load), with the viewport at (column − x, −y, W, H) and the scissor on its column, and draws each run with the pick pipeline of its alpha mode and cull mode. Push constant `argument` = `getPickArgument(desc)`: the skip mask and `minAlpha` in 1/255 steps, rounded down. Then `copyTextureToBuffer` of both textures' used corner into the slot's host-readable buffer. The textures, the cull buffer and the buffer grow ×2 and stay (GPU timer `Pick`).
- **Pipelines.** `shaders/passes/rasm.slang` compiled with `RASM_PICK` (and `RASM_ALPHA_MASK` or `RASM_ALPHA_BLEND`), `vertMain` and `pickMain`, no blending, depth test, depth write but for the sorted `BLEND` run (`Picks::pipelines[2]`; the weighted and additive runs, not sorted, take the same shaders with depth write, `pipelines[3]`, so the nearest surface with alpha at least `minAlpha` is hit), made on the first pick (`P31`). When the view sorts its blend run (`SceneView::sortBlend`), the pick's SceneData names that run with sort buffers in the slot's cull buffer, and `recordBlendSort(sceneDataAddress, capacity)` sorts it after the cull as the view's (draw order, then back to front), so with no depth write the see-through draw the view shows on top writes last. Through a mirrored view the cull modes swap, as in `drawSceneView`. `getVertexOutput` takes the instance row: the pick varyings carry the row, a vector's feature id (`tangent.w`) and `PICK_HIT_*` flags; a vector's position is `VectorVertex::depthPoint` (its point under the pixel without the bias; an overlay's too) and its normal the ground's up. `isPickSkipped` collapses the triangles of a skipped kind. `pickMain` cuts `MASK` at the cutoff, drops `BLEND` single-sided back faces and alpha below `minAlpha` (a vector's alpha from `shadeVector` / `shadeGlyph`), and writes (row + 1, feature, flags, material row) and (render-space position bits, `packPickNormal`). The point run has its own pick pipeline (`Picks::pointPipeline`, `shaders/passes/points.slang`'s `vertMain` and `pickMain` with `RASM_PICK`), made with the first pick of a scene that has one: it writes the point's index in its chunk's order as the feature and `PICK_HIT_FEATURE | PICK_HIT_POINT`, and `readPicks` turns the index into the application's through the mesh's `Mesh::pointOrder`; `PICK_SKIP_POINTS` collapses the points. A view that drew glyphs has them culled again through the rect into the slot's own glyph buffer (`PickSlot::glyphCull`) and drawn with `Picks::glyphPipeline` (below, instanced glyphs): the feature is the glyph's index, with `PICK_HIT_FEATURE | PICK_HIT_GLYPH`.
- **Rows to entities.** `InstanceOwners` (`picking.h`) keeps the owner (entity index, generation, sub-mesh) of each instance row per frame slot: `syncGpuScene` calls `assign` when it gives rows to an entity, and `sync(slot)` before the slot's instance upload, so a slot's map is its table's. `readPicks` reads a slot before its next sync (in `beginFrame` right after the fence wait), so a row given to a new entity at once still names the destroyed one (`Renderer::isAlive` false). The owner also keeps the mesh the rows were given for, through which a point pick maps its index (`Mesh::pointOrder`; a destroyed mesh: no feature). CPU only: 20 B a row per slot and current.
- **Hits.** `groupPickSamples` sorts the rect's samples by (row, feature) and keeps per group its count and its pixel nearest the centre of the rect asked for (a rect cut at the view's edge keeps it), then nearest the camera; the groups are sorted the same way. Requests, their hit vectors and the scratch keep their capacity (`Picks::spare`), so a pick every frame allocates nothing (the `picking` image tests count it).
- **Cost.** 0 while nobody picks. A 1 × 1 pick: 0.153 ms in `05_city --pick` (145 000 entities, the cull of the whole draw list is most of it) and 0.033 ms in `26_picking` (RTX 3060, Release, 1920 × 1080, `--bench`).
- **Window points.** `getViewPixel(window, rect, x, y, ...)` (`src/renderer/view.cpp`) turns a mouse position into the pixel of a window view.

### Exports

`Renderer::exportView(view, ExportDesc)` draws a still of a view at any size in tiles and writes it to a PNG or EXR file as it goes, or keeps it in memory for `takeExport` (`src/renderer/export.cpp`, `include/rasm/export.h`, private state `Exports` and `ExportJob` in `src/renderer/export.h`; guide `docs/guides/tiled-export.html`; example `32_export`). Nothing runs while no export does.

```
exportView        -> ExportJob (RUNNING), queued behind the running one
render(views)     -> ViewGraph::render: updateExports (start: camera copy, target, buffers, ImageWriter; the phase's plan into
                     Exports::view), the graph built again when Exports::graphKey changed (addExportPasses after the views),
                     collectExports (finished and cancelled jobs give their resources back once the graph lets go)
beginFrame        -> readExports(slot): the tile the slot's last frame finished, its rows into the band (or the image), a full
                     band to the writer; prepareSceneView of the export's view reads Exports::view
the frame         -> scene passes into the render rect, MSAA resolve, bloom, Export pass (recordExport)
endFrame          -> finishExportSubmit: a submitted frame moves the phase on; a dropped one is drawn again
```

- **Layout.** `getExportLayout` rounds the image up to `EXPORT_ALIGNMENT` (64), cuts tiles of `ExportDesc::tileSize` (`DEFAULT_EXPORT_TILE_SIZE`, 2048, a multiple of 64, lowered so a tile and two guards fit `RenderContext::getMaxImageSize`) and gives every tile one render rect size: the tile with `EXPORT_GUARD` (64) on each side, or `EXPORT_BLOOM_GUARD` (384, the bloom's reach) when the view has bloom; one tile across draws the virtual image's whole width. `getExportTile` places a tile's rect with its guard before it and moves it back inside the virtual image at its edges, so the image's edges are where the passes clamp, as in a single render, and every rect origin is a multiple of 64 (the bloom levels' texels fall on the whole image's grid).
- **The view.** An `ExportJob` owns a copy of the camera (`registry.cameras`, controller `NONE`), a target of one render rect (`export_hdr`: `SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT` and `D32_SFLOAT`, cleared to 0 with `ExportDesc::transparent`, the view's MSAA samples only with `ExportDesc::msaa`), an RGBA32F sum buffer (`Export Sum`, the tile size squared times 16 B, device-local) and per frame slot a host-readable output buffer and an `ExportParams` buffer. `addExportPasses` adds `addScenePasses` (with the view textures as `sampled`), `addMsaaResolvePass`, `addBloomPasses` as the view has them, and the `Export` compute pass. `prepareSceneView` of that scene view (`getExportViewFrame`) projects with `ExportViewFrame::tileClip` times P (P at the whole image's aspect; `getExportTileClip`, then the sample's jitter), takes its LOD from P and the image's height, divides `lodPixelsPerUnit` by the pixel scale, writes `SceneData::pixelScale`, drops the sky with a transparent background, skips debug views, and draws nothing while `idle`. `placeViewLabels` places on the whole image's pixels with sizes, cells and the depth bias times the pixel scale and no fade; `getExportLabelProbe` turns the probes: in the survey only the anchors the tile keeps are tested, at once (`LABEL_PROBE_RESET`, in render rect pixels), elsewhere every depth label keeps its fade (`labelFadeStep` 0) and overlays stay as placed. `prepareWeightedBlend` gathers the survey frames' weighted words (`gatherExportWeightedWords`, by `ExportJob::surveySlots`) and, in the tiles, uses the span they found.
- **Phases** (`ExportPhase`). `SETTLE`: the view draws tile 0 until its pipelines exist (3 frames), the attached loader is idle and no `TileStreamer` of the scene reports work (`setExportStreamSettled`), at most `EXPORT_SETTLE_FRAMES`. `SURVEY` (when the scene has labels or weighted surfaces): one unjittered frame a tile. `DRAIN`: 2 frames, so the last survey words are read; then the span is snapped (`stepWeightedRange` from nothing) and fixed. `TILES`: `ExportDesc::samples` frames a tile, the jitter of `getExportJitter` (a stratified grid for a square count, Halton (2, 3) otherwise) and `TAA_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS`; a new row of tiles waits while the writer has `EXPORT_MAX_QUEUED_BANDS` bands. `FLUSH`: idle until every tile is read and the writer closed the file. From the survey to the last tile `isExportFrozen` keeps the scene's tile streamers from changing what they draw (`TileStreamer::update` polls loads only); while the export runs `getExportStreamView` adds its view (the image's size, errors in pixel-scaled pixels) to their screen error.
- **Export pass** (`shaders/passes/export.slang`, `exportMain`, warmed at startup; push constant `ExportPushConstants`, its `ExportParams` through a device address). Per kept pixel: the resolved HDR color, the bloom sampled as the tone-map pass samples it, exposure, AgX (`shaders/lib/agx.slang`, the tone map's) unless `toneMap` is false; with a transparent background the color is divided by its coverage before and multiplied after. It adds color times alpha, and alpha, times 1 / samples to the sum (the first sample writes it), and on the last writes the sum to the slot's output buffer: `EXPORT_OUTPUT_SRGB8` / `EXPORT_OUTPUT_SRGB16` (straight, 0 below alpha 1e-6), `EXPORT_OUTPUT_HALF` or `EXPORT_OUTPUT_FLOAT` (premultiplied), then a `COMPUTE -> HOST` barrier. A frame that is not submitted adds nothing: its sample is drawn again.
- **Writing.** `readExports` copies the tile's rows from the output buffer after the slot's fence wait: into the image without a path, else into the band (image width times tile height), which goes to the `ImageWriter` when its last tile is in. `ImageWriter` (`src/renderer/imageWriter.cpp`) runs on a thread of its own: a PNG through one zlib deflate stream (level 3, Paeth-filtered rows, 16-bit samples big-endian), an EXR as scanline ZIP blocks of 16 lines (channels A, B, G, R planar, reordered and delta-predicted, stored raw when deflate does not shrink them) with the offset table written at the end. Destroyed before it finished (a cancel), it stops and removes the file. zlib links `PRIVATE` to `rasm_renderer`.
- **Seams.** A tile matches a single render (an export of one tile) but in the pixels where the off-center matrix's rounding moves an edge sample: 0.02 % of the pixels of `32_export`'s check at 3072 x 1728, a few by more than 3 levels.
- **Shader variant.** An export's scene view (`SceneView::exported`) draws with its own shaded pipelines, `GpuScene::shaded[EXPORT_VARIANTS + log2 samples]`, compiled with `EXPORT_VIEW_DEFINE` (`#define RASM_EXPORT_VIEW`; `getMotionDefine` gives it, `warmScenePipelines` warms them when the export's view is made): `sky.slang` and `grid.slang` add the projection's offset terms (`projection[i][2]`) to their rays, and sizes in pixels read `SceneData::pixelScale` (`getPixelScale` in `core.slang`). In every other view `getPixelScale` is 1 and folds away, so a frame without an export runs the same shader code as before; `cull.slang` and `pointRaster.slang`, one pipeline for every view, define `RASM_PIXEL_SCALE` and read it.
- **Cost.** Nothing while no export runs: `updateExports` and `collectExports` look at an empty list. An export's frame costs one render rect of its scene, plus the Export pass.

### Image tests

`rasm_image_tests` (`tests/image/imageTests.cpp`) renders 15 scenes headless at 512 × 288 and compares each frame with `tests/golden/lavapipe/<test>.png`. The concept: `docs/guides/image-tests.html`.

```
tools/image_tests.py --lavapipe   fetch Mesa 26.3.0 (lavapipe) and Sponza, SHA-256 checked, into build/test-assets/
                                  VK_DRIVER_FILES = lavapipe's manifest, LP_NATIVE_VECTOR_WIDTH = 256
rasm_image_tests                  for each group: one headless Engine, one camera, the sunset environment, a cube
  for each test of the group      the scene (built once, shared by the tests of one build function)
                                  a graph: shadows, scene passes, MSAA resolve or TAA, bloom, tone map
                                  (views: Renderer::render with the content's four views instead)
                                  waitForLoads, N frames at a fixed animation time, readFrame, takeReadback
                                  compareImages with the golden (readPng): fail when > F of the pixels differ by > T
```

- `ImageTest` holds a test: its build function, samples, TAA, bloom, debug view, frame count, animation time, T (`threshold`, 0 to 255), F (`fraction`) and `views`, which draws with `Renderer::render(views, count)` (`buildViews`: split screen, a map in the corner without bloom, and a view texture a screen in the scene shows) instead of a graph. Every test uses T = 3, F = 0.1%; `determinism` renders the helmet again and compares it with `helmet_ibl`'s image of the same run, T = 0, F = 0 (`sameAs`).
- One engine runs every test, in the order of `TESTS`. Tests that share a build function share its scene, so their pipelines and loads are made once. Each test resets the camera's history and sets its projection, transform, bloom strength and debug view.
- A failure writes `actual.png`, `expected.png` and `diff.png` (gray same, orange ≤ T, red > T) to `build/<preset>/image-tests/<test>/`. `--update` writes the goldens; `--scenes` picks tests; `--sponza` points at the fetched Sponza.
- The goldens are lavapipe's. The script fails when the log names a device other than llvmpipe, or has a validation or engine error. The presets' `ctest` excludes the label `image`. CI's Windows job registers lavapipe and the validation layer in the registry (its processes are elevated, so the loader ignores `VK_DRIVER_FILES`) and runs the script after the Release tests.

### Frame stats and the overlay

`Renderer::getFrameStats()` returns a `FrameStats` (`rasm/renderer.h`), and with `EngineConfig::showStats` (F1 toggles it) the engine shows it: in the RASM window with `EngineConfig::ui` (below), else in a panel in the top-left corner. Measuring is in `src/renderer/frameStats.cpp` (state in `FrameStatsCollector`, `frameStats.h`), the panel in `src/renderer/statsOverlay.cpp` (state in `StatsOverlay`, `statsOverlay.h`); `Renderer` holds both through forward declarations like `GpuScene`. Frames are measured only while the overlay is shown or once `getFrameStats` has been called; until then there are no timestamps, read-backs or budget queries. `Renderer::beginGpuTimer`/`endGpuTimer` are public, so an application can time its own work into the frame's command buffer; the render graph uses them for compute passes.

| Value | Source |
|---|---|
| `cpuFrameMs` | `frameTime`, the time between the starts of the last two frames (measured, also under `fixedDeltaTime`) |
| `gpuFrameMs`, `gpuTimers` | timestamp queries: a pair around the frame's command buffer, each `beginPass`/`endPass` and each render graph compute pass, named after the pass (a scene view's `"Cull 1"`, `"Scene 1"`, `"Hi-Z"`, `"Cull 2"`, `"Scene 2"`, or `"Cull"` and `"Scene"` without occlusion culling), or after `Pass::timerName`, which compute passes next to each other in the order on one queue share (the 11 bloom passes are one timer, `"Bloom"`). At most `MAX_GPU_TIMERS` (64) per frame (`D21`); later ones are not timed, with one warning. `getTimestamps` takes each value as a distance from the first query it reads, modulo `timestampValidBits`, so a counter wrap inside a frame does not break the times. `gpuFrameMs` runs from the frame timer's start (query 0) to the latest end of a graphics-queue timer, and the timers are listed in the order they were recorded. Async compute timers are left out of `gpuFrameMs`, because timestamps of two queues may count from different origins; each one's time is read from its own two queries (`getTimestamps(pool, 2 * timer, 2, ...)`), since it begins and ends on one queue. |
| `uploadGpuMs` | timestamp queries around each upload batch, on its queue (`UploadManager`, where `Device::hasTimestamps` says that queue's timestamps count like the graphics queue's): the GPU time of the copies whose batches finished since the last frame, on both upload queues. Taken every frame, measured or not. |
| `draws`, `triangles` | CPU sums over the frame's scene views (`GpuScene::drawTotals`, added in `prepareSceneView`), from each draw list's size and `triangleCount` |
| `drawsVisible`, `trianglesVisible`, `trianglesVisibleLod0`, `drawsPerLod` | `cull.slang`: each group of `countMain` (early and late) adds the draws and triangles it keeps to the slot's `Cull Stats Buffer` (`SceneData::cullStatsPtr`, `CULL_STATS_WORDS` = 22 × u32, named by the `STATS_*` constants in `cull.slang` and `CULL_STATS_*` in `gpuScene.h`: draws; triangles at the picked LOD, low and high; the same triangles at LOD 0, low and high; draws per LOD; then the two light counts and the meshlets: drawn, culled, draws over budget; then the visible draws' triangles at their LOD before meshlet culling, low and high; then the glyphs in the chunks `glyphCull.slang` tested and those it drew, `FrameStats::glyphs` and `glyphsVisible`, whose triangles count with the visible ones) with atomics; `cullMeshletChunk` (in `meshletCountMain` or `taskMain`) adds the triangles of the meshlets it keeps. The triangles are 64-bit as two words with a carry (`addCount64`), since Metal and WebGPU lack 64-bit atomics. A view drawn without culling counts all its draws, at LOD 0. The overlay shows `trianglesVisibleLod0 − trianglesVisibleLod` as "LOD saved", `trianglesVisibleLod − trianglesVisible` as "Meshlets saved" and `drawsPerLod` as "LOD draws". |
| `lightsGlobal`, `lightsVisible`, `lightsDropped` | the global lights of the frame's shaded views (CPU); the local lights `lightsMain` found in a view's frustum, and the lights that did not fit a cluster (`MAX_CLUSTER_LIGHTS`, counted per cluster) or a tile's list, both added to the `Cull Stats Buffer` (words 13 and 14) by `lightClusters.slang`. A view without clusters counts all its local lights as visible. |
| `memory` | `queryVRAMStats` (`VK_EXT_memory_budget`) |

```
frame N in slot s                         frame N+2 in slot s (after the fence wait)
 record: timestamps, scene view totals ──▶ updateFrameStats: read timestamps + Cull Stats Buffer[s]
 cull:   atomics into Cull Stats[s]          reset the query pool on the host, zero the counters
 COMPUTE → HOST barrier, in endFrame or      updateStatsOverlay: write the overlay text into Stats Overlay Text[s]
   the frame's last compute batch
```

- The cull stats are zeroed every frame, measured or not, and the dropped lights are read every frame: the first time a frame drops lights, `updateFrameStats` logs a warning with the count and what to do, and again at most every 10 seconds while it goes on.
- The GPU values are `MAX_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT` frames old: a slot is read only after its fence wait. A frame that was recorded but not submitted leaves its slot's fence value unchanged, so `updateFrameStats` skips it, since its queries were never written.
- One `COMPUTE → HOST` barrier per frame makes the counters visible to the CPU after the fences: `endFrame` records it after the last pass, or with a compute queue, where the culling runs, `submitBatches` records it at the end of the frame's last compute batch. `RenderContext::readBuffer` reads a buffer created with `hostReadable` (cached memory).
- The text is rebuilt every 0.25 s of frame time (`gridAge` adds up `deltaTime`), so the numbers can be read, and with `EngineConfig::fixedDeltaTime` the same frames rebuild it in every run (`C15`). It is copied into the slot's host-visible text buffer every frame. It is a grid of at most 40 × 76 characters (`MAX_TEXT_ROWS`: room for every GPU timer).
- `endFrame` draws the overlay in a pass of its own after every other (`drawStatsOverlay`), so it covers whatever the frame drew: the pass loads the swapchain image's color and has no depth attachment (`beginRendering` with an invalid depth handle; the overlay pipeline is made with `Format::UNKNOWN` depth), so it costs no depth clear. It has no GPU timer. `statsOverlay.slang` draws two triangles over the panel at depth 1 (the near plane) and looks up each pixel's character and glyph bit in an 8x8 bitmap font (the public-domain IBM VGA font, `'!'` to `'~'`) kept in the shader. Its 16-byte push constant holds the text's address, the grid size and scale, and the screen size. Each font pixel covers `max(1, height / 1080)` screen pixels on each side. It sets a full-swapchain viewport and scissor and `CullMode::NONE` first, since it works in window pixels and its triangles wind clockwise.
- The overlay pipeline is created at startup, after the bindless layout. If it fails to compile, the error is logged once and the overlay stays off.
- With the UI on, the overlay is neither written nor drawn: `updateStatsOverlay` and `drawStatsOverlay` are skipped.

### The UI (Dear ImGui)

`EngineConfig::ui` runs Dear ImGui (`src/renderer/ui.cpp`, `src/renderer/engineUi.cpp`, `src/platform/uiPlatform.cpp`, `src/renderer/inspector.cpp`, `shaders/passes/ui.slang`), in a rasm built with the CMake option `RASM_IMGUI` (on by default). The option adds the `imgui` feature of `vcpkg.json` (the plain port, no backends: RASM feeds the input and draws), compiles those four files, links `imgui::imgui` PUBLIC and defines `RASM_IMGUI` PUBLIC; `rasmConfig.cmake` finds `imgui` only then. Without the option `renderer.cpp` defines `addUiPass`, `setUiCallback` and `getUiTexture` as stubs, and `ui` logs a warning. The engine owns the one ImGui context (`Ui::context`), which the application's `ImGui::` calls use.

- **Startup** (`createUi`): the context with `IniFilename = nullptr` (no `imgui.ini`), `ImGuiBackendFlags_RendererHasVtxOffset` and `RendererHasTextures`, the style and `FontScaleDpi` scaled by `Window::contentScale` (`setUiScale`; 1 headless), RASM's platform side (`initUiPlatform`: `BackendPlatformName` "rasm", `ImGuiBackendFlags_HasMouseCursors` when the platform has cursors, and the clipboard through `Platform::getClipboardText` / `setClipboardText`), and the UI pipeline: no vertex input, no depth, `PREMULTIPLIED_ALPHA` blending, for the swapchain's encoded format with `#define RASM_UI_ENCODED` when it has one (§12), else for its sRGB format. A failure leaves the UI off and the stats overlay on.
- **Input** (`pollEvents`, §8): no ImGui platform backend runs (the vcpkg port has no backend feature). Every `InputEvent`, from any platform or from `addInputEvent`, goes to `processUiEvent` while a UI is built (`Ui::active`): `AddKeyEvent` (the four `ImGuiMod_*` first, then the key by `InputEvent::key`, or by `physicalKey` when the meaning has no `Key`), `AddMousePosEvent`, `AddMouseButtonEvent` (with its position first), `AddMouseWheelEvent` (x negated), `AddInputCharacter`, `AddFocusEvent`, and `-FLT_MAX` for `MOUSE_LEAVE`. With ImGui's `WantCaptureMouse` a button press and the wheel do not reach the camera, with `WantCaptureKeyboard` neither do W/A/S/D, and with `WantTextInput` Escape and F1–F3 are ignored. `newUiPlatformFrame` sets `DisplaySize` to the window's size in points and `DisplayFramebufferScale` to pixels per point (1 headless). After `Render`, `updateUiPlatform` tells the platform when `WantTextInput` changes (`Platform::setTextInput`: SDL starts and stops its text events) and when the cursor changes (`Platform::setCursor`, hidden with `MouseDrawCursor`, skipped with `ImGuiConfigFlags_NoMouseCursorChange`); a UI that stops building frames gets the arrow back and text input off (`suspendUiPlatform`). When `Window::contentScale` changes (the window moved to another display), `buildUi` scales the current style by the ratio of the new scale to the old (`setUiScale`), so the application's colors and sizes stay (ImGui's `ScaleAllSizes` rounds each size down, so a size can lose a pixel over moves between displays). A UI that resumes gets its keys and mouse cleared and the pointer from `InputState` (`resumeUiInput`). With or without a window, ImGui's `DeltaTime` is the engine's `deltaTime` (1/60 s on the first frame), so `EngineConfig::fixedDeltaTime` steps the UI too.
- **Building** (`buildUi`, in `beginFrame` after `updateFrameStats` and before `syncGpuScene`, Tracy zone `UI`): only while the RASM window is shown (`showStats`) or a callback is set; otherwise nothing runs and no events are fed. `NewFrame`, `drawFrozenFrustums` (below), `drawEngineWindow` (stats renewed every 0.25 s of frame time, `Ui::statsAge`, the forced LOD, the debug view and Freeze culling, the Buffers and GPU tables sections (§4), the last F3 dump), `Ui::callback`, `Render`. Then texture requests, and the draw data: each list's vertices and 16-bit indices are copied (`fillBuffer`) into the frame slot's host-visible `UI Vertices` and `UI Indices` buffers, which grow to the next power of two (at least 64 KiB and 16 KiB) and are made again only then. Each `ImDrawCmd` becomes a `UiDraw`: the device address of its first vertex, its index range, its texture and its clip rectangle in framebuffer pixels (`getUiScissor`). A user draw callback runs here.
- **Textures** (ImGui's `RendererHasTextures` protocol): a texture ImGui asks for, and one it changed, becomes a new `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` texture with all its pixels (an `Alpha8` one expanded to RGBA) in a free bindless slot (`LINEAR_CLAMP`). The old texture goes to `RenderContext::destroyTexture`, so it and its slot are freed once no submitted frame samples them (`releaseUiTexture`, §2 deferred destruction). A texture that cannot be made keeps ImGui's request and its old texture, and the next frame tries again. An `ImTextureID` is the bindless slot + 1, with bit 32 for a texture in an sRGB format (`getUiTexture`, which also gives a loaded texture without a slot one).
- **Drawing** (`drawUi`): the pass `addUiPass` adds (`UI`, `writeSwapchain`, `Pass::encodedSwapchain`), or `drawUiFallback` in `endFrame` (`beginPass("UI", {.loadColor = true, .encodedColor = true})`, with a debug-level log line the first time) when no UI pass ran and there are draws. Per draw: a scissor, the 16-byte `UiPushConstants` (`vertices` address, `texture` slot with bit 31 for sRGB, `displaySize` in points), `drawIndexed` with vertex offset 0. The vertex shader reads `vertices[SV_VertexID]`, so the base vertex travels in the address (D3D12's `SV_VertexID` leaves it out).
- **Color.** ImGui's colors are sRGB numbers, blended as numbers. Through the encoded view the shader passes them as they are and encodes an sRGB texture's samples back, which matches ImGui's own backends. Without it, the shader decodes the vertex colors and a UNORM texture's samples, and the blend happens in linear light (`D31`). Either way an image shows the texture's stored colors.

## 4. The GPU-driven path: GPU scene tables + the scene passes

The scene lives on the GPU in tables that last for the engine's lifetime. They are created in `Renderer::createGpuScene` (`src/renderer/gpuScene.cpp`), and their state is `GpuScene` (`src/renderer/gpuScene.h`), which `Renderer` holds through a forward declaration, like the `GeometryPool`. Only `gpuScene.cpp` touches `GpuScene`: the scene, entity and material code calls `Renderer` methods (`queueInstanceRows`, `writeInstanceRows`, `writeMaterialRow`, `addEntityToDrawList`, `refreshEntitiesAfterMaterialChange`).

```
ENGINE-WIDE (all scenes share them)                                        PER SCENE
 geometry table        material table          instance table              draw list
 1 row per sub-mesh    1 row per material      1 row per entity x sub-mesh DrawInfo[]
 GeometryData          MaterialData            InstanceData                firstInstance = instance row
 written once          rows rewritten when     rows rewritten when         rebuilt when the scene changes,
 (GeometryPool)        a material changes      a transform changes         stored as runs by alpha and cull mode
```

| Table | Buffer(s) | Row | Written by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Geometry | `GeometryPool::tableBuffer()`, one copy, `EngineConfig::geometryPoolSubMeshes` rows | `gfx::GeometryData`: `vertexOffset`, bounding sphere (center, radius), `lodCount` and `lods[MAX_MESH_LODS]` (`firstIndex`, `indexCount`, error, `firstMeshlet`, `meshletCount` per LOD), `colorOffset` (the sub-mesh's first vertex color in the color pool, -1 without colors), `skinOffset` (its first `SkinVertex` in the skin pool, -1 when not skinned), `morphOffset` and `morphTargetCount` (its first `MorphDelta` in the morph pool, -1 without morph targets) | `GeometryPool::upload`, once per sub-mesh. No frame has read a new row yet, so one copy is enough. |
| Materials | `GpuTable materials`, `EngineConfig::maxMaterials` + 1 rows, row = material handle index, reused at once after `destroyMaterial` | `gfx::MaterialData` (352 B): `gfx::Material`, `gfx::MaterialId` (`PBR`, `UNLIT`, `TERRAIN`, `VECTOR`, `POINTS`, `GLYPHS`), `alphaMode`, `alphaCutoff`, `flags` (`MATERIAL_DOUBLE_SIDED`, `MATERIAL_TEXTURE_TRANSFORM`), one `TextureTransform` per texture slot, then `gfx::TerrainData` (a `TERRAIN` material's heights) `gfx::VectorData` (a `VECTOR` material's style) and `gfx::PointData` (a `POINTS` material's, and the colormap of a `GLYPHS` material or a `VECTOR` one with scalars), zero for the others (below) | `writeMaterialRow`, from `createMaterial`, `loadMaterial` and `updateMaterial` |
| Meshlets | `GeometryPool::meshletBuffer()`, one copy, `EngineConfig::geometryPoolMeshlets` rows | `gfx::GeometryMeshlet`: sphere, cone axis and cutoff, `firstIndex`, `indexCount`, `firstData`, `vertexCount` (below, meshlets) | `GeometryPool::write`, once per sub-mesh |
| Meshlet data | `GeometryPool::meshletDataBuffer()`, one copy, `EngineConfig::geometryPoolMeshletData` words, only with mesh shaders | per meshlet: its vertices, then a word per triangle (three local indices, a byte each), at `SceneData::meshletDataPtr` | `GeometryPool::write`, once per sub-mesh |
