| Instances | `GpuTable instances`, `EngineConfig::maxInstances` rows | `gfx::InstanceData` (160 B): `world` (the three rows of the affine world matrix, w = the high part of the translation) and `translationLow` (render space, below), normal matrix, geometry index, material index, `previousWorld` (the rows of the affine matrix the frame before drew, w = its translation minus this one, for motion), `meshletVisibility` (its first word of the views' meshlet bits, -1 for none; a vector mesh's first label slot, a point chunk's `PointChunkLod`, a glyph chunk's shape's geometry row), and `animationRow` (the row's `InstanceTransform` while a clip moves its sub-mesh, -1 otherwise; below, node animation) | `writeInstanceRows`, from `updateTransform` and when an entity gets its rows |
| Lights | `GpuLights::lights` (a `GpuTable`), `EngineConfig::maxLights` + 1 rows, row = light handle index | `gfx::Light` (68 B): position (the high part), intensity, color, range, direction, type, the spot cone's `spotScale` and `spotOffset`, `positionLow` | `writeLightRow`, from `createLight` and `updateLight` |
| Scene light list | `SceneLightList::buffers[2]` per scene, grown 2x | `uint32_t` light rows: the scene's global lights, then its local ones, each in the scene's order (`buildSceneLightList`); a 0 after them, which the local loop of a scene with only global lights reads on lavapipe; a scene without one has no buffer, and a shaded view's `SceneData::sceneLightsPtr` names the light table, whose row 0 is zero, so the light loops never index a null pointer (`B38`) | `syncGpuLights`, when `addLightToScene` added a light or `updateLight` moved one between global and local |
| Light clusters | `ViewClusters::buffers[2]`, one per frame slot and shaded view, grown to the scene's local lights | the slices' depths, a count and `MAX_CLUSTER_LIGHTS` rows per cluster, the tiles' lists, the lights' view-space spheres and visible bits (below) | `lightClusters.slang`, on the GPU, in the view's first cull pass |
| Draw list | `SceneDrawList::buffers[2]`, grown 2x when too small | `gfx::DrawInfo{indexCount, 1, firstIndex, firstVertex, firstInstance = instance row}` | `rebuildDrawList` + `uploadDrawList` |
| Visible draws | `SceneView::visibleDraws[2]`, one per frame slot and scene view, grown 2x when too small | `gfx::DrawInfo`, the draw list's rows that pass culling, packed per run in list order. Then culling's scratch: a `uint32_t` slot per draw (`cullSlotsPtr`) and a visible count per group of 64 (`groupCountsPtr`). Then the draw counts: `MAX_DRAW_RUNS` `uint32_t`s (`drawCountsPtr`), the visible draws of each run, which `drawIndexedIndirectCount` reads. The meshlet draws sit between the draws and the scratch, and the meshlet scratch after it (below, meshlets; `getVisibleDrawLayout`) | `cull.slang`, on the GPU (`writeMain`, the run's last group, writes the counts) |
| Visibility | `SceneView::visibility`, one copy per scene view, `EngineConfig::maxInstances` `uint32_t`s, zero when the view is made | 1 if the instance row passed its last occlusion test | `cull.slang` (late `countMain`) |
| Meshlet visibility | `SceneView::meshletVisibility`, one copy per scene view with occlusion culling, `EngineConfig::maxInstances` `uint32_t`s, zero when made | a bit per meshlet of an instance row's LOD, at `InstanceData::meshletVisibility` | `cullMeshletChunk`, late phase: `meshletCountMain` in `Cull 2`, or `taskMain` in `Scene 2` with mesh shaders |
| Hi-Z | `SceneView::hiz[2]`, one per frame slot and scene view, grown to the target's size | `float`: the farthest depth under each texel, all levels packed | `hiz.slang` (`hizMain`) |

**`GpuTable`** (`src/renderer/gpuTable.h`) is N rows of a fixed size with a CPU copy and one device-local buffer per frame slot. `setRow` writes the CPU copy and marks the row dirty for both slots. `upload(frame)` sorts that slot's dirty rows, merges neighbours into ranges (`mergeRows`) and writes each range with `fillBuffer`. If a write fails, the rows stay dirty and the slot sends them again in its next frame. A slot is written only in its own frame, after the fence wait, so the GPU is done with it. This holds on both `fillBuffer` paths (a staged copy, or a direct write into ReBAR memory).

**Instance rows.** An entity created with a mesh goes on `entitiesWaitingForMesh` (`queueInstanceRows`). Once the mesh is READY, `syncGpuScene` takes a range of one row per sub-mesh from `GpuScene::instanceRows` (a `RangeAllocator`) (`GpuScene::entities[entity]`, an `EntityDrawState`: first row, row count, a bit per scene that holds the entity, and whether it waits for textures) and writes them. An entity whose mesh failed to load leaves the list with a warning and never gets rows. The rows go back when the entity or its mesh is destroyed, and the next entity may take them at once (each slot has its own copy of the table). A row's matrix is entity transform (`Transform::toMatrix`: scale, then rotate, then translate, in doubles) x node transform, computed in doubles and written by `writeWorldRows` (`src/renderer/renderSpace.cpp`): the linear part as floats, the translation split in a high and a low float (render space, below). Its material is the primitive's, else the entity's, and `getMaterial` resolves an invalid one to the default material. Each row also keeps its `CullMode` and its material's `AlphaMode` on the CPU (`instanceCullModes`, `instanceAlphaModes`): `NONE` for a double-sided or a `BLEND` material, otherwise `BACK`, or `FRONT` when the matrix has a negative determinant (a mirror turns the front faces clockwise, as glTF specifies). A row whose cull mode or alpha mode changes (`updateDrawModes`) marks the draw lists of the entity's scenes for a rebuild. A sub-mesh missing vertices or indices gets a row but no draw, with a warning.

**Waiting for textures.** An entity is drawn only once every texture of every sub-mesh's material (the primitive's, else the entity's) is loaded, so nothing is drawn half-loaded. When it gets its rows with a texture still loading, it goes on `entitiesWaitingForTextures` instead of into the draw lists. `finishLoads` sets `GpuScene::texturesFinished` when a texture load finishes (its copies are on the GPU, `publishUploadedLoads`), and only then does `syncGpuScene` check the waiting entities again. `updateMaterial` calls `refreshEntitiesAfterMaterialChange` when `doubleSided` or `alphaMode` changed or a new texture still loads. It recomputes every entity's cull modes and alpha modes and moves entities with a texture that still loads back to waiting. It uploads no instance rows: the material keeps its row index.

**Draw lists.** `SceneDrawList::rebuild` is set by `addEntityToDrawList` (from `addEntityToScene`, when the entity already has rows) and by `markDrawListsDirty(entity)`, which marks only the scenes that hold the entity. That happens when the entity becomes drawable (rows and textures ready), starts waiting for a texture again, or changes cull mode. `rebuildDrawList` walks the scene's entities in order. It adds one `DrawInfo` per drawable sub-mesh of each entity that has rows and does not wait for textures. The draws are stable-sorted by alpha mode (`NONE`, `MASK`, `BLEND`), then cull mode, the `BLEND` ones by blend method (`getRunKey`), and stored as runs `{alphaMode, cullMode, points, blendMethod, first, count}`: at most `MAX_DRAW_RUNS` (10), three cull modes for each of `NONE` and `MASK`, one point run (`DrawRun::points`, the point cloud chunks with a `POINTS` material and the glyph chunks with a `GLYPHS` material, below) and three `BLEND` runs, weighted, additive and sorted (`GpuScene::instanceBlendMethods`, from `getBlendMethod`), all with cull mode `NONE`, the sorted one last so that it can be drawn in one sorted order. The list is then stale in both slots (`staleSlots`), and `uploadDrawList` writes it into each slot's buffer in that slot's frame. Growing a slot's buffer destroys the old one, which only that slot's last frame used.

**`syncGpuScene`** runs in `beginFrame`, after the fence wait for the frame's slot. It assigns rows to entities whose mesh is ready, checks the entities waiting for textures, rebuilds dirty draw lists, then uploads this slot's dirty instance and material rows and its stale draw lists. Its cost follows what changed, except that a scene holding a changed entity rebuilds its whole draw list (`P7`). A change made after `beginFrame` reaches the GPU in the next frame.

**The scene passes.** `Renderer::addScenePasses(graph, scene, camera, renderTarget, shadows, shadowCount)` adds the passes that draw a scene into `renderTarget`. They are ordinary graph passes: the graph sorts them, records their barriers from what they declare (§9), and gives later passes on the same target `load` ops. A depth-only `renderTarget` (no colors, `D32_SFLOAT` depth) gets the scene's depth only, and its passes are named `Shadow Cull 1`, `Shadow 1`, … instead (`Cascade 0 Cull 1`, `Cascade 0 1`, … for a shadow cascade, `Spot 2 Cull 1`, `Spot 2 1`, … for the map of spot light 2, `Cube 3 Face 0 Cull`, `Cube 3 Face 0`, … for the cube of point light 3); `shadows` (below, at most `MAX_VIEW_SHADOWS`, 4) are such targets that the shaded view samples.

```
Cull 1 (compute)          Scene 1 (graphics)     Hi-Z (compute)          Cull 2 (compute)          Scene 2 (graphics)      Transparent (graphics)
prepareSceneView,         early draws,           readDepth(target),      late countMain,           late draws, the sky     the blend run, back to
early countMain,          clears the target      hizMain per level       writeMain, blend sort     loads the target        front; loads the target
writeMain, light clusters
readPrevious: visibility  read: visible draws,   write: hiz              read: hiz                 read: visible draws,    read: visible draws,
write: visible draws,       light clusters                               write: visible draws,       light clusters          light clusters
  sort buffers, clusters                                                   visibility, sort buffers
```

A shaded view has a `Label Depth` compute pass between `Scene 2` (or `Scene`) and `Transparent`: `readDepth(target)`, read the label probes, write the label buffers, `readPrevious` and write the label fades (see **Label placement** below). The visible draws hold the draw counts too. The cull stats are not declared: views only add to them with atomics, and only the CPU reads them, after the slot's fence, so views need no order between them. Without `EngineConfig::occlusionCulling`, or when the view's visibility or Hi-Z buffers cannot be made (an error, then frustum culling only), it adds only `Cull` and `Scene`. A shaded view always ends with `Transparent` (`drawGrid`, then `drawTransparent`), also while its scene has no `BLEND` draws, since the graph is fixed and the scene is not; a depth-only view has none. Adding the same view twice to one graph logs an error, adds nothing and returns `false`. An invalid scene, camera or render target, a render target that has neither one `SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT` color and `D32_SFLOAT` depth (the uber pipeline's formats) nor `D32_SFLOAT` depth only, or a `shadow` on a depth-only view, or one that `addShadowPasses` did not make for the same scene and camera in this graph, logs an error, adds nothing and returns `false`.

**Shadows.** A light's shadow is a set of depth-only maps, each drawn by a scene view of its own. `Renderer::addShadowPasses(graph, scene, camera, ShadowDesc{light, cascadeCount, size, distance, splitLambda})` creates the maps of `size`² in the graph, adds the scene passes of each as a view of `camera` (whose LODs they use), and returns the `SceneShadow{light, maps, mapCount, distance, splitLambda}` to give to `addScenePasses` for the shaded view (`examples/06_shadows`, `examples/07_lights`). It depends on the light's type:

| Light | Maps (`SceneShadow::maps`) | Views | `distance` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DIRECTIONAL` | `cascadeCount` (1 to `MAX_SHADOW_CASCADES`, 4) cascades `shadow_cascade_i`, each an orthographic box around a slice of the camera's view | one per cascade | from the camera, beyond its near plane |
| `SPOT` | one map `shadow_spot_<light>`, a perspective camera at the light along its direction, field of view 2 × `outerConeAngle` (at most 160°) | one | the far plane, from the light |
| `POINT` | one cube `shadow_cube_<light>` (`RenderTargetDesc::cube`), six 90° faces | one per face (6) | the far plane, from the light |

It rejects an invalid light, a zero size, for a directional light a count outside 1–4, a `distance` not beyond the camera's near plane or a `splitLambda` outside 0–1, for the others a `distance` that is not finite and above 0, and fewer free scene views than the shadow's views; it adds nothing then, and returns a shadow with `mapCount` 0. A spot or point light's near plane is `distance` / 200 (`localShadows.cpp`). `addScenePasses` takes up to `MAX_VIEW_SHADOWS` (4) shadows, of different lights, each only for the scene and camera it was made for in the same graph, and each for one view: cascades are fitted to that view's aspect ratio. Every view of a shadow computes the maps' matrices again every frame in `prepareSceneView` with the same function (`getShadowMaps` in `gpuScene.cpp`), so the maps are drawn and read with the same matrices and no light camera exists. The maps' views are world-space `glm::dmat4`s, made in doubles from the camera's world view and the light's double position, and each view moves them into its render space with `toRenderMatrix` (below):
- `getShadowSplits` (`src/renderer/shadowCascades.cpp`) puts the end of cascade *i* at `splitLambda` · logarithmic + (1 − `splitLambda`) · even split of near…`distance`; the last split is `distance`. An orthographic camera's near plane can be at or behind it, and then the split is even. When the near plane has moved to `distance` or beyond since `addShadowPasses` (`setCameraProjection`), or the light is no longer in the scene, the shaded view has no shadow from that light and its maps' views draw nothing.
- `fitShadowCascade` takes the smallest sphere centred on the view axis around the slice, in doubles. It depends only on the projection and the two depths, so turning the camera keeps its size. The box around it is an orthographic camera along the light, moved in whole texels in the light's x and y, so moving the camera does not move the texel grid and shadow edges do not crawl. The snap is in world space, in doubles: in render space, whose origin moves with the camera, the grid would move too. The box reaches `distance` past the sphere toward the light, for casters outside the slice.
- `getSpotShadowView` and `getSpotShadowProjection` (`src/renderer/localShadows.cpp`) look from the light along its direction; the map's square holds the whole cone.
- `getCubeFaceView` and `getCubeFaceProjection` give face *f* of a point light's cube in Vulkan's order (+X, −X, +Y, −Y, +Z, −Z). Vulkan samples a face's texel (s, t) at direction `forward + S·(2s − 1) + T·(2t − 1)` (the spec's cube map face table, `CUBE_FORWARD`, `CUBE_S` and `CUBE_T` in both `localShadows.cpp` and `shadow.slang`), so the face's view has rows S, −T and −forward. That frame is mirrored, so the face's view (`getShadowMaps` with the view's `face`) sets `SceneView::mirrored` (its 3x3 has a negative determinant) and `drawSceneView` swaps `BACK` and `FRONT` culling. `tests/localShadowsTests.cpp` checks every face against the table.

A map's view uses the map's matrices for its frustum planes and culling, and the camera's own projection and the shaded view's target size for LOD. A cube's faces are frustum culled only (no Hi-Z: the Hi-Z build reads a 2D depth). The shaded view's draw passes declare `readDepth` on every map, so the graph runs them after the map passes, makes the last pass of each store its depth and moves it to `SAMPLED` before them; the next frame's first map pass moves it back. `prepareSceneView` writes the maps into `SceneData::shadowMaps` (`MAX_SHADOW_MAPS`, 16 = 4 lights × 4 maps), one table for all the view's shadows: each `gfx::ShadowMap` has a `matrix` (projection times view for a cascade or a spot map, the faces' projection for a cube), the depth's bindless `texture` and a cascade's `split`. Each shadowed light is a `gfx::ShadowedLight` in `SceneData::shadowedLights` (`shadowedLightCount` of them): its row in the light table, the index of its first map and their number; a light no longer in its scene (`GpuLights::lightScenes`, a bit per scene for each light) has none. `shadePBR` shades these lights in a loop of their own (below, lights) and calls `sampleShadow` (`shaders/lib/shadow.slang`) only where the light reaches the surface (`P12`); the ambient term is not shadowed. Per light type:
- A directional light takes the fragment's view depth, picks the first cascade whose split is beyond it, and reads it with `sampleShadowMap`. A lookup that the offset pushes off a cascade's edge goes to the next cascade. The cascade loop is unrolled, so each cascade's texture index is a constant element of `SceneData`, uniform over the draw: neighbouring pixels may pick different cascades without `NonUniformResourceIndex`, which would make Slang raise the SPIR-V profile. Past the last split a fragment is lit, faded in over the last tenth of `distance` so the shadow does not end in a line.
- A spot light reads its one map with `sampleShadowMap`; outside the map it is lit.
- A point light reads its cube with `sampleShadowCube` through `gCubeTextures[]`, a `SamplerCube` array declared on the same binding as `gTextures[]` (`rasm.slang`): a slot is read only through the declaration that matches its view type. It picks the face by the biggest axis of fragment − light, whose length along that axis is the depth in the face's projection (`getCubeDepth`), and builds the PCF on that face; each gather turns a texel corner back into a direction, so a gather on a face's edge reads the next face (Vulkan's cube sampling is seamless), and compares with the depth along the axis of the face its corner falls on.

Both lookups move the point off the surface by about one texel of the map in world units, along its normal (more where the light grazes the surface) and toward the light (`offsetLookup`). A texel covers `2 · w / (|row 0 of the matrix| · size)`, with `w` the point's clip w: 1 everywhere in an orthographic cascade, the depth from the light in a perspective map. Then a 3x3 PCF with bilinear weights (`getPcfWeights`) compares the 4x4 texels around it, read as four 2x2 `Gather`s (reversed-Z: lit when its depth is at least the map's). Slang counts any gather as the `spvImageGatherExtended` capability, so `ShaderCompiler::getTargetDesc` names it for SPIR-V; a plain gather emits no such SPIR-V capability. `examples/07_lights` has a directional light (4 cascades), a spot light and a point light: 11 map views and the shaded view take 12 of the `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` (16) (`D20`).

**Scene views.** Each (scene, camera, target, face, shadows) is a `SceneView` in `GpuScene::views`, at most `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` (32) at once (one more logs an error naming the limit, and that view adds nothing); `face` is the cube face a point light's map view draws, and a map's view holds its own shadow as `shadows[0]`. A view lives while a graph holds it: the graph keeps the views it added (`RenderGraph::sceneViews`), and graphs built for the same five share one view (`createSceneView` counts them in `SceneView::graphs`). A destroyed or replaced graph lets go of its views (`releaseSceneView`); the last one waits for the submitted frames and frees the view's buffers, and its slot is free for a new view. A view owns a visible-draw buffer (with its draw counts) and a Hi-Z buffer per frame slot and its visibility flags, and has its own slot in `GpuScene::sceneData[frame]`. The GPU reads `SceneData` after the whole frame is recorded, so each view needs its own slot, visible list and counts; that is what lets one frame draw scenes with several cameras. A graph's declarations are fixed when the passes are added, so the passes declare the per-slot buffers of both slots; the frame order needs those barriers anyway. The buffers start small and grow with `RenderContext::resizeBuffer`, which keeps the handle, so the declarations stay valid. Two graphs that draw the same view share its buffers, and the graph does not order buffers between graphs (§9).

**`prepareSceneView`** runs at the start of the view's `Cull` pass, every frame, and decides what the view's passes record:
1. Nothing when the scene's draw list is empty or this slot's copy could not be uploaded. The scene passes keep their cleared attachments. A color view still writes the camera and the environment into its `SceneData` then, so the sky shows while the meshes load.
2. Creates the uber pipeline on first use: `Renderer::createScenePipeline` with `shaders/passes/rasm.slang` (`vertMain`/`fragMain`), no vertex input, the bindless set layout and the formats and sample count of the view's target (`SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT` and `D32_SFLOAT` depth). A shaded view's pipelines (uber, `MASK`, `BLEND`, sky) are a `ShadedPipelines` set of `GpuScene::shaded`, one per sample count (1, 2, 4, 8; `getShadedPipelines`), so views with and without MSAA can share a frame. A depth-only view uses `GpuScene::depthPipeline` instead, from `shaders/passes/shadow.slang` (the same vertex pulling, an empty `fragMain`) and the target's formats: no color. The pipelines of the other alpha modes are made the first time the scene's draw list has their run (below), so a scene without them compiles nothing more; a failed one leaves out only its runs. With a `BLEND` run and culling, it also makes the four sort pipelines of `shaders/passes/sort.slang` and grows the slot's sort buffer to the run. The culling pipelines come from `Renderer::createComputePipeline` with the `countMain` and `writeMain` entry points of `shaders/passes/cull.slang` and (with `EngineConfig::occlusionCulling`) `hizMain` of `shaders/passes/hiz.slang`. If either of the first two fails, the view draws its whole list unculled with `drawIndexedIndirect`, so a culling shader error does not blank the scene. Without `hizMain` it is frustum culled only. A shader error logs and retries next frame, for the failed pipelines only, so a shader fixed on disk is picked up while the application runs. Cull mode is dynamic state, so one pipeline serves all cull modes.
3. Grows the slot's visible-draw buffer to the draw list's size if needed, and for occlusion culling the slot's Hi-Z buffer to the target's size. It samples the target's depth texture through the bindless array (`NEAREST_CLAMP`), where the texture went when it was created: `createSwapchainResources` (a depth texture replaced by a resize takes the old one's slot) and `RenderContext::createAttachments`, both only with `EngineConfig::occlusionCulling`, which also gives the depth textures `SAMPLED` usage (a depth-only target's depth, and the depth of a target with a `MOTION_FORMAT` color for TAA, always have both). The view is frustum culled only this frame (`hizLevelCount` 0) when the depth texture has no slot or the Hi-Z buffer cannot grow: the Hi-Z covers the whole attachment and `isOccluded` maps NDC onto it.
4. Writes the view's slot of `GpuScene::sceneData[frame]`, which holds `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` host-visible `gfx::SceneData`s per frame slot: the view's origin (`originHigh`, `originLow`: its camera's position, split), camera (its projection fitted to the target's aspect ratio, its view in render space), the six render-space frustum planes, lights, the scene's environment (`writeSceneEnvironment`, below; none for a depth-only view), the run starts and the blend run (`blendRun`, `MAX_DRAW_RUNS` for none and for a depth-only view), the weighted and additive runs and the weighted fields (`weightedRun`, `additiveRun`, `weightedRangePtr`, `weightedDepths`, the weighted target's bindless indices and samples: `prepareWeightedBlend`, below), the target's size, the depth texture's bindless index, the Hi-Z level count (0 without occlusion culling), the shadow map table (above), and the addresses of the geometry pool and its color pool (`colorsPtr`), this slot's animated transforms and skinned vertices and the other slot's skinned vertices (`animatedTransformsPtr`, `skinnedVertsPtr`, `previousSkinnedVertsPtr`), the blend sort's keys and copied draws (`sortKeysPtr`, `sortDrawsPtr`, with `sortCapacity`), the scene's whole draw list (`allDrawsPtr`), the view's visible draws (`drawInfoPtr`), draw counts and culling scratch, the visibility flags and the Hi-Z, the instance and material tables, and the geometry table.
5. Counts the view's cull groups and adds its draws and triangles to `GpuScene::drawTotals`.

`recordSceneCulling` (both `Cull` passes) dispatches `countMain`, records a `COMPUTE → COMPUTE` barrier inside the pass, and dispatches `writeMain`; in the phase that keeps the blend run's draws it then sorts them (`recordBlendSort`, below). `drawSceneView` (both `Scene` passes) binds the geometry pool's index buffer, then per run the run's pipeline when it changes (`NONE`: the uber or the depth-only pipeline; `MASK`, and `BLEND` in a depth-only view: the mask ones) with the bindless set, `setCullMode`, push constant `{SceneData address, firstDraw = run.first, argument = CULL_EARLY or CULL_LATE}` (the pass; only the culling view reads it), and `drawIndexedIndirectCount(visible draws + run.first, count = the run's draw count, max = run.count)` (unculled: `drawIndexedIndirect` of the whole list). A shaded view leaves its blend run to `drawTransparent`. It sets the cull mode back to `NONE`, which is what `beginPass` sets for every other pipeline. `Hi-Z`, `Cull 2` and `Scene 2` record nothing in a frame without a late half (`SceneView::late`: false when `hizLevelCount` is 0 or culling is frozen, below). The view's last draw pass of the frame (`Scene 2`, or `Scene 1` in such a frame, or `Scene` without occlusion culling) then calls `drawSky` (below).

**GPU frustum culling.** Each run of the draw list is split into groups of 64 draws (`getCullGroup`), the groups of run 0 first:
- `countMain` tests each draw. It transforms the geometry table's bounding sphere with the instance's world matrix and scales the radius by the matrix's largest stretch, bounded from above with Gershgorin's theorem on `transpose(M) * M`. The bound is exact when the columns are orthogonal, and never too small under a non-uniform scale followed by a rotation.
- A sphere fully outside one of the frustum planes is culled. The planes come from the rows of projection × view (`getFrustumPlanes`), normalized, in the view's render space. The infinite reversed-Z far plane comes out as `(0, 0, 0, nearZ)`, which every sphere passes.
- `countMain` picks each visible draw's LOD and writes the draw's slot among its group's visible draws, with the LOD above it (`slot | lod << 16`, or `CULLED`), to `cullSlotsPtr`, and the group's visible count to `groupCountsPtr`. The group's last thread also adds its visible draws and triangles (at the picked LOD and at LOD 0) to `cullStatsPtr`, and threads 0 to 7 add the group's draws per LOD, for the frame stats (§3).
- `writeMain` adds up the counts of the run's earlier groups, copies each visible `DrawInfo` to `drawInfoPtr[runFirst[run] + that sum + slot]` with the index range of the LOD `countMain` picked (below), and the run's last group writes the run's count. The visible draws therefore keep the draw list's order every frame, and no count needs zeroing.

**Occlusion culling** (`EngineConfig::occlusionCulling`, on by default) is the two-pass Hi-Z scheme. The push constant's `argument` tells `countMain` the phase.

```
Cull 1                   Scene 1        Hi-Z                         Cull 2                                Scene 2
in frustum AND           early draws    depth → SAMPLED (the graph)  seen = in frustum AND not occluded    late draws
visibility[row] == 1     (stores        hizMain, one dispatch        visibility[row] = seen                (depth → DEPTH and
                          its depth)    per level                    draw if seen AND not drawn early       loaded, beginPass)
```

- `Cull 1` reads the visibility flags that the last frame's `Cull 2` wrote. It declares them with `PassBuilder::readPrevious(buffer)`: that adds no edge from `Cull 2`, makes `Cull 1` run before this frame's `Cull 2`, and still waits for the previous write. Rows start at 0, so a new instance is drawn by the late pass first. The late culling reuses the view's visible-draw list and counts: `Scene 1` read them, so the graph records a `GRAPHICS | COMPUTE → COMPUTE` barrier before `Cull 2`. The late `countMain` reads the early pass's `cullSlotsPtr` entry of the draw before it overwrites it, so it knows what was drawn early.
- Each view has its own flags, one per instance row, shared by both frame slots because the GPU runs the frames in order: only the compute passes use them, and those run on one queue.
- `Cull 1`, `Hi-Z` and `Cull 2` are async compute passes (`addAsyncComputePass`, §9), so with `EngineConfig::asyncCompute` they run on the compute queue. The visible draws and the Hi-Z are declared per frame slot (`PassBuilder::write(perFrame)`), so `Cull 1` of a frame waits only for the frame two before, which the slot's fences cover, and not for the last frame's draws. It can then run next to them.
- The Hi-Z is a buffer of `float`s. Level 0 is half the target's size, rounded up, and each level halves the one before down to 1x1 (`getHizLevelCount`; in the shaders `getHizLevel` gives a level's offset and size). A level-L texel holds the farthest depth (reversed-Z: the smallest value) of the 2^(L+1)-pixel square it covers; an odd size repeats the last row or column. `hizMain` reads level 0 from the depth texture with `Load` through the bindless array (`loadDepth`: with `SceneData::depthSamples` above 1, every sample through `Sampler2DMS`, keeping the farthest, so a multisampled target needs no depth resolve), and every other level from the level before; `recordHiz` records a `COMPUTE → COMPUTE` barrier between levels.
- `isOccluded` projects the eight corners of the view-space box around the bounding sphere, so it works for perspective and orthographic cameras. A box that reaches behind the camera or past the near plane is visible. Otherwise it takes the first level where the box's pixel rectangle covers at most 2x2 texels, and the draw is occluded when its nearest depth is farther than the farthest of those texels.
- The `Hi-Z` pass declares `readDepth(target)`. So the graph makes `Scene 1` store its depth, moves the depth to `SAMPLED` before `Hi-Z`, and `Scene 2`'s `beginPass` moves it back to an attachment and loads it. Depth is otherwise `DONT_CARE` at the end of a pass unless a later pass loads or reads it.

**Level of detail.** The draw list always holds LOD 0. For each visible draw, `selectLod` (`shaders/lib/lodSelection.slang`) picks the coarsest LOD whose error, seen from the view's LOD camera, stays under `SceneData::lodErrorPixels` (`EngineConfig::lodErrorPixels`, 1 pixel by default; 0 keeps LOD 0), and replaces the draw's `firstIndex` and `indexCount` with that LOD's range. Only `countMain` calls it: it counts the LOD's triangles and keeps the LOD in `cullSlotsPtr` for `writeMain`. The LOD camera comes in as `SceneData::lodPixelsPerUnit` (pixels per world unit at view depth 1) and `lodDepthPlane` (a point's view depth is its dot product with the plane; zero for an orthographic camera). It is the view's own camera. A shadow cascade's view has the camera of the view that reads it, and takes the size of that view's target (the first one, if several read it), so a caster is drawn into the map at the LOD the main view shades it with, and a surface is not shadowed by a different version of itself.

```
 pixels = lods[i].error × sphere.scale × |projection[1][1]| × viewportHeight / 2 / depth
 depth  = clip w of the sphere center − sphere radius   (≤ 0: the sphere reaches the camera plane, LOD 0)
```

- `EngineConfig::forcedLod` (`SceneData::forcedLod`, -1 by default) overrides the error test for debugging: from 0 up, every draw uses that LOD, or its mesh's coarsest one if it has fewer. F2 cycles it at runtime through -1, 0, …, `MAX_MESH_LODS - 1` (`pollEvents`), and the stats overlay shows the mode.
- `sphere.scale` is the world matrix's largest stretch, the same bound the frustum test uses for the radius. An orthographic projection (`projection[3][3] == 1`) skips the division by depth. The view depth, not the distance, is what `projection[1][1]` divides by: off the screen's centre an object is nearer in depth than in distance, and the distance would pick too coarse a LOD there.
- All LODs of a sub-mesh share its vertices, so `vertexOffset` stays the same and the vertex shader needs no change. An unculled view draws LOD 0.

**Meshlets** (guide: `docs/guides/meshlets.html`; example `18_meshlets`). With `EngineConfig::meshletPath` other than `MeshletPath::OFF` (`Renderer::setMeshletPath` at run time), a visible draw whose LOD has at least `MESHLET_MIN_DRAW` (8) meshlets, in a run that is not the blend run, is culled again per meshlet. `VERTEX_PULLING` culls the meshlets in two more compute passes and draws one indirect draw per visible meshlet; `MESH_SHADERS` culls them in a task shader inside the draw pass and draws them with mesh shaders (below). The default, `AUTO`, is `MESH_SHADERS` where `RenderContext::hasMeshShaders`, else `OFF` (`P28`); `MESH_SHADERS` without them draws by vertex pulling (`resolveMeshletPath`).

```
countMain (per draw)        writeMain (per draw)                meshletCountMain (per chunk)         meshletWriteMain (per chunk)
frustum, Hi-Z, LOD;         small draws → visible draws;        frustum, cone (object space), Hi-Z   slot = Σ earlier chunkSums +
a LOD with ≥ 8 meshlets     meshlet draws → chunks of 64        per meshlet; mask → chunkMasksPtr;   earlier chunks of its 64 + bits
→ CULL_MESHLETS, its        meshlets in list order, each run's  count → chunkSumsPtr (atomic);      below it → a DrawInfo per meshlet
chunks and meshlets per     from runChunkFirst; per run: the    late: the view's meshlet bits        at runMeshletFirst; the run's last
group (groupMeshletsPtr)    dispatch args (meshletArgsPtr)                                           chunk writes meshletDrawCountsPtr
```

- **Build** (`src/assets/meshlets.cpp`). `buildMeshlets` runs after `buildMeshLods` in the same child job (`createMesh`: on the calling thread), always, whatever the path: every LOD of a sub-mesh with at least `MIN_MESHLET_TRIANGLES` (1024) triangles and no skin and no morph targets is cut with `meshopt_buildMeshlets` (`MESHLET_MAX_VERTICES` 64, `MESHLET_MAX_TRIANGLES` 124, cone weight 0.25), each meshlet reordered with `meshopt_optimizeMeshlet` and bounded with `meshopt_computeMeshletBounds`, and the LOD's indices are written again in meshlet order (`ObjRaw::meshlets`, `lodMeshletCounts`). For the mesh shaders each meshlet also writes its vertices (sub-mesh vertex numbers) and then its triangles, three local numbers of a byte in one word each, to `ObjRaw::meshletData` (`Meshlet::firstData`, `vertexCount`). A meshlet is a range of them, so a whole draw of the LOD draws the same triangles and the image does not depend on the path (the `meshlets` and `meshlet_sponza` image tests compare both with threshold 0). Skinned and morphed vertices move, so their meshlets' bounds would not hold (`D37`).
- **Storage.** `GeometryPool::allocate` takes a range of the meshlet stream when it fits (otherwise the sub-mesh has no meshlets and is drawn whole, with one warning) (`EngineConfig::geometryPoolMeshlets`, `gfx::GeometryMeshlet`, 48 B: sphere, cone axis and cutoff, `firstIndex` in the index pool, `indexCount`, `firstData` in the meshlet data stream, `vertexCount`), freed with the mesh; where the device has mesh shaders, also a range of the meshlet data stream (`EngineConfig::geometryPoolMeshletData` words, `GeometryPool::meshletDataBuffer`, `SceneData::meshletDataPtr`; a sub-mesh whose data does not fit has no meshlets); each `GeometryLod` names its `firstMeshlet` and `meshletCount`. `MeshGeometry::maxLodMeshlets` sizes an instance's bits.
- **Bits.** Each instance row of a mesh with meshlets takes `ceil(maxLodMeshlets / 32)` words of `GpuScene::meshletWords` (`EngineConfig::maxInstances` words, freed with the rows; a row that finds none has `InstanceData::meshletVisibility` -1 and no bits) when it gets its rows. Each view with occlusion culling has its own `SceneView::meshletVisibility` of that many words, zero when made: `Cull 1` reads it with `readPrevious`, `Cull 2` writes it. The early phase draws a meshlet in the frustum, facing, and with its bit set; the late one tests every meshlet of a seen instance against the Hi-Z, writes the chunk's two words, and draws the seen ones the early phase did not (`CHUNK_DRAWN_EARLY`, `CHUNK_EARLY_MESHLETS`). The bits are a hint: the late phase draws what the early one left out whatever they hold.
- **Cone test.** `isMeshletBackFacing` moves `SceneData::cullViewPoint` (the view's camera position, or an orthographic view's direction; kept with frozen culling) into object space with the transposed normal matrix, so it is exact under any affine matrix, mirrors included, and only runs in runs with a cull mode (`SceneData::coneRuns`).
- **Buffers.** The visible draw buffer (`getVisibleDrawLayout`) holds the draws, then the meshlet draws (so both are `DrawInfo`s at `drawInfoPtr`, and `drawSceneView` draws a run's meshlets with `firstDraw` = `visibleCapacity` + `runMeshletFirst`), then the scratch. `prepareSceneView` gives each meshlet run, in order, `min(DrawRun::meshlets, budget left)` of `EngineConfig::maxViewMeshlets` plus one draw per meshlet draw of the run (`DrawRun::meshletDraws`): `writeMain` draws a draw whose meshlets do not fit by the budget prefix whole (`CHUNK_WHOLE`, `FrameStats::meshletDrawsWhole`). Chunks get `DrawRun::chunks`, each run's from a multiple of 64. The buffer grows only for views that draw meshlets, to what their meshes can need: in `18_meshlets` (the camera and 4 cascades, 2756 draws of meshes with meshlets) about 1.6 MiB per view and frame slot, 16 MiB in all, plus 256 KiB of bits per view (2¹⁶ words).
- **Dispatches.** `recordMeshletCulling` (inside each `Cull` pass, GPU timer `Meshlet Cull`, vertex pulling only): a barrier, `meshletCountMain` per run with `RenderContext::dispatchIndirect` (x up to 65535, then rows), a barrier, `meshletWriteMain` the same way.
- **Mesh shaders** (`MESH_SHADERS`, `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`, enabled by `VulkanDevice::enableMeshShaders` whenever the GPU has it with limits that hold 64-thread workgroups, 64 vertices and 124 triangles; then the shared pipeline layout's push constants and `PipelineStage::GRAPHICS` barriers include the task and mesh stages). `writeMain` writes the same chunks and per-run group counts, with no budget (`runMeshletBudget` = `UINT32_MAX`, no meshlet draws, no `recordMeshletCulling`). `drawSceneView` draws a meshlet run's chunks with `RenderContext::drawMeshTasksIndirect` on `meshletArgsPtr` (`PushConstants::argument` = phase | run << `MESHLET_RUN_SHIFT`): `taskMain` (`shaders/lib/meshletTask.slang`), a workgroup per chunk, runs `cullMeshletChunk` (`shaders/lib/meshletCulling.slang`, the test `meshletCountMain` runs too, so both paths keep the same meshlets) and launches a mesh workgroup per visible meshlet in chunk order with the chunk and the lanes in its payload. `meshMain` (`rasm.slang`, `shadow.slang`; `meshMask` for depth-only MASK) reads the meshlet's vertices through `getVertexOutput` / `getDepthPosition`, the same functions `vertMain` uses, and its packed triangles. So the draw passes now touch the meshlet bits: `Scene 1` reads them (`readPrevious`), `Scene 2` reads the Hi-Z and writes them. Pipelines (`Renderer::createMeshletPipelines`, `createMeshScenePipeline`): shaded and MASK (alpha-to-coverage with MSAA), per debug view, OVERDRAW and depth-only, made the first time a view needs them; one that fails leaves the view on vertex pulling (on whole draws under `AUTO`). Without the meshlet data stream (`geometryPoolMeshletData` = 0) the path resolves as on a GPU without mesh shaders.
- **Cost** (`P28`; `--bench`, Release, headless, 1920 × 1080, RTX 3060, 600 frames after 120; off / vertex pulling / mesh shaders): `18_meshlets` with LODs 1.97 / 2.18 / 1.99 ms (3.32 → 2.33 M triangles); without shadows 0.58 / 0.61 / 0.54 ms; every mesh at LOD 0 5.81 / 5.59 / 5.15 ms (21.4 → 15.9 M by vertex pulling, which hits its budget, 13.1 M by mesh shaders); LOD 0 without shadows 1.71 / 1.17 / 1.19 ms (4.72 → 1.80 M); async compute 1.96 / 2.22 / 1.97 ms. `05_city`: 1.91 / 1.92 / 1.93 ms. The mesh shaders cost about 1% where LODs already cut the triangles and save up to 30% where they do not, so `AUTO` takes them where the GPU has them; vertex pulling stays an explicit choice.

### Render space

Positions are doubles on the CPU (`Transform::position`, `Light::position`, `CameraController::TrackBall::center` are `glm::dvec3`; `Transform::toMatrix` and `getViewMatrix` return `glm::dmat4`), so a scene in Earth-centred coordinates (millions of metres from the origin) keeps nanometres. The GPU never sees world space: each scene view draws in its camera's **render space**, the world moved so that the camera's position is the origin (guide: `docs/guides/large-world-precision.html`; example `23_large_world`).

```
CPU, on change                                   CPU, each frame per view                       GPU
writeWorldRows(entity x node, doubles)           prepareSceneView                               toRenderSpace (core.slang)
  row.world[3]: linear part, w = float(t)          SceneData::originHigh/originLow =              subtractSplit (renderSpace.slang):
  row.translationLow = float(t - float(t))           splitPosition(camera position)               (high - originHigh) + (low - originLow)
writeLightRow: position, positionLow             view, shadow matrices = toRenderMatrix(         getWorldMatrix, getLight,
writeAnimationRow: AnimatedEntity::entity[3]       world-space dmat4, origin)                    getPreviousWorldPosition
```

- **Rows.** `splitPosition` (`src/renderer/renderSpace.cpp`) keeps a double as two floats, `high = float(d)` and `low = float(d − high)`. `InstanceData`, `InstanceTransform` and `AnimatedEntity` store the three rows of their affine matrix with the translation's high part in w, and `translationLow`; `gfx::Light` stores `position` and `positionLow`. Moving the camera rewrites no row: the CPU cost of a frame does not grow with the entities (`examples/23_large_world --pillars 12000`, a flying camera, Release, RTX 3060: 1.34 ms per frame on main and on this path, `syncGpuScene` 0.3 µs).
- **Shaders.** `toRenderSpace` subtracts the view's origin part by part with `subtractSplit`: near the camera the high parts are equal or a few float steps apart, so their difference is exact, and the low parts carry the rest (error about 2⁻⁴⁸ of the distance from the origin, `tests/renderSpaceTests.cpp`). Every step is ORed with `SceneData::zero` (`AnimatePushConstants::zero` in the Animate pass), a 0 the compiler cannot know, so no compiler or driver regroups the sums: Slang drops `precise`, and the NVIDIA driver regroups across SPIR-V's `NoContraction` (lighting then falls in 0.5 m blocks). `getWorldMatrix` gives a row's matrix in render space and `getLight` a light with its render-space position; nothing reads a world position. The camera is at 0, so `fragMain` uses `float3(0)` as the camera position.
- **Animated rows.** `animateMain` keeps the entity's high part and adds the node's offset, turned and scaled by the entity, to its low part.
- **Matrices.** View, shadow and LOD matrices are built in world space in doubles and moved with `toRenderMatrix(matrix, origin)` (`matrix × translate(origin)`, then floats): the view becomes the rotation alone, the frustum planes, `lodDepthPlane` and `cullViewPoint` come out in render space. A shadow map's views use the camera of `addShadowPasses`, so a map is drawn and read in the same render space.
- **Across frames.** The origin moves with the camera, so what a view keeps from the last frame is kept in world space or with its origin: `SceneView::previousViewProjection` is a world-space `glm::dmat4` moved into this frame's render space by `writeViewMotion`; a row's `previousWorld` holds last − this translation in w (`writePreviousRows`, `EntityDrawState::syncedTransform`; `animateMain` from the other slot's rows); frozen culling keeps `frozenOrigin` and moves its planes and LOD plane with `moveRenderPlane`. The Hi-Z and the visibility flags need nothing: the Hi-Z is built from this frame's depth, and the flags hold no position.
- **Conversions.** RASM's own targets build with `GLM_FORCE_EXPLICIT_CTOR` (`src/CMakeLists.txt`, PRIVATE), so a `glm::dvec3` or `glm::dmat4` becomes a float type only through a written cast; applications keep glm's implicit conversions. `toRenderPosition` and `toWorldPosition` (`rasm/renderSpace.h`) convert a position between world space and a view's render space, for picking and readbacks.
- **Depth.** Unchanged: reversed-Z with `D32_SFLOAT` and an infinite far plane is a distance from the camera, about 2⁻²³ of the distance per step (12 cm at 1000 km).

### Terrain

A terrain tile is an entity whose `MaterialType::TERRAIN` material holds the tile's heights (guide: `docs/guides/terrain.html`; example `24_map_tiles`). The code is `include/rasm/terrain.h`, `src/scene/terrain.cpp` (with the private `src/scene/terrain.h`) and `shaders/lib/terrain.slang`.

```
CPU, once per tile                                          GPU, every vertex of a TERRAIN draw (main and shadow passes)
createTerrainTile(TerrainTileDesc)                          getVertexOutput / getDepthPosition: materialId == TERRAIN
  material: TerrainMaterial -> gfx::TerrainData row           getTerrainVertex: load sample (i + 1, j + 1) and its 4 neighbours,
  entity on getTerrainMesh(heights width - 3)                   h = offset + scale x value, minus d^2 / 2R, minus the skirt,
  transform: getTerrainTransform (the tile's box)               y = (h - box floor) / box height; normal from the slopes
```

- **The grid.** `Renderer::getTerrainMesh(cells)` builds `buildTerrainGrid` once per size (1 to `MAX_TERRAIN_CELLS`, 256) straight into the geometry pool, with no LODs and no meshlets (their bounds would be the flat grid's), and keeps it in `Renderer::terrainMeshes` until the renderer goes; `destroyMesh` refuses it. Its (cells + 1)² vertices sit at y = 1 and a skirt of 4 (cells + 1) at y = 0, so its bounds are the unit box; `uv[0]` is (u, v), `uv[1]` the grid point (i, j), `uv[2].x` the cells. Each cell is two triangles split from (i, j + 1) to (i + 1, j).
- **The box.** `getTerrainTransform` stretches the unit box to the tile: x and z by its size, y from `minHeight` lowered by the skirt and by the curvature at the corners (size² / 4R) to `maxHeight` (at least 1 mm), in the tile's orientation. Culling, Hi-Z and LOD selection use the grid's sphere through that matrix like any mesh's. The shader reads the box's size and height back from the matrix's columns.
- **Heights.** `R16_UNORM` (a sample is its integer: `round(s × 65535)`) or `R32_SFLOAT`, (cells + 3)² samples with a border of one, read with `Load` (no filtering), through a `NEAREST_CLAMP` bindless slot that `updateMaterial` gives the texture. Metres = `heightOffset` + `heightScale` × value, in floats. A power-of-two scale and an offset that is a multiple of it make neighbours' edges bit-equal (`tests/terrainTests.cpp`). A heights texture is waited for like any material texture (`texturesLoaded`), and `detachTexture` clears it (the shader then draws the tile flat).
- **Normals.** Per vertex, from the central differences of the four samples around the point (the border gives an edge point the same four on both tiles) plus the curvature's slope, in the box's coordinates; the normal matrix takes them to world space. `fragMain` shades `TERRAIN` as `PBR`, with the imagery as base color (`textures[ALBEDO]`, `textureTransforms[ALBEDO]` its sub-rect; `createTerrainTile` gives an imagery without a slot a `LINEAR_CLAMP` one).
- **CPU.** `sampleTerrainHeight` follows the shader's rule (the grid point's float sum, then the cell's two triangles), and `getTerrainPosition` adds the curvature and the tile's frame, in doubles: for placing objects and cameras, and to check a picked point.
- **Checks.** `createTerrainTile` refuses heights that are not a square `R16_UNORM` or `R32_SFLOAT` texture of 4 to `MAX_TERRAIN_CELLS` + 3 samples; `updateMaterial` drops heights of another format (an error). A `TERRAIN` material on a mesh that is not a terrain grid (`uv[2].x` below 1) draws the mesh as it is. The heights' width must match the entity's grid; nothing checks it after `createTerrainTile`.
- **Limits.** The curvature is a parabola: d⁴ / 8R³ off the sphere, 5 cm at 100 km, so whole-globe roots are not covered (`D44`). No geomorphing between levels.

### Vector features

Lines, polygons and points are entities whose mesh the vector builders made and whose material is `MaterialType::VECTOR` (guide: `docs/guides/vector-rendering.html`; examples `25_vector_map`, `24_map_tiles`). The code is `include/rasm/vector.h`, `src/scene/vector.cpp` and `shaders/lib/vector.slang`.

```
any thread                                       renderer's thread              GPU
addVectorLines / Polygons / Points ->            createMesh(MeshBuild&&):       cull.slang: getWorldSphere pads VECTOR spheres per view;
  VectorGeometry (anchor, offset, kind)            upload only                    depth-only views skip them; overlays never occluded
buildVectorMesh -> MeshBuild (no LODs,           entity at the origin,          sort.slang: key = order << 24 | depth >> 8
  no meshlets)                                     VECTOR material              getVectorVertex (spread), shadeVector (coverage), motion
```

- **Vertices.** `pos` the anchor (offset from the entity's origin, in floats), `normal` the ground's up, `tangent.xyz` the direction the offset is measured from (`tangent.w` the feature id's bits, `std::bit_cast`), `uv[0]` the offset in half widths (x along, y across = `cross(along, up)`, falling back to `getGroundAxis(up)` on both sides), `uv[1]` the distance along a line in metres and, at a join, the turn from its frame's outer normal to the other segment's plus 8 times the outer side, or an icon's coordinates, `uv[2]` the kind (`FILL`, `LINE`, `POINT`) and the metric of the distance to the edge (`ROUND`: length; `BOX`: the larger of |x| and |y|; `ACROSS`: |y|; `FILL`: none). A segment is a quad; a join (miter up to `LineShape::miterLimit` ≤ `MAX_VECTOR_MITER` half widths, else bevel; bevel; a round fan in steps of 1/16 of a turn) fills the outer side, each miter triangle in its own segment's frame, a bevel measured round; caps are butt, square (a box quad) or round (a half fan). Polygons go through earcut (`mapbox/earcut.hpp`, vcpkg `earcut-hpp`, header-only, PRIVATE: no `find_dependency`) in the ground's plane through the origin. Points are a quad of corner offsets.
- **Draping.** With `VectorPlacement::drapeTile` and `drapeHeights`, points are placed across the tile in grid units, segments are cut where they cross x = k, z = k and x + z = k (the grid's split), polygons' triangles are cut by the same lines into convex pieces (`cutTriangle`), and every point is lifted by `getTerrainPosition` with the triangle's normal as its up: each piece lies flat on one terrain triangle. That holds for a line's middle only: its width spreads in the plane of the triangle under it. With `LineShape::drapeWidth` > 0 a draped line is a band instead (`pushDrapedLine`): `pushLine` builds it that wide on the flat grid (metres along the grid's x and z, up +y, the points not clamped to the tile), each of its triangles is clipped to the tile's square (`clipHalf`, the rest is the neighbour's: give a tile the lines within half the width of it, as `examples/24_map_tiles` does) and cut by `cutTriangle`, each corner snapped to a lattice of 2⁻²⁰ cells (so pieces that share a point lift it to the same ground and up) and lifted by `liftOnGrid`. Its vertices are kind `BAND`: `pos` on the ground, `normal` the triangle's up there, `tangent` the segment's direction in that plane, `uv[0]` the offset interpolated in the triangle, `uv[1]` the distance along and the built half width (`B35`).
- **The style.** `VectorMaterial` → `gfx::VectorData` (64 B): color, outline color, width, outline width, dash and gap, flags (`VECTOR_METRES`, `VECTOR_GROUND`, `VECTOR_OVERLAY`, `VECTOR_ICON` when the albedo slot has a texture, `VECTOR_TEXT` when it names a live font), `drawOrder` clamped to ±127, and the font atlas's bindless index and distance range. `createMaterial` and `updateMaterial` force a `VECTOR` material to `BLEND` and double-sided, so its draws are in the blend run of the Transparent pass.
- **Vertex stage** (`getVertexOutput` → `getVectorVertex`): the width in pixels at the anchor (`getPixelWorld`: 2w / (|P₁₁| · height)), at least one pixel, with `fade` below; the geometry reaches one pixel past the edge. `GROUND`: the offset in the ground's plane, widened to half a pixel and faded where it is seen edge-on. `SCREEN`: the offset across the line's direction on screen (a point: the screen's axes), a join's offsets turned from the ground's turn to the screen's (`getScreenJoinOffset`), and each corner's depth point is the nearer of the anchor and the ground under its pixel (`getGroundPoint`, through the ground plane's screen Jacobian). A width in pixels (either facing) also moves the depth point toward the camera by its half width in the world, as the ground beside a draped anchor may rise above the plane the corners take their depth from. `BAND`: the vertex is where it is drawn; it only moves out in its plane by the smooth edge's pixel (or to a pixel's width when the band is thinner), keeps the depth of its unmoved point moved toward the camera by the distance it moved, and the built width, in the material's unit, goes to the fragment stage (`VectorVertex::width`, `worldTangent.z`) in place of `VectorData::width`. Then the depth of the depth point moved toward the camera by 1/4096 of its distance (`VECTOR_DEPTH_BIAS`, along the view's +z, so any projection), or for an overlay, and for a label when the Label Depth pass runs, the near plane. Sizes use a w no smaller than the near plane's (`getSafeW`), so a vertex behind the camera stays bounded. Motion is the anchor's.
- **Fragment stage** (`fragMain` → `shadeVector`): coverage from the distance and `fwidth`, the outline band, dashes, the fade, an icon's texel; `toVectorOutput` writes the motion times alpha, which the premultiplied blend mixes with what is behind.
- **Culling.** `getWorldSphere` adds `getVectorPadding` to a `VECTOR` draw's sphere: `MAX_VECTOR_MITER` half widths and 3 pixels, each pixel the view's own size at the sphere's far side (`lodPixelsPerUnit`, `lodDepthPlane`), so the frustum test, the Hi-Z test, the blend sort and the culling debug view all use it. `countMain` leaves vector draws out of depth-only views (`SceneData::viewFlags`, `VIEW_DEPTH_ONLY`), never occlusion-culls an overlay, and never draws them by meshlets.
- **Order.** `sortKeysMain`'s key is the draw's order (`getBlendOrder`: `VECTOR_ORDER_SCENE` + `drawOrder`, other see-through draws at `VECTOR_ORDER_SCENE`) in the top 8 bits, then the top 24 bits of the complemented view depth. Without the sort (frozen culling, a failed sort buffer) the blend run is drawn in list order.
- **Labels** (guide: `docs/guides/text-and-labels.html`). A font is a `FontAtlas` (`rasm/text.h`): one byte a texel, `0.5 + distance / distanceRange`, `GlyphMetrics` sorted by codepoint, `KerningPair`s, ascent, descent, line height, a fallback glyph. `buildFontAtlas` / `AssetLoader::loadFont` (`src/assets/fontLoad.cpp`, `rasm::rasm` only; stb_truetype's `STB_TRUETYPE_IMPLEMENTATION` in `thirdparty_loaders_impl.cpp`) make one from a TrueType/OpenType file: stb's SDF per codepoint (ASCII and Latin-1 by default, 32 texels per em, range 16), packed in rows into an atlas 512 texels wide, doubled while it does not fit, at most 4096 × 4096, kerning for every pair. `Renderer::createFont` (`src/scene/text.cpp`) uploads it as an `R8_UNORM` texture in its own bindless slot (`LINEAR_CLAMP`), at most `MAX_FONTS`; `VectorMaterial::font` names it and `writeMaterialRow` puts its slot and range in `VectorData` with `VECTOR_TEXT`. `addVectorLabels` (`vector.cpp`) decodes UTF-8 (`decodeUtf8`), lays each label out (advance, kerning, centred lines, control characters other than the line feed skipped, a priority that is not finite made the lowest, `LabelAnchor` with a quarter-em gap, `LabelDesc::offset`), and emits per glyph a halo quad then, after all of the label's halo quads, a fill quad: kind `GLYPH`, `uv[0]` the corner in ems, `uv[1]` the atlas coordinate, `uv[2].y` 1 on halo quads, `tangent.x` the label's index in the mesh. Each label's box goes into `VectorGeometry::labels` → `MeshBuild::Data::labels` (`src/assets/meshBuild.h`) → `Mesh::labels` (`MeshLabel`).
- **Label placement.** An entity with labels takes a range of `MAX_LABELS` slots (`GpuScene::labelSlots`, a `RangeAllocator`) when its rows are written, freed with them; `labelStamps` counts each slot's new owners. Its instance rows carry the first slot in `InstanceData::meshletVisibility` (a vector mesh has no meshlets); an entity that finds no slots keeps -1 there, and its glyphs collapse. Placement skips an entity whose material is not `VECTOR` with a live font: its labels draw nothing, so they take no room. A draw-list rebuild lists the drawn entities with labels (`SceneDrawList::labelEntities`). For each shaded view, `prepareSceneView` calls `placeViewLabels`: each label's anchor through the entity's transform and the view-projection in doubles, its box in pixels from `VectorMaterial::width` and the halo (pixels, or metres at the anchor's depth), then `placeLabels` (`src/renderer/labelPlacement.cpp`): sort by priority, then placed last frame, then slot; a box is placed when its anchor is in view and none of its `LABEL_CELL_PIXELS` cells is taken; alpha moves toward placed by `deltaTime / LABEL_FADE_SECONDS`, except on the view's first placement (shown at once), and a slot with a new stamp starts at 0. The view keeps the state (`SceneView::labels`, `labelCandidates`, vectors that keep their capacity: no allocation in a steady frame) and writes the alphas of the slots in use into its buffer for the frame slot (`labelBuffers`, grown ×2), whose address is `SceneData::labelsPtr` (0 for a view without labels: every label shown). `placeViewLabels` also writes each slot's anchor test (`LabelCandidate::probe`: the anchor's pixel, the reversed-Z depth of the anchor moved toward the camera by `LABEL_DEPTH_PIXELS` (4) pixels' size there, and `LABEL_PROBE_DEPTH`, `LABEL_PROBE_OVERLAY`, or `LABEL_PROBE_NONE` for an anchor off the screen or a slot of another scene in the range, plus `LABEL_PROBE_RESET` for a label that was hidden, and for all of them in the frame the fade buffer grows) into `labelProbes` (`SceneData::labelProbesPtr`), with `labelFadesPtr`, `labelFadeStep` and `labelSlotCount`; a view whose labels are all overlays or off the screen sets none, and the pass does nothing. The `Label Depth` compute pass (`recordLabelDepth`, `shaders/passes/labelDepth.slang`), after the view's last draw pass and before `Transparent`, runs a thread per slot from the view's first (the push constant's `argument`) to its last, and reads the depth (`readDepth`; with MSAA each pixel's farthest sample, like the Hi-Z) at the 3 × 3 pixels around each anchor: the label is in view when the anchor's depth is in front of one of them. Its fade (`SceneView::labelFades`, one buffer per view kept across frames, declared `readPrevious` and `write` like the visibility) steps toward the answer, or starts at it after `RESET`, and multiplies the slot's alpha in place. The label buffers, probes and fades are made with the view and grow under the same handles (`resizeBuffer`), since the graph's passes name them (`B36`). The glyph vertex reads its alpha and collapses at 0, collapses a halo quad without a halo, spreads by the text's size across the screen like a point, takes the near plane's depth when the pass ran (else the nearer of the anchor and the ground under it, `getGroundPoint`), and drops TAA's jitter (`removeJitter`); `shadeGlyph` gives the coverage from the distance and `fwidth(uv)`, the halo up to half the screen range less a pixel. `getVectorPadding` pads a `VECTOR_TEXT` draw by `VECTOR_LABEL_REACH` (= `MAX_LABEL_REACH`) ems and the halo.
- **Meshes off the main thread** (`P30`). `buildMesh(vertices, indices, colors, MeshBuildOptions)` checks the data and builds the LODs and meshlets it asks for, on any thread, into a `MeshBuild` (an `ObjRaw` behind a pointer); `Renderer::createMesh(MeshBuild&&)` only writes it into the geometry pool. `createMesh(vertices, indices, colors)` is the two in a row, with LODs as `RendererConfig::meshLods` says. `buildVectorMesh` builds with neither.

### Point clouds

A point cloud is drawn in chunks: each chunk a mesh of up to `MAX_POINT_CHUNK_POINTS` (65 536) points, drawn by an entity with a `MaterialType::POINTS` material (guide: `docs/guides/point-clouds.html`; example `28_point_cloud`). The code is `include/rasm/pointCloud.h`, `include/rasm/colormap.h`, `src/scene/pointCloud.cpp`, `src/scene/colormap.cpp`, `shaders/lib/points.slang` and `shaders/passes/points.slang`.

```
any thread                                  renderer's thread                    GPU, each view
buildPointCloudChunk(PointCloudChunkDesc):  createMesh(MeshBuild&&):             cull.slang, point run (SceneData::pointRun):
  16-bit steps of the box, a 16-bit           GeometryPool::allocate/write:        selectPointCount -> a prefix k, stored as
  scalar, RGBA8 colors; Morton sort read      point pool range + geometry row,     k - 1 above CULL_LOD_SHIFT; draw = 3k indices
  in bit-reversed steps; pointOrder           the shared index range 0, 1, 2 ...   points.slang: a triangle a point, cut, SV_DepthLessEqual
                                                                                   or isRasterChunk -> appendPointBatches (CULL_RASTER):
                                                                                   pointRaster.slang: clear, raster (64-bit atomicMax),
                                                                                   resolve quad in the last draw pass
```

- **Chunk data.** `buildPointCloudChunk` checks the points (1 to `MAX_POINT_CHUNK_POINTS`, finite) and writes `ObjRaw::points`: a `gfx::PointChunkHeader` (48 B: `boxMin`, `pointCount`, `boxStep`, `flags` `POINT_CHUNK_COLORS` / `POINT_CHUNK_SCALARS`, `scalarMin`, `scalarStep`, `colorOffset`, `spacing`), then two words per point (x | y << 16, z | scalar << 16, 16-bit steps of the box and of the scalars' range), then a word per point of RGBA8 colors when given: 8 B a point, 12 B with colors. The order: the points sorted by the Morton code of their 16-bit steps, then taken at the bit-reversed positions of the next power of two, so every prefix is an even sample (`tests/pointCloudTests.cpp` checks one point per cell for prefixes of 4^k). `MeshBuild::Data::pointOrder` keeps each drawn point's index in the desc (2 B a point), which `createMesh` moves to `Mesh::pointOrder` for picks.
- **Storage.** `GeometryPool` keeps a point stream (`RendererConfig::geometryPoolPoints`, 8 B units, the buffer made with the first chunk) beside the others. A chunk takes `pointUnits` from `firstPoint` (`MeshGeometry`, `pointCount` > 0 marks a chunk; `vertexCount` stays 0) and a geometry row: `vertexOffset` its first unit, the sphere around its box, one LOD of `gfx::POINT_INDICES` (3) × `pointCount` indices from the shared range 0, 1, 2, … (`POINT_INDICES` × `MAX_POINT_CHUNK_POINTS` of the index pool, taken and written with the first chunk, never freed). `free` gives back the point range and the row only.
- **The run.** `updateDrawModes` marks an instance row `POINT_RUN_POINTS` in `GpuScene::instancePoints` when its sub-mesh is a chunk and its material is `POINTS` (a chunk with another material is not drawn; a `POINTS` material on triangles shades as `PBR`), and a glyph chunk with a `GLYPHS` material `POINT_RUN_GLYPHS` (below, instanced glyphs); `rebuildDrawList` sorts those rows into one run after the `MASK` runs and before the blend run, with cull mode `NONE`. `prepareSceneView` makes `ShadedPipelines::pointPipeline` (`points.slang`, alpha-to-coverage with more than one sample, the motion target with TAA) once the list has a point run, writes `SceneData::pointRun` and `pointsPtr`; `drawSceneView` binds it for the point run (also in a debug view) and skips the run in a depth-only view, whose cull leaves the chunks out: points cast no shadow.
- **Culling and LOD.** `getWorldSphere` pads a `POINTS` draw's sphere by `getPointPadding` (half the size and a pixel at the sphere's far side). For the point run, `countMain` calls `selectPointCount` instead of `selectLod`, the one place that decides what a chunk draws: k = min(N, ⌈`density` × D² / a⌉), D the sphere's diameter in pixels at its nearest point (`lodPixelsPerUnit`, `lodDepthPlane`), a the point's area (π/4 of the square for `ROUND` and `SPHERE`), all with `lodErrorPixels` 0 or `density` 0, N >> k with a forced LOD k. It stores k − 1 above `CULL_LOD_SHIFT`, counts k triangles, and `writeMain` writes the draw with `getPointDraw` (3k indices). The point run is never drawn by meshlets.
- **The shader.** `vertMain` reads the draw, the instance, the geometry row's `vertexOffset`, the material's `PointData`, the header and the point (`loadPointChunk`), places the point with the instance's render-space matrix, sizes it (pixels, or metres through the pixel's world size at its w, at least one pixel; round shapes under 3 pixels are drawn square), and spreads corner c of 3p + c to (−1, −1), (3, −1), (−1, 3) of its square in clip space; a `SPHERE` point moves its triangle to the sphere's front. Its color is `PointData::color` times the point's color or, for `POINT_SCALAR`, the colormap at the scalar through the range (`POINT_LOG`, `POINT_CLAMP`; out of range without clamp collapses the point). `fragMain` cuts the triangle to the square or the disc (with MSAA as alpha-to-coverage from the distance and `fwidth`), writes `SV_DepthLessEqual` (the sphere's depth for `SPHERE`, else the triangle's) and the centre's motion.
- **The compute path** (`src/renderer/pointRaster.h/.cpp`, `shaders/passes/pointRaster.slang`; Schütz, Kerbl and Wimmer 2021). Where the device has `shaderBufferInt64Atomics` (`VulkanDevice` enables it when present, `RenderContext::hasBufferInt64Atomics`), `createSceneView` gives each shaded view a `Point Raster Buffer` per frame slot (`SceneView::pointRaster`, the header alone at first, grown under the same handle; `writeCulling` writes it and the draw passes read it, so the graph gains no edge). Its layout (`getPointRasterLayout`): a `gfx::PointRasterHeader` (48 B: the raster and clear dispatches' group counts and the rect of pixels the chunks reach), a `uint2` a batch (instance row, first point | count << 16; `getPointBatchCapacity`: 64 a chunk of the point run, at most `POINT_MAX_BATCHES` 65 535), then a 64-bit word a pixel. Each frame `prepareSceneView` calls `preparePointRaster`, which sets `SceneView::rasterPoints` and `SceneData::pointRasterPtr`, `pointBatchesPtr`, `pointPixelsPtr`, `pointBatchCapacity` for a shaded, culled view with a point run, no debug view and `RendererConfig::computePoints` (`Renderer::setComputePoints`); else they stay 0 and every point goes by triangles, as in a pick (`recordPicks` zeroes them) and a depth-only view.
  - **Routing.** `countMain`, after `selectPointCount`, sends a counted chunk with an instance row under `POINT_RASTER_MAX_ROWS` (65 536) to the compute path when `isRasterChunk` holds (its points' diameter in the view's own projection: in a perspective view at most `POINT_RASTER_MAX_SIZE`, 6 pixels, at its sphere's centre and `POINT_RASTER_MAX_NEAR`, 8, at its nearest point; in an orthographic view `POINT_RASTER_MAX_ORTHO`, 4; in a view with MSAA (`SceneData::depthSamples` > 1), where the triangles' edges cover samples by distance, `POINT_PIXELS_MAX_SIZE`, 2, at its nearest point; its box's corners all in front of the camera; at least `POINT_RASTER_MIN_DENSITY`, 0.125, drawn points per pixel of its rect, a point of s pixels at the centre counting as max(s² / 4, 1); the rect reaches max(ceil(s), 2) pixels of the nearest size past the box's corners) and `appendPointBatches` finds room (an `InterlockedCompareExchange` loop on the header's group count that adds only batches that fit, so every batch below the count is written; a chunk that does not fit goes to the triangles). A point the triangles would collapse (clear style color, scalar out of range, clear colormap entry) writes no pixel, so the point behind it shows on both paths. The chunk takes no slot (`cullSlotsPtr` = `CULL_RASTER`, which `writeMain` skips and the late phase reads as drawn early); its points count in the stats as drawn.
  - **Passes.** `recordSceneCulling` runs `recordPointReset` before the early cull (a one-thread dispatch: no batch, an empty rect) and `recordPointRaster` after the view's last cull (the late one, or the early one without a late half): `clearMain` zeroes the rect's words (as many groups as the largest chunk's rect needs, looping over the rect) and `rasterMain`, a group a batch, places each point as `points.slang`'s `vertMain` does and writes the pixels whose centre its shape holds (`rasterPoint`: `getPointShape`'s square, or from 3 pixels a disc tested per pixel; a SPHERE point's centre is its front's and its depth per pixel `getSphereDepth`; the centre snapped to 1/256 of a pixel, at most ceil(s) + 1 columns and ceil(s) rows) with `InterlockedMax` of `asuint(depth) << 32 | row << 16 | point`, both indirect from the header, so a frame without a batch runs no group. The last draw pass (`Scene 2`, or `Scene 1` without a late half) calls `drawPointResolve` before `drawSky`: 6 vertices (`ShadedPipelines::pointResolvePipeline`, depth test and write) whose vertex stage spans the rect, or collapses it when it is empty; `resolveFrag` reads the pixel's word, finds the point, writes its color (the colormap read once a pixel), the centre's motion with TAA and `SV_Depth`, so meshes in front still hide it and `Label Depth`, the grid, `Transparent` and TAA see it. One fragment a pixel writes all its MSAA samples. A pixel that ends empty discards and returns at once: a demoted lane runs on, and would read the point of instance row 0 (id 0), which may be a mesh, and follow its words out of the point buffer (`B39`); a helper lane outside the rect reads no word. An empty pixel takes the nearest of its 8 neighbours' points (`getHoleFill`) when at least `POINT_FILL_MIN_NEIGHBOURS` (6) hold one, their depths are within `POINT_FILL_DEPTH_TOLERANCE` (5 %) and that point's chunk was thinned by the LOD: the batch carries `POINT_BATCH_THINNED` and `rasterMain` stores it in the depth's lowest bit (the resolve writes the depth without it). That closes the LOD's single-pixel holes without growing a silhouette or filling the data's own gaps: in `28_point_cloud` the top view's holes go from 0.044 % to 0 % (main 0.001 %), the perspective view's from 0.027 % to 0.025 %, for 0.025 ms. The triangle path has no fill. An indirect draw of a count in the header drew nothing on lavapipe, so the draw is direct.
  - **Same pixels.** Both paths cut a point to the same shape (`getPointShape` in `shaders/lib/points.slang`: `POINT_SHAPE_PIXELS` up to 2 pixels, a square under 3, then the style's square, disc or sphere) and take a pixel when its centre is in it (edges included) around the same snapped centre and depth (a SPHERE point's per pixel, `getSphereDepth`), and a tie of depth goes to the higher (row, point), as the later triangle with `GREATER_OR_EQUAL`; so a chunk can switch paths between frames. Under MSAA a point of 2 pixels or less covers whole pixels on both paths (`points.slang`'s shape 3); in a view with the compute path its triangle reaches half a pixel past the square, so no sample of a covered pixel lies outside it. A view drawn by triangles only keeps the triangle (that half pixel costs a fifth more there) and differs in 0.7% of the pixels of `points_msaa4`. The image tests `points_triangles`, `points_msaa4_triangles`, `points_taa_triangles` draw the scene by triangles only against the compute path's image, and `points_large_triangles` does for discs of 5 pixels, spheres of 4 and squares of 3 (`points_large`). A centre exactly on the shape's border goes as the rasterizer's top-left rule sends it: kept on the left, right and top sides, left out on the bottom, the triangle's own bottom side (`rasterPoint` takes up to ceil(s) + 1 columns and ceil(s) rows). A SPHERE pixel whose surface lies past a finite far plane (depth ≤ 0) writes nothing. With the hole fill off, `28_point_cloud`'s compute frames differ from the triangles' in 0.012 % of a single-sample view's pixels for squares of 2 pixels, 0.016 % for squares of 3, 2 pixels for discs of 4 and spheres of 5.
- **Colormaps.** `Renderer::createColormap` resamples 1 to `COLORMAP_SIZE` linear colors (or a `ColormapPreset`: matplotlib's tables for viridis, turbo, magma, inferno, plasma, cividis, and gray) to a 256 × 1 `R8G8B8A8_SRGB` texture in a `LINEAR_CLAMP` bindless slot; the shader reads t at (t · 255 + 0.5) / 256. `detachTexture` clears a destroyed colormap from the materials.
- **Cost** (RTX 3060, Release, `28_point_cloud --bench`, 1920 × 1080 in a perspective TAA view and an orthographic 4× MSAA view): by triangles 1.07 ns per drawn point in PR 2 (1.14 ns now, 2 pixels), nearly the same for 1 and 2 pixel points; one triangle a point is 12–16% faster than a quad of two. By compute 0.081 ns per point of 1 pixel and 0.28 ns per point of 2 pixels (four atomics), plus the clear and the resolve of the rect, about 0.3 ms for the two views' 2 M pixels: every point of 20 M (33.5 M drawn) in 3.31 ms against 30.5 ms by triangles; 4 M points at the default density (3.78 M drawn) in 1.09 ms against 4.02 ms. By size, every point (`--all-points`, 10 M against 20 M), one 1920 × 1080 perspective view of one sample, round points (triangles / compute, ns per point): 1 px 1.03 / 0.088, 2 px 1.07 / 0.26, 3 px 1.39 / 0.45, 4 px 1.99 / 0.79, 6 px 3.58 / 1.73, 8 px 5.53 / 3.02; squares 3 px 1.44 / 0.58, 8 px 5.84 / 3.88; spheres as discs. At the LOD's density (`--size N --points 20000000`, both views) a frame costs the same on both paths at about 7 pixels in the perspective view (4 px 2.25 against 3.06 ms on main, 6 px 2.41 against 2.64, 8 px: compute 2.69 against 2.45 by triangles) and 5 in the orthographic one (3 px 3.31 against 3.66, 4 px 3.09 against 3.20, 6 px: compute 2.82 against 2.78): the compute path writes every pixel of every point, while the rasterizer's early depth test drops hidden pixels cheaply. Under 4× MSAA the compute path stays at 2 pixels, as it would turn the triangles' sample-covered edges into whole pixels.
- **Adaptive size** (`PointSizeMode::ADAPTIVE`, `gfx::POINT_ADAPTIVE`, the largest size in `PointData::flags`' high 16 bits, `POINT_MAX_SIZE_SHIFT`). `getPointDiameter` (`shaders/lib/points.slang`) is the one place a point is sized, for `vertMain`, `rasterMain` and `isRasterChunk`: a fixed size, or one grown from where the chunk's spacing on screen times √(density / a) passes the size, √(`POINT_ADAPTIVE_COVER` (4) / density) times faster, up to `maxSize`, so far chunks keep their size and the compute path, and near ones cover their ground about 4 times. The spacing is `PointChunkHeader::spacing` (word 11): `PointCloudChunkDesc::spacing`, or `measurePointSpacing` (`src/scene/pointCloud.cpp`), the cell size at which the points take half as many cells as there are points, counted per octree level along a 63-bit Morton order. `getPointPadding` pads an adaptive chunk's sphere by `maxSize`. A near chunk whose points grow past the compute path's sizes goes to the triangles: `28_point_cloud --adaptive` takes 2.01 ms of GPU frame against 1.62 with fixed points at 1920 × 1080 (3.60 before `P33`); 28 keeps `FIXED` by default, as its scan is dense.
- **A chunk's LOD word** (`PointChunkLod`, `Renderer::setPointChunkLod` in `src/scene/entity.cpp`). `EntityDrawState::pointLod`, -1 until set, goes into a point chunk's `InstanceData::meshletVisibility` (a chunk has no meshlets): bits 24 to 30 the drawn share (of 127), bits 3o to 3o + 2 the finer share (of 7) of octant o = (x ≥ 0) | (y ≥ 0) << 1 | (z ≥ 0) << 2 of the entity's space (`encodePointChunkLod`; read by `getPointDrawnShare`, `getPointFinerShare`). The cull keeps the drawn share of `selectPointPrefix`'s count (`selectPointCount`; 0 draws nothing), and `getPointDiameter` lerps an adaptive point back to the size by the finer share at its position. A changed word rewrites the entity's rows (`writeInstanceRows(entity, false)`).
- **The point raster buffer grows geometrically.** `preparePointRaster` raises `SceneView::pointBatchCapacity` to twice what it held (at most `POINT_MAX_BATCHES`) when the run needs more batches, so a streamed cloud whose chunk count creeps up does not make the buffer (with its 8 B a pixel) again at each new count; before this, each new count allocated it again and freeing the old one stalled frames for 40 to 90 ms.
- **Octrees** (`include/rasm/pointCloudOctree.h`, `src/scene/pointCloudOctree.cpp`; guide `docs/guides/point-octree.html`; example `29_point_streaming`). `buildPointCloudOctree` (any thread) sorts the points by a 63-bit Morton code of their cube (`spreadMortonBits`, `MORTON_LEVELS` 21, `src/scene/pointCloudInternal.h`) and builds each node from its run of that order (`buildNode`): a node with at most `maxNodePoints` (16 384) points left is a leaf with them all (up to `MAX_POINT_CHUNK_POINTS`, the rest dropped with a warning); else it keeps the point nearest the centre of each cell of a `cells`³ grid (128, coarser while the cells pass `maxNodePoints`), compacts the others in order and builds a child per octant run. A node's points are float offsets from its cube's centre (`PointCloudNode::center`), its spacing `measurePointSpacing` of them, capped at its parent's (a few edge points are no surface), its `geometricError` the spacing or 0 for a leaf, its bounds the sphere around its and its children's points grown to hold each child's. `PointCloudOctree::indices` maps a point back to the desc. `getPointCloudNodeChunk` is a node's chunk desc. The octree stays in CPU memory (24 B a point, 28 with colors).
- **LAS and LAZ** (`AssetLoader::loadPointCloud`, `Engine::loadPointCloud`, `src/assets/pointCloudLoad.cpp`, in `rasm::rasm`). RASM's own reader parses the public header (LAS 1.0 to 1.4: scale, offset, bounds, format, the 64-bit count of 1.4), the variable length records (WKT, `LASF_Projection` 2112, and the LASzip record 22204) and the extended ones of 1.4, then the point records of formats 0 to 10 in blocks of 65 536 (`getPointLayout`: sizes, GPS time and RGB offsets; formats 6 to 10 a full classification byte and 4-bit return numbers).
