A `D32_SFLOAT` depth texture is always created `SAMPLED_DEPTH_ATTACHMENT` and enters the bindless array, so a pass can sample it (`PassBuilder::readDepth`, `Renderer::getRenderTargetDepthTexture`), as the `Hi-Z` pass, the shadowed scene passes and `TAA` do; between passes it stays an attachment unless a pass reads it. Other depth formats are not sampled, since a depth/stencil view cannot be.
- **Inspect pass.** `RenderGraph::inspectPass` is the index of the pass `Renderer::updateInspectPass` adds for the RASM window's Buffers section (§4); `compile` moves it right after the last pass it depends on, after the sort and before the transient lifetimes. `removePass` takes a pass out and keeps `inspectPass` pointing at the same pass.
- **Debugging.** `RenderGraph::dump()` returns the graph as text: the passes in the order they run (or in the order they were added, when the graph did not compile), each with its queue, reads, writes, attachment ops and the passes that wait for it, then a lifetime table with one column per pass: `W` write, `R` read, `P` read of the frame before, `-` alive between the first and last use, `.` not used. A pass shows `async compute` only when `Renderer::asyncCompute` is on; otherwise an async pass runs on the graphics queue and shows `compute`. A per-frame buffer is one row named after its first slot, the swapchain's depth has its own row (a swapchain pass writes both), and transient targets are marked. The dump and the ordering walk the same access list (`accessesOf`). Names come from the buffer, texture and render target descs (`RenderContext::getRenderTargetDesc`); an unnamed resource, or any resource in a graph without an engine as in the tests, shows `#index:generation`. F3 (`Renderer::pollEvents`), or the RASM window's Dump button, sets `Renderer::graphDumpRequested`; the next `RenderGraph::execute` that reaches the end of its frame clears it, keeps its dump in `Renderer::graphDump` (which the RASM window shows) and logs it with `spdlog::info`, whether `Renderer::render` or the application runs the graph. Each pass also has a GPU timer (§3).
- **Texture state.** `RenderContext` keeps each texture's current usage in `rhi::Texture::state`, so `transitionImageLayout(cmd, texture, newUsage)` takes only the new one. A texture starts as `UNKNOWN`, `fillTexture` leaves it `SAMPLED`, and `acquireNextImage` sets the acquired swapchain image back to `UNKNOWN`. A transition from `SAMPLED` to `SAMPLED` records nothing, since reads after reads need no barrier; any other one records a barrier, even to the same attachment layout, because attachments are written, and `STORAGE` to `STORAGE` too, since storage images are written. `discard` makes the barrier start from `UNKNOWN`, which drops the contents and still waits for every earlier use. The rules live in `rhi::TextureStates` (`src/rhi/textureStates.h`, tested in `tests/textureStatesTests.cpp`). The state changes when the barrier is recorded, and `TextureStates` remembers every change not yet submitted, with its command buffer and the state from before it. A successful `submit` forgets the changes of its command buffers only, since a frame with async compute is several submits; `discardRecording` puts the rest back, last first, for commands that will not run. `submitBatches` calls it when a submit failed, so the next frame starts from the layouts the GPU really has, those of the batches that went out included. This holds because a texture's changes on two queues are ordered by waits, so they go out in the order they were recorded. Each history clear also remembers its command buffer and is forgotten only when that one goes out. Like `memoryBarrier`, it refuses to record inside a rendering scope.
- `transitionImageLayout` takes the barrier's source scope from the old usage and its destination scope from the new one. `SAMPLED` covers the fragment and compute shaders, and the attachment usages include reads, so a transition to the same attachment layout orders earlier attachment writes before a `beginRendering` with `load`. `STORAGE` is the `GENERAL` layout with compute shader storage reads and writes; only a `SAMPLED_STORAGE` texture may take it (`transitionImageLayout` checks). `UNKNOWN` discards the contents and waits for every stage the image could have been used in, attachment and storage writes included, and `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT`, which chains the barrier to the acquire semaphore of a swapchain image. A usage without a defined scope logs an error, records no barrier and returns `false`.

## 10. Compile-time limits

| Constant | Value | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `MAX_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT` | 2 | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_COLOR_ATTACHMENTS` | 4 | `rasm/types.h` |
| `RendererConfig::maxBindlessTextures` (bindless texture slots) / `MAX_BINDLESS_TEXTURES` (the most it may be, and the "any slot" value) | 16384, lowered to the device's limit / 65536 | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_BINDLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` / `RESERVED_STORAGE_IMAGES` | 1024 / 32 (the environment bake's; a bake uses up to 19) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `BLOOM_LEVELS` / `BLOOM_MIN_LEVEL_SIZE` | 6 (1/2 to 1/64) / 2 | `src/renderer/bloom.h` |
| Environment sizes: cube / `SPECULAR_CUBE_SIZE` × `SPECULAR_MIPS` / `IRRADIANCE_CUBE_SIZE` / `BRDF_LUT_SIZE` | 64 to 1024 / 256 × 6 / 32 / 128 | `src/renderer/environmentBake.h` |
| `MAX_ENTITIES` / `MAX_ENTITIES_PER_SCENE` | 262144 (2¹⁸) | `rasm/entity.h`, `rasm/scene.h` |
| `EngineConfig::maxMaterials` (materials alive at once) | 4096 by default (2 × 0.9 MiB) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_CAMERAS` / `MAX_CAMERAS_PER_SCENE` | 32 / 32 | `rasm/renderer.h`, `rasm/scene.h` |
| `MAX_TONE_MAP_FORMATS` (output formats with tone-map pipelines) | 4 | `rasm/renderer.h` |
| `EngineConfig::maxLights` (lights; a scene holds any of them) | 16384 by default | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_CLUSTER_LIGHTS` (lights per cluster of a view) / clusters | 256 / 16 × 9 × 24 | `rasm/light.h`, `src/renderer/lightClusters.h` |
| `MAX_SCENES` | 16 | `rasm/scene.h` |
| `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` | 32 | `rasm/scene.h` |
| `MAX_FONTS` / `MAX_LABELS` (label slots of every entity alive) / `MAX_LABEL_REACH` (ems, culling's pad) | 64 / 65536 / 12 | `rasm/text.h` |
| `MAX_WINDOWS` (windows at once, the main one included; 7 fit) / `MAIN_WINDOW_INDEX` | 8 / 1 | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_SHADOW_CASCADES` / `MAX_VIEW_SHADOWS` | 4 / 4 (shadowed lights per view) | `rasm/scene.h` |
| `MAX_GPU_TIMERS` | 64 (the frame and the passes) | `rasm/renderer.h` |
| `MAX_VERTEX_UV_SETS` | 3 | `rasm/mesh.h` (mirrored in `core.slang`) |
| `MAX_MORPH_TARGETS` (per glTF primitive) | 256 | `rasm/mesh.h` (mirrored in `geometryTypes.slang`) |
| `MAX_TERRAIN_CELLS` (cells per side of a terrain grid) | 256 | `rasm/terrain.h` |
| `MAX_VECTOR_MITER` (a miter join's reach, in half widths) | 2 | `rasm/vector.h` |
| `EngineConfig::geometryPoolVertices` / `geometryPoolIndices` / `geometryPoolColors` (defaults) | 2²¹ (128 MiB) / 2²⁴ (64 MiB) / 2²⁰ (4 MiB) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `EngineConfig::geometryPoolSkins` / `geometryPoolMorphs` (defaults) | 2²⁰ (16 MiB) / 2²⁰ (24 MiB) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `MAX_DRAW_RUNS` | 10 (3 cull modes × `NONE`, `MASK`, the point run and the weighted, additive and sorted `BLEND` runs) | `src/renderer/uberMaterialTypes.h` (mirrored in `core.slang`) |
| `MAX_GLYPH_CHUNK_GLYPHS` / `GLYPH_BATCH_GLYPHS` / `GLYPH_MAX_BATCHES` / `GLYPH_MAX_CHUNKS` (a view's glyph cull) | 65536 / 256 / 65535 / 65536 | `rasm/glyphs.h`, `src/renderer/uberMaterialTypes.h` (mirrored in `glyphs.slang`) |
| `EngineConfig::geometryPoolSubMeshes` / `maxInstances` (defaults) | 2¹⁴ (3.1 MiB) / 2¹⁶ (2 × 10.3 MiB) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `EngineConfig::geometryPoolMeshlets` / `maxViewMeshlets` (defaults) | 2¹⁷ (5 MiB) / 2¹⁶ meshlet draws per view and frame slot (1.3 MiB) | `rasm/types.h` |
| `EngineConfig::maxAnimatedEntities` / `animationPoolBytes` / `maxMorphWeights` (defaults) | 2¹⁴ (2 × 1.9 MiB), at most 65535 / 16 MiB / 2¹⁶ (4 × 256 KiB) | `rasm/types.h` |

## 11. Conventions that cross files

- **Paths.** Nothing depends on the working directory unless the application asks for it.
  - `EngineConfig::shaderDir` is the folder holding `lib/` and `passes/`. When empty, it takes the `RASM_SHADER_DIR` default, which is `shaders`.
  - `AssetLoaderConfig::assetRoot` (`EngineConfig::assetRoot` for an `Engine`) is the folder that relative paths passed to `loadMesh`, `loadGLTF`, `loadTexture` and `loadEnvironment` resolve against. Absolute paths ignore it. When empty, paths are opened as given, which means relative to the working directory.
  - A relative `shaderDir`, `assetRoot` or `cacheDir` resolves against the folder of the binary that holds RASM (`getBaseDir`, `src/platform/paths.cpp`): the executable's directory (`getModulePath`, its links resolved) for a program linked to the static libraries, or the shared library's own for the C API, which calls `setBaseModule` with an address in its code before anything resolves, so its installed `shaders/` beside it are found under any host process (`python.exe`). `getConfig()` returns the absolute result. `resolveAssetPath` gives the path a load would open.
  - Paths are UTF-8. `src/platform/paths.h` converts them, because `std::filesystem::path` otherwise assumes the Windows ANSI code page. A string that isn't valid UTF-8 is read in that code page instead, which is what `path::string()` returns. `resolveAssetPath` always returns UTF-8, and stb_image (through `STBI_WINDOWS_UTF8`), tinygltf and the `std::ifstream` that `loadMesh` gives tinyobjloader all open it as such.
- **Coordinate system.** The world is right-handed and Y-up, like glTF (`D8`). Positions are doubles on the CPU, and the GPU works in each view's render space (§4). Cameras look down −Z, and clip space is Y-up on every backend. The Vulkan backend flips Y with a negative-height viewport inside `setViewport`, so callers pass a normal rectangle. Front faces are counter-clockwise. Single-sided materials are back-face culled, double-sided ones are not (glTF `doubleSided`, or the `doubleSided` argument of `createMaterial`, which defaults to `false`), and their back faces are lit with the reversed normal (`PBRInfo::backFace`). Mouse positions reach the camera controllers in Y-up NDC.
- **Depth.** Reversed-Z. The swapchain has no depth: `beginPass` on the window attaches only color, and the two-argument `Renderer::createPipeline` makes a pipeline without depth (depth test and write off, `createGraphicsPipeline` in `vulkanDevice.cpp`). Anything with a depth test draws into a render target, which always has one. A graph pass on the swapchain loads only color after an earlier one (`assignAttachmentOps`), and `dump` shows it as `no depth`. Render target depths and the scene pipeline use `D32_SFLOAT` without stencil, cleared to 0.0, compare `GREATER_OR_EQUAL`. Depth is stored only for a later pass that reads it (the `Hi-Z` pass of occlusion culling, a shadowed scene view) or loads it (`AttachmentOps::storeDepth`). The clear value (`VulkanCommandBuffer::beginRendering`, which clears each attachment unless its `AttachmentOps` load flag is set) and the compare op (`createGraphicsPipeline`) are fixed for every pass and pipeline, so a custom pass that writes its own depth must use the same convention: a skybox at the far plane writes depth 0 (`z = 0`), not `z = w`. A render target may ask for a stencil format (`D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT`, `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`); the Vulkan backend then adds the stencil aspect and attachment. The image aspect comes from the format alone. A pipeline must declare the same depth format, which `Renderer::createPipeline(name, source, renderTarget)` takes from the target. `RenderContext::bindPipeline` checks a pipeline's color and depth formats against the current pass's attachments; on a mismatch it logs an error, does not bind, and skips the pass's draws until a matching pipeline is bound.
- **Output.** Shaders output linear color. The scene passes write unbounded radiance into a `SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT` (`R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT`) target. `Renderer::addToneMapPass(graph, source, camera, bloom)` adds a `Tone Map` pass that reads color 0 of `source`, mixes in the bloom target when one is given and the bloom ran (`lerp(color, bloom, Camera::bloomStrength)`, bilinear from half size), multiplies it by the camera's exposure (`Camera::exposure`, a linear factor, 1 by default; `setCameraExposure` takes only a positive value) and maps it to [0, 1] with AgX (`shaders/passes/toneMap.slang`: Blender's inset matrix, a log2 encoding over 16.5 stops (2^-12.47 to 2^4.03, 10 stops below middle gray 0.18 and 6.5 above), the polynomial fit of its curve, the outset matrix, then a 2.2 power to undo the curve's display encoding), into its `ViewOutput`: by default the whole swapchain; `rect` (a `ViewRect`, fractions of the image) sets the viewport and the scissor to `getViewPixels` of the swapchain's or the target's size when the pass runs, and `target` writes a color target with one color, no depth and one sample instead (a view texture; anything else, or `source` or `bloom` as the target, is refused). Passes on one output run in add order, so the first clears it and a later one draws its rect over the earlier ones. The exposure is read when the pass runs, so changing it needs no new graph. The sRGB swapchain format does the final encoding. Every color of the scene goes through the curve, unlit materials and the clear color too (`BACKGROUND_COLOR` in `src/renderer/viewGraph.h`, 0.018 linear, so exposure changes the background); a frame with no pass on the swapchain shows that color unmapped (`endFrame`'s `Clear` pass). The pipeline is made the first time the pass runs for an output format (`getToneMapPipeline`: an `Renderer::ToneMapPipelines` per format, at most `MAX_TONE_MAP_FORMATS` (4), each with its tone map and its two debug pipelines); if the shader fails, the pass logs an error, draws nothing and tries again the next frame, like the scene pipeline. Its push constants are `gfx::ToneMapPushConstants` in `uberMaterialTypes.h`, which `validateRasmStructs` checks against `ToneMapPushConstants` in the shader by name.
- **MSAA.** `RenderTargetDesc::samples` (1, 2, 4 or 8) gives a target's color and depth that many samples per pixel (`TextureDesc::samples`, the Vulkan image's `samples`). `checkRenderTargetDesc` takes only a power of two the device can draw into and sample for color and depth (`Device::supportedSampleCounts`: the four `framebuffer*`/`sampledImage*SampleCounts` limits; `RenderContext::supportsSampleCount`), and only for a target with colors and no history, storage or cube; `createTexture` takes samples only for a color or depth attachment of one mip. Pipelines carry `PipelineDesc::samples` (hashed, `createPipeline(name, source, renderTarget)` and `createScenePipeline` take the target's), and `RenderContext::createPipeline` refuses a graphics pipeline with another count; `beginRendering` refuses attachments of different counts, and `bindPipeline` skips the draws of a pipeline whose count is not the pass's (`passSamples`). Counts above 8 are refused too (`isSampleCount`), since the scene passes keep one pipeline set per count up to 8 (`SAMPLE_VARIANTS`). `addScenePasses` draws into such a target as into any (§4: pipelines per count, alpha-to-coverage for `MASK`, Hi-Z from every depth sample; the light clusters read no depth). `Renderer::addMsaaResolvePass(graph, source, camera)` (`src/renderer/msaaResolve.cpp`, `shaders/passes/msaaResolve.slang`) adds the `MSAA Resolve` compute pass, which reads each pixel's samples of color 0 through `Sampler2DMS<float4>` on the bindless binding and writes `<source>_resolved`, a transient storage target (§9) of the source's size: Σ c·w / Σ w with w = 1 / (1 + luminance(c) · exposure), the camera's exposure read when the pass runs. A plain mean (a resolve attachment) lets a bright sample win: an edge pixel of sky 8.0 and roof 0.05 shows byte 239 after AgX, the weighted mean 197, against 159 for the mean on screen. `addBloomPasses` and `addToneMapPass` refuse a multisampled source. `EngineConfig::antiAliasing` (`NONE`, `MSAA_2X`, `MSAA_4X`, `MSAA_8X`) makes `ViewGraph::render` create `scene_hdr` with that count (or the most the device has below it, with a warning, `getSceneSamples`) and add the resolve before the bloom and the tone map. At 1920x1080 color RGBA16F and depth D32 take 47.5, 94.9 and 189.8 MiB at 2, 4 and 8 samples, plus 15.8 MiB for the resolved target; `examples/09_gltf_materials --render-size 1920 1080` (RTX 3060, Release, median of 5 runs) measures `MSAA Resolve` 0.12, 0.14 and 0.38 ms and `Hi-Z` 0.06, 0.11, 0.16 and 0.41 ms at 1, 2, 4 and 8 samples. Its push constants are `gfx::ResolvePushConstants`, checked like the tone map's.
- **TAA.** `Renderer::addTaaPass(graph, source, camera)` (`src/renderer/taa.cpp`, `shaders/passes/taa.slang`) takes a single-sample target with colors `{SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT, MOTION_FORMAT}` (`R16G16_SFLOAT`) and `D32_SFLOAT` depth that `addScenePasses` drew in the same graph (it refuses a multisampled one: TAA and MSAA exclude each other), sets `SceneView::jitter` on that view, and adds the `TAA` compute pass, which writes `<source>_taa`, a storage target with history (two copies, owned by the graph). Its declarations: `read(source)` (both colors to `SAMPLED`), `readDepth(source)`, `readPrevious` and `write` of the history; the graph moves last frame's copy to `SAMPLED` and this frame's to `STORAGE`. A new copy of a storage history target is not cleared, so the pass drops its history (writes the current frame alone) on its first frame, after a resize, when `Camera::historyResets` changed (`Renderer::resetCameraHistory`) and on a camera jump (`isCameraJump`: a turn above `TAA_JUMP_ANGLE`, 30°, or a move above `TAA_JUMP_NEAR_PLANES`, 100, near planes, half the width of an orthographic camera), all from a `TaaState` the pass keeps.
  - **Jitter.** `writeViewMotion` (`gpuScene.cpp`, in `prepareSceneView`) gives a jittered view's projection a translation of clip space by `getTaaJitter(SceneView::jitterFrame++)` (Halton 2 and 3, points 1 to `TAA_JITTER_PHASES` = 8, −0.5 to 0.5 pixels) times 2 / size, writes it to `SceneData::jitter`, and sets `SceneData::textureLodBias` to `TAA_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS` (−1), which `sampleMaterial` (`core.slang`) adds to every material texture lookup. Culling, the clusters and the Hi-Z use the jittered projection; shadow fitting and LOD use the camera's. The counter is the view's, so the same frames give the same pixels (`09` and `02` with `--aa taa`: two runs to frame 60 are equal to the pixel).
  - **Motion.** A target with a motion color gets the shaded pipelines of `ShadedPipelines` slot `MOTION_VARIANT`, compiled with `RASM_MOTION_VECTORS`: `vertMain` also outputs the point's clip position without the jitter (`removeJitter`) and through `InstanceData::previousWorld` and `SceneData::previousViewProjection` (the view's last unjittered view-projection, kept in world space as a `glm::dmat4` in `SceneView::previousViewProjection` and moved into this frame's render space; this frame's on the view's first), and `fragMain` writes `getMotion` (UV now minus UV then) to `SV_TARGET1`. The sky writes the motion of its direction (w = 0, so only the camera's turn), none for an orthographic camera. A `BLEND` pipeline writes zero motion with alpha 0, which its premultiplied blend keeps the motion behind with.
  - **Previous transforms.** `writeInstanceRows(entity, moved)` writes `previousWorld = EntityDrawState::syncedTransform × sub-mesh transform`, in doubles, with its translation minus this one in w (`writePreviousRows`), and, for a move, puts the entity on `GpuScene::movedEntities` once per sync window (`movedSync`). `syncGpuScene` first rewrites the rows of `settlingEntities` that did not move again (`previousWorld = world`), then makes each moved entity's transform its `syncedTransform` and moves the list to `settlingEntities`. Rows are only changed through `GpuTable::setRow`, uploaded in the sync. `InstanceData` is 160 bytes: 20 MiB for the 65536 rows × 2 frame slots of the default `maxInstances`. An animated sub-mesh's `previousWorld` comes from the `Animate` pass instead (§4, node animation).
  - **The pass** (`taaMain`): the 3×3 neighbors' colors, compressed by `1 / (1 + luminance · exposure)` and in YCoCg, give a mean and a standard deviation; the nearest depth among them picks the motion; the history is read at `uv − motion` with a 5-tap Catmull-Rom filter, clipped toward the box mean ± 1 σ, and blended at 10 % (up to 30 % for a pixel moving 8 pixels a frame), then decompressed. A history position off the screen uses the current frame, and so does a point that was behind the camera last frame (`getMotion` gives it a motion of 2, off the screen). Without a sky (`SceneView::sky` false) nothing writes the background's motion, which holds the clear color, so the pass takes a pixel with no depth around it as still (flag bit 17). The history starts over on the pass's first frame, when its first copy's texture changes (a resize or a new swapchain made new, uncleared copies), after `resetCameraHistory` and on a jump. Its push constants are `gfx::TaaPushConstants` (two 16-bit indices per word). At 1920x1080 on an RTX 3060 (Release, median of 5 runs of `09 --render-size 1920 1080 --aa taa`) the pass takes 0.37 ms and the motion output adds about 0.13 ms to the draw passes; the motion target takes 7.9 MiB of the transient heap, the history 31.6 MiB.
  - `EngineConfig::antiAliasing = AntiAliasing::TAA` (or `View::antiAliasing`) makes `ViewGraph::render` add the motion color and the pass before the bloom and the tone map; the view's history target and `TaaState` outlive its graph (above). The pass resets on a new `RenderTarget::version` of its history (textures made again under the same handles: a resize, a new swapchain). A scene view held by two graphs is jittered when either has a TAA pass (`D27`). See-through surfaces keep the motion of what is behind them, so a moving background under glass can smear; it does not show in the examples, whose glass and background are still.
- **Bloom.** `Renderer::addBloomPasses(graph, source, camera)` (`src/renderer/bloom.cpp`, `shaders/passes/bloom.slang`) adds 11 compute passes on the graphics queue that write transient storage render targets (§9) of `SCENE_COLOR_FORMAT`, and returns the last, for `addToneMapPass`: `Bloom Down 1` to `Bloom Down 6` halve the source into `bloom_down_1` … `bloom_down_6` with Jimenez's 13-tap filter (five 2x2 boxes of bilinear taps, 0.5 for the inner one and 0.125 for each corner one; `firstDownsampleMain` weights each box by 1 / (1 + luma), Karis's average against fireflies), and `Bloom Up 5` to `Bloom Up 1` write `bloom_up_i` = `bloom_down_i` + a 3x3 tent of the level below (`upsampleMain`), `Bloom Up 1` times 1 / the levels, so the bloom has the source's light. Each pass samples its inputs (`LINEAR_CLAMP`) and writes one image, never reading the one it writes. The levels take `swapchainScale` / 2^level of a window-sized source, or its size halved (`getBloomLevelSize`, at least 1 x 1); every frame the chain reads the levels' sizes and runs `getBloomLevelCount` of them, the levels from the first that are at least `BLOOM_MIN_LEVEL_SIZE` (2) on each side, at least one (`tests/bloomTests.cpp`); the rest record nothing. `Bloom Down 1` finds the count and shares it with the others. With `Camera::bloomStrength` 0 (0.04 by default, `setCameraBloomStrength` takes 0 to 1) or before the three pipelines compile (`createBloomPipelines`, in the first pass, retried each frame) they dispatch nothing (the graph still records their barriers) and the tone map does not sample the bloom: both ask `Renderer::bloomRuns`. The passes share the GPU timer `Bloom` (`Pass::timerName`): about 0.2 ms at 1280x720 (0.10 to 0.27 ms from run to run), 0.37 ms at 1920x1080, 0.75 ms at 3840x2160 (`examples/09_gltf_materials --gpu-times --render-size`, RTX 3060, Release). The 11 targets take 13.4 MiB at 1080p each in its own memory; in that example they reuse the shadow cascade's memory and add nothing at 1080p, 27.2 MiB at 4K. `EngineConfig::bloom` (off by default) makes `ViewGraph::render` add them. Its push constants are `gfx::BloomPushConstants`, checked like the tone map's.

## 12. RHI

`src/rhi/device.h` is the boundary between the engine and a graphics API. Code above it (`RenderContext`, and through it `Renderer`) never sees a native type, and each backend implements it under `src/rhi/<backend>/`. Vulkan is the only implementation today.

**Objects.**
- `rhi::createDevice(Backend, DeviceDesc)` picks the implementation for `EngineConfig::preferredBackend`. `DX12`, `METAL` and `WEBGPU` log that they are not implemented and return null.
- Resources are plain structs (`Buffer`, `Texture`, `Heap`, `Sampler`, `Shader`, `Pipeline`, `DescriptorSetLayout`, `DescriptorPool`, `DescriptorSet`, `Fence`) that hold their neutral description. A backend derives from each (`VulkanBuffer : Buffer`) and adds its native objects. An object passed to a device always came from that device, so the backend `static_cast`s it to its own type. `CommandBuffer`, `Queue` and `Swapchain` are interfaces with virtual functions.
- A `PipelineDesc` describes either a graphics pipeline (`vertexShader` + `fragmentShader`, attachment formats, topology, `blendMode` (`NONE`, `PREMULTIPLIED_ALPHA` or `ADDITIVE`, the same for every color), `depthWrite` and `depthTest` (only with a depth format; with the test off nothing is written either), `samples` and `alphaToCoverage` (`rasterizationSamples` and `alphaToCoverageEnable`)) or a compute pipeline (`computeShader` only; the graphics fields are unused). `RenderContext::createPipeline` picks `createGraphicsPipeline` or `createComputePipeline` from the shaders set, and rejects a desc that sets both kinds. Both share one handle type and one dedup cache, and one pipeline layout (`P8`): the one `VulkanDevice::createBindlessDescriptorSetLayout` makes with the set layout (`VulkanDescriptorSetLayout::pipelineLayout`, destroyed with it, so `RenderContext::cleanup` destroys set layouts after pipelines), the set plus one `PUSH_CONSTANTS_SIZE` range for the vertex, fragment and compute stages (`PUSH_CONSTANT_STAGES`). `VulkanPipeline::layout` points to it. The set therefore stays bound across pipeline changes.
- **Placed textures.** `getTextureMemoryRequirements(desc)` returns the size, alignment and memory types a texture would need (`vkGetDeviceImageMemoryRequirements`, without creating it). `createHeap` allocates one block of device memory (`vmaAllocateMemory`, dedicated), and `createPlacedTexture(desc, heap, offset)` creates a texture inside it (`vmaCreateAliasingImage2`). The texture does not own its memory: `destroyTexture` frees only the image, and the heap must outlive every texture in it. Several textures may share bytes of a heap when they are never used at the same time.
- `create*` logs a failure and returns null. `rhi::Device::destroy*` releases at once, so the caller must know the GPU is done with the object: `RenderContext` calls it from the release queue (§2), from `cleanup` after `waitIdle`, or for objects no frame has used. Swapchain images belong to the swapchain, and `RenderContext::destroyTexture` refuses them.
- `RenderContext` maps handles to these pointers and adds what no API provides per object: handle allocation, dedup of shaders, pipelines and layouts, render targets, bindless slot allocation and the shared sampler set, and the one bindless set (made in `initialize`, read with `getBindlessDescriptorSetLayout` and `getBindlessDescriptorSet`), which it fills itself for render targets. It is made from a `RenderContextDesc` and knows no `Renderer`: `createSwapchain` and `recreateSwapchain` take the `Window` (its handle, `NativeWindow` and pixel size), and `RenderContextDesc::headless` gives every window an offscreen swapchain.

**Function loading (Vulkan, `D24`).** Nothing links `vulkan-1.dll` or `libvulkan`: volk loads every function at run time. RASM compiles with `VK_NO_PROTOTYPES` and `VOLK_NO_DEVICE_PROTOTYPES` (`src/CMakeLists.txt`), and the Vulkan headers come from vcpkg's `vulkan-headers` at volk's version, the one VMA and vk-bootstrap see too.
- Loader and instance functions are volk's globals, one set per process. `loadVulkan` fills them with `volkInitialize`, with a window or headless (volk also tries `/usr/local/lib/libvulkan.dylib` on macOS). The surface is made from the window's native handles through that loader (`createVulkanSurface`), so no windowing library loads a second copy. `volkLoadInstanceOnly` loads the instance's; they are the loader's trampolines, which work for any instance.
- Device functions live in the device: `VulkanDevice::vk`, a `VolkDeviceTable` from `volkLoadDeviceTable` (`vkGetDeviceProcAddr`), so each call goes to the first layer or the driver without the loader's trampoline. `VulkanCommandBuffer`, `VulkanQueue` and `VulkanSwapchain` keep a reference to it (`getVkTable`). There are no global device functions, so a call that skips the table does not compile. `vkSetDebugUtilsObjectNameEXT` is an instance extension's command and stays global.
- vk-bootstrap gets volk's `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` (`SystemInfo::get_system_info`, `InstanceBuilder`) and VMA `vmaImportVulkanFunctionsFromVolk` (`VMA_STATIC_VULKAN_FUNCTIONS` and `VMA_DYNAMIC_VULKAN_FUNCTIONS` are 0).
- A 1.3 loader is enough: Vulkan 1.4 added only device-level commands, and they come from the driver through the table. The device must have 1.4. See the [Vulkan loader guide](guides/vulkan-loader.html).

**Command buffers.** Each frame slot records one command buffer per batch (§3), on the queue the batch is for (`Device::createCommandBuffer(queueType)`). `begin` resets it, so the GPU must have finished its previous submission, which the frame fence wait guarantees. On Vulkan each command buffer owns a command pool, and `begin` resets the pool. Barriers are `TextureUsage` transitions from each texture's tracked state (§9).

**Compute.** `CommandBuffer::dispatch` records a dispatch (`dispatchIndirect` one whose group counts a buffer of `BufferUsage::INDIRECT` holds), and `memoryBarrier(before, after)` orders memory between `PipelineStage::COMPUTE` (compute shader storage reads and writes, and an indirect dispatch's group counts), `PipelineStage::GRAPHICS` (indirect arguments, index fetch, vertex and fragment shader storage and uniform reads and writes) and `PipelineStage::HOST` (host reads and writes; as `after` it makes shader writes visible to CPU reads that follow a fence wait). `PipelineStage` is a set of flags, so one barrier can name several stages (`GRAPHICS | HOST`). Shaders reach buffers through device addresses, so it is a global memory barrier (`VkMemoryBarrier2`), not a per-buffer one: it waits for the `before` stages, makes their writes available, and makes them visible to the `after` stages' reads and writes. Its first scope includes earlier submissions on the queue, so a `GRAPHICS → COMPUTE` barrier at the start of a frame also covers the previous frame's draws on that queue; work on the other queue is ordered by a semaphore wait instead (Queues, below). A command buffer for a compute-only queue fits every barrier to it (`fitToComputeQueue` in `vulkanCommandBuffer.cpp`): graphics stages in the source scope become `ALL_COMMANDS`, which chains to the submit's semaphore wait, graphics stages leave the destination scope, and graphics-only accesses (attachments, index and vertex reads) are dropped, since the semaphore already made the other queue's writes available. `RenderContext::dispatch` and `memoryBarrier` refuse to record inside a rendering scope (between `beginRendering` and `endRendering`), where Vulkan forbids both, and draws refuse to record outside one. `dispatch` also needs a compute pipeline bound since `beginCommandBuffer`; a failed `bindPipeline` clears that and skips draws, so nothing records with a stale pipeline. `pushConstants` takes no stage: every push names `PUSH_CONSTANT_STAGES`, as Vulkan requires for a range those stages share. `createPipeline` checks each shader's `ShaderType` against its slot. Binding a compute pipeline skips the attachment-format check and leaves the pass's graphics pipeline bound. Compute writes buffers and storage images. It reads textures through the bindless set, which every stage sees: bind it to the compute pipeline with `bindDescriptorSet` (the Hi-Z build does).

**Storage images.** A texture created `TextureUsage::SAMPLED_STORAGE` can be written by compute and sampled. `TextureDesc::mipLevels` sets its mip count (0 keeps the old rule: one level, or the full chain for a sampled RGBA8 texture; any count is clamped to the full chain; `RenderContext::createTexture` refuses more than one for any other texture, since attachments are drawn into one mip and `fillTexture` makes mips only for RGBA8), and with `cube` it is a cube of 6 layers. The Vulkan backend gives it one `VK_IMAGE_VIEW_TYPE_2D_ARRAY` view per mip with every layer (`VulkanTexture::mipViews`), so a shader writes a 2D texture and a cube alike, as `RWTexture2DArray`. `RenderContext::updateBindlessStorageImage(set, slot, texture, mip)` writes one mip into slot `slot` (below the layout's `storageImageCount`, which `rhi::DescriptorSet` keeps; `MAX_BINDLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` for the engine's set, whose first `RESERVED_STORAGE_IMAGES` are the environment bake's and the rest the storage render targets', §9) of binding 1 of the bindless set, in the `GENERAL` layout. The binding is `UPDATE_AFTER_BIND` (`descriptorBindingStorageImageUpdateAfterBind`), so a slot may be rewritten while earlier frames are pending, but not while one of them still uses it: the caller waits for those frames. Before the dispatches that write it, the texture moves to `TextureUsage::STORAGE`; between a dispatch that writes a mip and one that reads it, a `memoryBarrier(COMPUTE, COMPUTE)` is enough, since the texture stays in `GENERAL`. Shaders declare the binding with the format: `[format("rgba16f")]`, so no `shaderStorageImage*WithoutFormat` feature is needed. `RenderContext::getTextureState` reads a texture's tracked state, and `getFenceValue` a fence's current value without waiting.

**Timestamps.** `Device::createTimestampQueryPool` makes a pool of timestamp queries, or returns null when the graphics queue has none (`timestampValidBits` is 0), which is not an error. `CommandBuffer::writeTimestamp` writes one once all earlier commands are done (`ALL_COMMANDS`), so two of them bracket the commands between them. `resetQueries` resets the whole pool from the host (`vkResetQueryPool`, `hostQueryReset`), so the GPU must be done with it. `getTimestamps(pool, firstQuery, count, ...)` returns `count` queries from `firstQuery` in nanoseconds since the first of them: the distance is masked to the valid bits, one in the upper half of that range reads 0 (it is before the first), and the rest is scaled by `timestampPeriod` and clamped, since a query of another queue may count from another origin. It returns false while any of them is unwritten. `RenderContext` gives pools a `QueryPoolHandle`.

**Fences are timelines.** A submit signals a 64-bit value and `waitForFence(fence, value)` blocks until the GPU has reached it. Values only grow and are never reset. This maps to Vulkan timeline semaphores, `ID3D12Fence` and `MTLSharedEvent`, and on WebGPU to counting `onSubmittedWorkDone` callbacks.

**Swapchain.** `acquireNextImage`, `present` and `resize` return `SwapchainStatus`. The swapchain owns whatever synchronizes it with the queue, so no semaphore crosses the interface: a `SubmitDesc` lists swapchains in `acquireSwapchains` to wait for each one's image of the last acquire, and in `presentSwapchains` to make each one's next `present` wait for it (at most `MAX_SUBMIT_SWAPCHAINS`, 8). A frame of several submits lists the acquired windows' swapchains for waiting on every graphics submit: only the first of them waits on each, since the semaphore is binary and a second wait would never end. The last graphics submit lists them for presenting; a single submit does both.
- The swapchain enforces acquire → submit → present. A submit that names the swapchain without an image acquired since the last such submit fails, and so does a present without such a submit. Acquiring again while the last image is still unsubmitted (e.g. its submit failed) returns that image again rather than acquiring another, and acquiring while the last submitted image is unpresented fails.
- Vulkan keeps one acquire semaphore per frame in flight and uses them in turn. Every presenting submit also signals a timeline semaphore the swapchain owns, and acquire waits until the submit that last waited on a semaphore has finished before reusing it, so correctness doesn't depend on the caller's frame pacing.
- Vulkan keeps one present semaphore per image: an image is acquired again only after its present finished. They survive `resize`, because the presentation engine may still wait on one after the device is idle; a larger new swapchain only adds semaphores.
- `resize` returns `OUT_OF_DATE` on a 0×0 surface and `FAILURE` if the surface query fails, and both keep the old swapchain. Otherwise it consumes any pending acquire or present signal with a wait-only submit, waits for the device to be idle and replaces the images. `RenderContext::recreateSwapchain` re-registers the image handles when the images changed, so earlier `getSwapchainImages` handles are invalid then; `getSwapchainImages` returns a copy. If building the new swapchain fails there are no images, and acquire reports `OUT_OF_DATE` until a resize succeeds.
- A swapchain presents to the window the device was created for (`DeviceDesc::window`, a `NativeWindow`), or with `SwapchainDesc::window` to another window, whose surface it makes (`createVulkanSurface`), checks with `vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfaceSupportKHR` on the graphics family, and destroys with itself (`VulkanSwapchain::ownsSurface`; `getSurfaceDevice` gives vk-bootstrap the device with that surface).
- **Encoded view.** Where the device has `VK_KHR_swapchain_mutable_format` (enabled when present), the `B8G8R8A8_SRGB` swapchain is created `MUTABLE_FORMAT` with a format list of the sRGB and the UNORM format, and each image gets a second, `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` view (`VulkanTexture::encodedView`). `Swapchain::getEncodedFormat` (and `RenderContext::getSwapchainEncodedFormat`) returns that format, or `UNKNOWN` without the view. A pass begun with `AttachmentOps::encodedColor` draws color 0 through it when the attachment is a swapchain image: it stores and blends the sRGB numbers as they are, and a clear stores the encoded clear color. `RenderContext::beginRendering` records that format as the pass's, so `bindPipeline`'s format check expects pipelines made for it. Only the UI pass uses it (§3, the UI).

**Buffers and uploads (`P1`).** `BufferDesc::hostVisible` marks data the CPU rewrites every frame. Such a buffer is always mapped, and `fillBuffer` writes it at once. `BufferDesc::hostReadable` asks for cached host memory (`HOST_ACCESS_RANDOM`) instead of write-only memory, for buffers the CPU reads: `RenderContext::readBuffer` accepts only those, and reads (after invalidating the range) once a barrier to `PipelineStage::HOST` and a fence wait have passed. Any other buffer is device-local. VMA maps it too when that memory is also host-visible (ReBAR, integrated GPUs, Apple), and then `fillBuffer` is a plain write as well, except for a `BufferDesc::staged` buffer on a discrete GPU (the geometry pools and the animation pool, filled once), which asks for no host access (`P35`): Windows clears new video memory before the device's next submit runs, about 8 times slower when it is host-visible, and the mapped geometry pools held back the first frame by up to 50 ms. Otherwise `Buffer::mapped` is false, and `RenderContext::fillBuffer` hands the data to `rhi::UploadManager` (`src/rhi/uploadManager.cpp`):
- The data is copied into a 64 MB host-visible staging ring and a `BufferCopy` is recorded. A ring is a `BufferUsage::TRANSFER` buffer, which has no device-address bit, so VMA puts it in system memory; where all of video memory is host-visible (resizable BAR, memory shared with the CPU: `VulkanDevice::wholeVramMapped`) it asks for video memory instead, since the GPU then copies within video memory (on the RTX 3060 2.6 times faster than across the bus) and no small BAR heap is used up (`P10`). Writes over 16 MB are split into chunks. Back-to-back copies into the same buffer merge into one.
- `RenderContext::submit` flushes the pending copies first: one command buffer that calls `CommandBuffer::copyBuffers`, submitted on the graphics queue, signalling the upload fence. The barriers inside `copyBuffers` order the copies after earlier submissions and before later ones on the same queue, so a graphics submit needs no semaphore wait. The compute queue is outside those barriers: every flush, the ones made while recording (full ring, overlap) too, waits for the last compute submit, which may still read what the copies overwrite (`UploadManager::waitFor`, set by each compute submit), and a compute submit waits for the last upload (`UploadManager::lastSubmit`).
- Two upload command buffers alternate. Ring space is freed once the upload fence passes the batch that used it (`Device::getFenceValue`, which doesn't block). A full ring flushes and waits for every copy, except for a texture fill in parts, which stops there (`uploadTexture` with a byte limit, `hasRoom`).
- **Two upload managers.** `RenderContext` has the frames' (`uploads`, on the graphics queue, as above) and, with a transfer queue, the loads' (`transferUploads`, on that queue, with its own 64 MB ring, fence and timers). `beginLoadUploads` / `endLoadUploads` route the fills between them to the loads' (`fillUploads`); without a transfer queue both are the frames' one. The loads' copies wait for the frame and compute values the CPU saw reached (`waitFor`), not for the last submits, since what they write is new or given back by the release queue; every graphics and compute submit waits for the load uploads' value `updateLoadUploadsDone` saw reached, once per new value (`loadWaited`). `finishReadyLoads` counts an environment whose image is still copied as a pending load, so `waitForLoads` returns with it ready to bake. An application's `fillTexture` of a texture a load still fills is refused (`UploadManager::isFilling`).
- The data reaches the buffer with the next submit, not at once. Destroying a buffer drops its pending copies and waits for submitted copies into it, which a mid-frame flush (full ring, overlap) can leave in flight.
- A batch whose flush fails stays pending and goes out with the next flush, since `fillBuffer` already returned true for it.
- Copies in one batch have no barriers between them, so a write that overlaps a range already written in the batch starts a new batch.

**Texture uploads through the ring.** `UploadManager::uploadTexture` copies each mip into the ring in whole rows of blocks (`rhi::getFormatBlock`), as many as fit in a chunk, each chunk 16-byte aligned, and adds a `TextureCopy` (mip, first row, rows, `discard` on the texture's first copy, `last` on its last). A level or a texture larger than a chunk or the ring spans chunks and batches; with a byte limit and a `TextureFillProgress` it copies one part and stops, and the next call goes on from there. `CommandBuffer::copyToTextures` records, per batch: each texture to `TRANSFER_DST` (from `UNDEFINED` in the batch with its first copy, else it is in `TRANSFER_DST` still, keeping what an earlier batch wrote), the `vkCmdCopyBufferToImage2` copies, then each texture whose last copy is in the batch to `SHADER_READ_ONLY`. On the graphics queue that barrier's second scope is every later command on the queue. On the transfer queue it has none (the frames wait for the submit's semaphore), and for an exclusive texture it is the release half of an ownership transfer, transfer family → graphics family; `UploadManager` keeps the texture (`releases`) until a graphics submit runs the acquire half: `recordAcquires(loadDone)` records the same barrier with the graphics stages into one of three graphics command buffers (`acquireTextures`), `RenderContext::submit` puts it first in the next graphics submit with a signal fence, and `acquiresSubmitted` forgets them once that submit went out; a frame that is skipped, dropped or fails its submit takes them again next time. Filling a texture twice in one batch flushes first. A destroyed texture's release (§2) cancels copies still pending and waits for submitted ones, like a buffer's; by then the upload fence has passed them, so it does not block. `RenderContext::fillTexture` sets the tracked state to `SAMPLED` at once: the copy goes out with the next submit, before any frame command buffer.

**Draws.** Besides direct draws there are `drawIndexedIndirect` and `drawIndexedIndirectCount`; the second reads the draw count from a buffer on the GPU, clamped to `maxDrawCount`. Both buffers need `BufferUsage::INDIRECT`.

**Queues.** There is a graphics queue (`QueueType::GRAPHICS`), used for frames and uploads, with `DeviceDesc::asyncCompute` a compute queue (`QueueType::COMPUTE`) from a family without graphics, and with `DeviceDesc::transferQueue` a transfer queue (`QueueType::TRANSFER`) from a family that only copies, for the loads' copies; `Device::hasAsyncCompute` and `hasTransferQueue` say whether there is one, and without it `getQueue` and command buffers for that type use the graphics queue. `RenderContext::createCommandBuffer` and `submit` refuse `TRANSFER`: it is the uploads' own. `RenderContext::submit(SubmitInfo)` names the queue and takes several command buffers, which run in the given order as one submission, plus `FenceValue` waits (`MAX_SUBMIT_WAITS` less the one a compute submit keeps for the frames' uploads, and less one when the submit waits for a new load uploads' value; a graphics submit that runs acquires takes one command buffer fewer): the submit starts once each fence reaches its value, with the wait at `ALL_COMMANDS`, since the first commands are often barriers whose layout transitions must come after it. `P11` has what async compute costs and saves today; the transfer queue's measurements are in `D13` (with a busy GPU loads end 6 to 11 % sooner, with an idle one the same). Vulkan submits with `vkQueueSubmit2` and waits for the acquired image at `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT`, the stage the barrier out of `UNKNOWN` also waits for.

**Adding a backend.**
1. Implement `Device`, `CommandBuffer`, `Queue` and `Swapchain`, and derive the resource structs, under `src/rhi/<backend>/`.
2. Add a case to the switch in `src/rhi/device.cpp`, the sources to `target_sources(rasm ...)`, and the libraries to `src/CMakeLists.txt` and `cmake/rasmConfig.cmake.in`.
3. The shader target follows the backend (`getShaderCompilerTarget` in `renderer.cpp`: MSL, HLSL or WGSL). The shaders still read buffers through device addresses and `spirv_asm`, so D3D12 and WebGPU need the binding work in `D12` first, and WebGPU also the Tier B path of the development plan.
4. A window reaches the device as a `NativeWindow` (`DeviceDesc::window`), with no API type: the Vulkan backend makes its surface in `createVulkanSurface`; D3D12 takes the `HWND`, Metal the `CAMetalLayer`, and WebGPU makes its surface from the same handles.

## 13. C API

`include/rasm/rasm_c.h` is a C99 header over the C++ API, for C programs and language bindings (`docs/guides/stable-c-abi.html`, `docs/api/c-api.html`). The C layer in `src/c/` is thin: each function checks its arguments, converts them, calls one C++ function and converts the result back. It adds no engine feature.

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `src/c/cApi.h`, `src/c/cApi.cpp` | The opaque `rasm_renderer` and `rasm_image`; handle and struct conversions (`toC`, `toCpp`); `readSized`, `writeSized`, `initSized` for sized structs and their 1.0 sizes; `checkVersion`, `checkEnum`; the `static_assert`s that tie every C constant to its C++ enum value and `rasm_vertex` to `Vertex`; the spdlog sink (`CSink`) behind `rasm_get_last_error` and `rasm_set_log_callback`; `initialize` (the sink, `setBaseModule`); `rasm_get_version`, `rasm_get_base_path`. |
| `src/c/cRenderer.cpp` | `rasm_renderer_config_init`, create and destroy, the frame (`rasm_render`), readbacks and `rasm_image`. |
| `src/c/cScene.cpp` | Scenes, entities, meshes, materials, textures (`rasm_create_texture` is `createTexture` + `RenderContext::fillTexture`), lights, cameras and their grids (1.8), environments. |
| `src/c/cInput.cpp` | Windows, input events (one by index, never an array), camera controllers, the button map (1.8), and the application-driven helpers: `rasm_camera_rig` (a `CameraRig`) and `rasm_camera_input_state` (a `CameraInputState`), `rasm_to_input_ndc`, `rasm_get_input_area`. |
| `src/c/cViews.cpp` | `rasm_render_views`: the caller's `rasm_view` array, stepped by the first element's size, turned into `View`s in an array of `MAX_SCENE_VIEWS` on the stack; render targets for view textures; `rasm_get_view_pixels`. |
| `src/c/cAnimation.cpp` | Animation playback and morph weights; clip and morph target names by index (`copyString`). |
| `src/c/cPicking.cpp` | 1.7: `rasm_pick_view` (a `rasm_view` read by `readViews`), `rasm_pick_target`, `rasm_is_pick_ready`, `rasm_take_pick` (the hits through an array of 64 on the stack, a vector beyond, at most `MAX_PICK_SIZE`² of them, written into the caller's array stepped by `hits[0].size`, each element's size set to it), `rasm_pick_desc_init`, `rasm_pick_hit_init`. `rasm_is_entity_alive` is in `cScene.cpp`, `rasm_get_view_pixel` in `cInput.cpp`. |
| `src/c/cText.cpp` | 1.6: `rasm_font_atlas` (a `FontAtlas`, defined in `cApi.h`) destroy, `rasm_create_font`, `rasm_destroy_font`, `rasm_label_desc_init`. `rasm_build_font_atlas`, `rasm_asset_loader_load_font` and `rasm_font_load_options_init` are in `cLoader.cpp` (`rasm_c` only); `rasm_vector_add_labels` in `cVector.cpp`, reading the `rasm_label_desc` array by the first element's size. |
| `src/c/cVector.cpp` | 1.5: `rasm_build_mesh`, `rasm_create_mesh_from_build` (`rasm_mesh_build` is defined in `cApi.h`) and `rasm_vector_geometry` (the vector builders), with their sized structs; both may be used on any thread. |
| `src/c/cPointCloud.cpp` | 1.10: `rasm_create_colormap`, `rasm_create_colormap_preset`, `rasm_point_cloud_chunk_desc_init` and `rasm_build_point_cloud_chunk` (on any thread); 1.11: `rasm_set_compute_points`, `rasm_get_compute_points`. |
| `src/c/cExport.cpp` | 1.16: `rasm_export_desc_init`, `rasm_export_progress_init`, `rasm_export_view` (a `rasm_view` read by `readViews`, the path copied), `rasm_get_export_progress`, `rasm_cancel_export`, `rasm_take_export`. |
| `src/c/cTerrain.cpp` | 1.4: `rasm_terrain_tile_desc` and terrain tiles (`rasm_create_terrain_tile`, `rasm_get_terrain_mesh`), and the CPU helpers (`rasm_get_terrain_transform`, `rasm_sample_terrain_height`, `rasm_get_terrain_position`). |
| `src/c/cTiles.cpp` | 1.3: `rasm_tile_streamer` (a `TileStreamer` and the lists a call converts handles into, kept between calls), its sized structs and calls; its views through `readViews`, which `rasm_render_views` uses too. |
| `src/c/cLoader.cpp` | `rasm_engine` (an `Engine` with the C views of its renderer and loader) and `rasm_asset_loader`. Only in `rasm_c`. |

- **Libraries.** `rasm_c_objects` (an OBJECT library: `cApi`, `cRenderer`, `cScene`, `cInput`, `cViews`, `cAnimation`, `cTiles`, `cTerrain`, `cVector`, `cText`, `cPicking`, `cExport`) goes into both shared libraries; `rasm_c` adds `cLoader.cpp` and links `rasm::rasm`, `rasm_c_renderer` links `rasm::renderer`. Option `RASM_C_API` (ON) builds them; `rasm_renderer` and `rasm` then get `POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE`. Only `rasm_*` leaves them: `RASM_C_BUILD` makes `RASM_C_API` `dllexport`, the C files are compiled with hidden visibility, and the static libraries inside are hidden by `-exported_symbol,_rasm_*` (macOS) or `--exclude-libs,ALL` (Linux). An application links exactly one of them: both export the renderer functions, each from its own copy of RASM.
- **Install.** `install(TARGETS ... EXPORT rasmCTargets)`, the component `c` of `rasmConfig.cmake` (no `find_dependency`), the header, `shaders/` beside the libraries (`bin/` on Windows, `lib/` elsewhere), and on Windows the vcpkg DLLs they load (`RUNTIME_DEPENDENCIES` from `vcpkg_installed/<triplet>/bin`, or `debug/bin` for a Debug build).
- **ABI rules.** Version `RASM_C_API_VERSION` = `major << 16 | minor` (1.1: 1.0 plus windows, input, views, render targets, camera helpers, animation and stats, appended; 1.2: positions in doubles, below, `free_fly_up` and the environment's `orientation`; 1.3: tile streaming, `src/c/cTiles.cpp`, and the renderer config's `max_bindless_textures`; 1.4: terrain tiles, `src/c/cTerrain.cpp`, the formats `R16_UNORM` and `R32_SFLOAT`, `RASM_MATERIAL_TYPE_TERRAIN` and `rasm_material_desc::terrain`; 1.5: mesh builds and vector features, `src/c/cVector.cpp`, `RASM_MATERIAL_TYPE_VECTOR` and `rasm_material_desc::vector`; 1.6: fonts and labels, `src/c/cText.cpp`, `RASM_FORMAT_R8_UNORM` and `rasm_material_desc::font`; 1.7: picking, `src/c/cPicking.cpp`, `RASM_FORMAT_R32G32B32A32_UINT`, `rasm_is_entity_alive` and `rasm_get_view_pixel`; 1.8: `rasm_camera_buttons` with `rasm_camera_drag`, `rasm_set_camera_buttons`, `rasm_camera_input_state_set_buttons`, `rasm_camera_rig_update_projection`, `pan_start`, `pan_end` and `pointer` at the end of `rasm_camera_input`, and `rasm_grid` with `rasm_set_camera_grid` and `rasm_get_camera_grid`; 1.9: `rasm_line_shape::drape_width`; 1.10: colormaps and point cloud chunks, `RASM_MATERIAL_TYPE_POINTS`, `rasm_material_desc::points`, `rasm_renderer_config::geometry_pool_points`, `RASM_PICK_SKIP_POINTS`, `RASM_PICK_HIT_POINT`; 1.11: `rasm_renderer_config::compute_points`, `rasm_set_compute_points`, `rasm_get_compute_points`; 1.12: point cloud streaming, `rasm_point_chunk_lod` with `rasm_set_point_chunk_lod`, the octree (`rasm_build_point_cloud_octree` and its getters, `src/c/cPointCloud.cpp`), `rasm_asset_loader_load_point_cloud` with `rasm_point_cloud_info` (`src/c/cLoader.cpp`), `rasm_point_material::size_mode` and `max_size`, `rasm_point_cloud_chunk_desc::spacing`, `rasm_pool_stats::points`; 1.13: `rasm_renderer_config::shader_threads`; 1.14: instanced glyphs and data lines, `RASM_MATERIAL_TYPE_GLYPHS`, `rasm_scalar_colors` with `rasm_material_desc::glyphs` and `vector_colors`, `rasm_glyph_chunk_desc` with `rasm_build_glyph_chunk`, `rasm_build_glyph_shape`, `rasm_get_glyph_orientation`, `rasm_vector_add_data_lines` (`src/c/cGlyphs.cpp`, `src/c/cVector.cpp`), `RASM_PICK_SKIP_GLYPHS`, `RASM_PICK_HIT_GLYPH`, `rasm_frame_stats::glyphs` and `glyphs_visible`; 1.15: `rasm_blend_method` and `rasm_material_desc::blend_method`; 1.16: exports, `src/c/cExport.cpp`, `rasm_export_desc`, `rasm_export_progress`, `rasm_export_view`, `rasm_get_export_progress`, `rasm_cancel_export`, `rasm_take_export`); within a major version only appending (functions, constants, fields at the end of a sized struct). `rasm_renderer_create` and `rasm_engine_create` take the caller's version and return `RASM_ERROR_VERSION` for another major or a newer minor. Enums are `uint32_t` typedefs with constants, booleans `rasm_bool` (`uint32_t`), every function `RASM_CALL` (`__cdecl` on 32-bit Windows).
- **Sized structs** start with `uint32_t size`: `rasm_renderer_config`, `rasm_asset_loader_config`, `rasm_engine_config` (pointers to the two parts), `rasm_material_desc`, `rasm_texture_desc`, `rasm_light_desc`, `rasm_scene_environment`, `rasm_camera_projection`; in 1.1 also `rasm_window_desc`, `rasm_window_info`, `rasm_input_event`, `rasm_camera_input`, `rasm_camera_controller`, `rasm_view`, `rasm_render_target_desc`, `rasm_animation_play`, `rasm_frame_stats`, `rasm_pool_stats`, `rasm_load_stats`. A size below the struct's size in the version that added it (`*_SIZE_1_0`, `*_SIZE_1_1` in `cApi.h`) is refused. Create reads the caller's size over the defaults, update over the object's current values, get writes only the caller's size. In 1.7 `rasm_pick_desc` is read and `rasm_pick_hit` written, as an array stepped by its first element's size (`PICK_DESC_SIZE_1_7`, `PICK_HIT_SIZE_1_7`).
- **Positions in doubles (1.2).** `rasm_dvec3` and `rasm_transform_d` are new small structs with their own functions beside the float ones (`rasm_get_transform_d`, `rasm_update_transform_d`, `rasm_get_camera_transform_d`, `rasm_set_camera_transform_d`, `rasm_get_light_position_d`, `rasm_set_light_position_d`, `rasm_camera_rig_create_d`, `rasm_camera_rig_get_transform_d`, `rasm_to_render_position`, `rasm_to_world_position`). The sized structs get a `rasm_dvec3` at their end that adds to the float field (`rasm_light_desc::position_d`, `rasm_camera_controller::track_ball_center_d`): a get writes the nearest float and the rest (`toFloatPart`, `toRemainder`), so get and update keep a position bit for bit, and a 1.1 caller, whose size stops before them, gets the float plus 0. `rasm_update_light` from a 1.1 caller keeps the exact position when the caller left the float part as it got it. `tests/c_api/ctypes_smoke.py` renders 6370 km from the origin and reads the positions back bit for bit. The `_init` functions fill the defaults from the C++ structs' (`toC(RendererConfig{})`).
- **Handles** are `{ uint64_t id; }` per type, `generation << 32 | index` (`packHandle`). `HandlePool` retires a slot at `MAX_HANDLE_GENERATION` (`UINT32_MAX`), so every handle packs with no loss and a valid one is never 0; a handle that would not fit packs to 0.
- **Errors.** Every entry point is a function try block ending in `RASM_C_CATCH`: an exception becomes `RASM_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` or `RASM_ERROR_INTERNAL`. Boundary errors go through `reportError` (`spdlog::error`). The sink, added once to spdlog's default logger, stores each error line as the logging thread's last error and counts it (`getErrorCount`); a wrapper of a C++ call that returns nothing compares the count (`CallErrors`) and returns `RASM_ERROR_FAILED` when RASM logged an error. A call on one object rejects the handle 0 (`checkHandle`), since some C++ getters answer it with a default and no error. The log callback runs on whichever thread logged, with no lock held (the sink copies it under its mutex first), so it may call RASM. On Windows spdlog is a DLL whose default logger the whole process shares (`D41`).
- **Memory.** Inputs are borrowed for the call. `rasm_create_mesh` copies the caller's vertices into the vectors `createMesh` takes, as a C++ caller builds them. Readbacks fill a caller-owned `rasm_image` (a `ReadbackImage`), which keeps its capacity. Strings out follow `snprintf` (`copyString`).
- **C++ only.** The render graph, `RenderContext` and buffers, custom passes, shadows in `render(views)`, Dear ImGui and `setUiCallback`, inspection, GPU timers and the debug views (an open plan item): callbacks into command recording, or C++ UI code. The stats structs leave out the GPU timer list and the memory numbers.
- **Tests.** `rasm_c_tests` (`tests/cApiTests.cpp`) links only `rasm_c`; `rasm_c_header_c99` and `rasm_c_header_c11` (`tests/cApiHeaderCheck.c`) compile the header as strict C and link `rasm_c_renderer`; `tests/cApiHandleTests.cpp` (in `rasm_tests`) checks the packing. `tools/check_install.py` installs a build, builds the consumers in `tests/consumer/` for the components `renderer`, `rasm` and `c`, runs the C ones, checks the libraries' exports and imports, and with `--ctypes` renders with the installed `rasm_c` from Python (`tests/c_api/ctypes_smoke.py`); CI runs it on the release build.
